# August 2026

## 2026-08-31 — Normalize post-construction semantics and text cache generations

`working tree` · area: UI element core accessibility and text measurement

Accessibility normalization now rebuilds its live projection from a static
compiler/embedding baseline plus current attributes, child text, styles, and
interaction state. This prevents removed ARIA roles, names, and state aliases
from surviving a later normalization while keeping surface metadata and
directly authored embedding metadata available. Intrinsic text measurements
now clear their bounded cache whenever the active resource or measurer
generation changes, including a generation that happens to reuse an older
revision number; font-alias changes also clear named-family availability.

**Verification.** Focused accessibility, compiler accessibility, and retained
layout tests passed; the full `mesh-core-render --lib` suite passed 253 tests
with 36 ignored. The full `mesh-core-elements --lib` suite reached 262 passed,
10 existing style/theme/prop fixture failures, and 57 ignored; the full
`mesh-core-frontend --lib` suite reached 83 passed, 1 existing layout/display
fixture failure, and 20 ignored. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-31 — Make embedded contribution wrappers transparent to layout

`working tree` · area: embedded contribution layout

Synthetic embedded `surface` wrappers now retain their compiler-assigned
structural layout style through full and targeted restyles while forwarding
inherited text and custom-property values to the contributed subtree. The
wrapper stretches across its host's cross axis and centers its authored root;
the navigation-bar controls now derive their height from that wrapper, using
`aspect-ratio` only for square controls.

**Verification.** The focused wrapper, layout, compiler, and shipped navigation
geometry tests passed; all six `real_surfaces::layout` tests passed. The full
`mesh-core-elements` suite reported 252 passed and 10 existing theme/fixture
failures. The full `mesh-core-shell` suite reported 787 passed and 32 failures,
including the known navigation popup-anchor assertions and unrelated service,
profiling, invalidation, graph, and legacy-fixture failures. No performance
claim is made.

## 2026-08-29 — Propagate the selected UI font through every surface root

`working tree` · area: frontend compiler surface inheritance

Top-level and embedded frontend roots now resolve their inherited font family
from the active `typography.family` token instead of constructing a fixed
`Inter` style. This fixes settings and every other surface because descendant
text inherits from the shared root path; explicit component font declarations
and preview samples remain independent.

**Verification.** The live `@mesh/settings` font-selection regression passed;
the compiler regression passed for both surface and embedded roots; all six
focused `mesh-core-render` tooltip tests passed; `cargo fmt --all -- --check`
and `git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-29 — Apply global UI font to component defaults

`working tree` · area: theme typography and text rendering

The live global UI font now rewrites the repeated typography custom-property
defaults emitted by theme component styles, including module component
defaults. This prevents those local `Inter` values from shadowing the selected
global family, so ordinary component text and the standalone tooltip overlay
both follow the current appearance setting while explicit component font
declarations remain unchanged.

**Verification.** The focused shell style-resolution regression passed for
`DejaVu Serif`; all six focused `mesh-core-render` tooltip tests passed;
`cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check` passed. No performance
claim is made.

## 2026-08-29 — Apply selected UI font to tooltip rendering

`working tree` · area: theme typography and tooltip painting

The standalone tooltip overlay now receives the active `typography.family`
token from the shell. Its measurement and glyph rasterization use that family,
so live appearance font changes apply consistently to overlay text as well as
display-list text. Added a regression for tooltip geometry using a configured
family.

**Verification.** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `git diff --check`, the
focused `mesh-core-render` tooltip tests, `cargo check -p mesh-core-shell`, and
the focused shell theme-interface test passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-29 — Publish one graph-authorized host resource snapshot

`working tree` · area: host resources, icon/font snapshot lifecycle

Host discovery is now explicit and captured once during resource candidate
preparation. The prepared graph/profile icon bindings and host font/icon
catalog are held in one immutable resource snapshot; its icon resolver handle
is published atomically, while failed candidates leave the prior snapshot
untouched. Recovery and package rollback restore the last-known-good snapshot,
and runtime settings/watchers consume the committed host catalog.

**Verification.** `cargo test -p mesh-core-resources` passed (23);
`cargo test -p mesh-core-icon` passed (32, 3 ignored); the Nix shell discovery
slice passed (8, 2 ignored); `cargo check -p mesh-tools-lsp` and
`nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` passed. `cargo fmt --all -- --check`
and `git diff --check` passed. A direct non-Nix shell test could not
start because the host environment lacks `xkbcommon` pkg-config metadata; the
same shell tests passed in the repository's Nix development environment. No
performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-29 — Share one immutable locale snapshot across frontend surfaces

`working tree` · area: localization snapshot ownership

Frontend surfaces now retain the shell coordinator's immutable
`LocaleSnapshot`, which combines the normalized locale decision with the
graph-authorized catalog snapshot. Surface mount and locale refresh paths no
longer reread or parse graph catalog files; the coordinator prepares one
candidate off-thread and publishes its `Arc` to every surface. Copy-on-write
coordinator updates preserve prior readers' last-known-good snapshots, while
module translators keep lookups and provenance scoped to their owning module.

The localized-miss diagnostic also uses the established `module_id` field name
for the owner carried by the resolution.

**Verification.** `cargo test -p mesh-core-locale` passed (18 tests);
`nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib locale --no-fail-fast`
reported 10 passed and 2 existing unrelated fixture failures (legacy
`mesh.surfaceLayout` and a graph test that does not create its catalog parent
directory); the exact missing-manifest-translation diagnostic test passed.
Scripting locale and `mesh.i18n` tests passed (6 total), focused shipped
surface i18n tests passed (3), and `nix develop --command cargo check
--workspace` passed. The full shell library suite reported 789 passed, 20
existing unrelated failures, and 131 ignored. Formatting and whitespace
checks passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-28 — Make canonical module manifests the only production path

`working tree` · area: module manifest loading and migration diagnostics

The canonical package `module.json` loader is now the only production manifest
path. Shell’s recursive compatibility discovery helpers are compiled only for
migration tests, and canonical-looking documents that mix legacy top-level
`id`, `type`, or `api_version` fields are rejected with source-aware migration
diagnostics instead of being made runnable by serde’s ignored fields. The legacy
TOML parser is a test-only dependency; legacy package, TOML, plugin, and
old-shaped module manifests continue to receive explicit replacement errors.

**Verification.** Focused loader tests passed (12), shell discovery tests passed
(7, 1 ignored), LSP manifest tests passed (19), and `nix develop --command
cargo check --workspace` passed. The package-scoped module suite reported 145
passed, 4 pre-existing unrelated Luau-scan/shipped-fixture failures, and 3
ignored; the full LSP suite reported 114 passed and 1 pre-existing unrelated
locale-completion failure. Scoped rustfmt and `git diff --check` passed. No
performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-28 — Make installed-graph activation fail closed

`working tree` · area: installed-graph activation

Installed-graph interface catalogs now rebuild from an immutable core-owned
baseline and the replacement graph's enabled provider/contract set. Graph
reloads no longer retain providers or contracts from prior generations,
disabled providers are excluded, core-owned providers survive replacement, and
recovery resets graph-owned registrations. Direct graph commits use the same
catalog boundary, while candidate activation continues to publish the
last-known-good runtime until preparation succeeds.

Added a regression covering stale provider removal, disabled candidates, and
core-provider retention.

**Verification.** The focused graph suite reported 10 passed and 2 existing
failures; the malformed graph-contract regression passed; the frontend catalog
suite reported 12 passed and 2 ignored; `nix develop --command cargo check
--workspace` passed; and the full shell library suite reported 778 passed, 23
existing unrelated failures, and 131 ignored. Scoped rustfmt and `git diff
--check` passed. No performance claim is made.

`working tree` · area: module filesystem containment

All module-controlled filesystem boundaries now validate contained canonical

**Verification.** Focused module path tests passed (5); new store, provenance,

reported 221 passed,
8 pre-existing failures, and 3 ignored; backend lifecycle reported 35 passed

## 2026-08-28 — Refresh one canonical graph-authoring snapshot for CLI, doctor, LSP, and runtime consumers

`working tree` · area: graph-authoring snapshot ownership

Added the core-owned `AuthoringSnapshot` boundary over the resolved installed
module graph. Each snapshot carries a stable revision derived from normalized
graph state, the root graph, lock/profile inputs, and canonical module trees.
CLI inventory, services, locale/profile/update/config commands, doctor, shell
discovery and profile candidates, and LSP registry indexes now load through
that boundary. LSP derives module-owned manifests, interface contracts,
providers, resource chains, themes, locales, and source-backed service shapes
from one snapshot, retaining the prior registry when a refresh fails; it
refreshes on initialize, open/change, and save notifications.

**Verification.** Focused authoring revision tests passed (2), LSP registry
tests passed (2), shipped LSP settings and snapshot integration tests passed
(6), targeted compile checks passed, and direct formatting checks plus
`git diff --check` passed. The broad module suite reported 249 passed, 9
existing failures, and 3 ignored; the shell suite reported 774 passed, 23
existing failures, and 131 ignored; the LSP suite reported 88 passed and 1
existing failure; and the CLI suite reported 11 passed and 4 failures. The
CLI lock-record test passed when run in isolation; the remaining broad CLI
failures are the existing update/contract regressions. The doctor command
reported its existing settings error and 16 authoring warnings and exited 1.
No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-28 — Make CLI and shell share the typed package transaction contract

`working tree` · area: package ownership and transaction contract

Added core-owned `PackageOwner` and `PackageOperation` types to
`PackageTransaction`. CLI mutations now identify themselves as `Cli`, shell
`mesh.packages` mutations as `Shell`, and the durable journal records both
fields. New transactions use journal schema v2 while schema-v1 journals remain
recoverable. Added a regression covering the public accessors and serialized
journal provenance, and migrated every CLI/shell transaction caller.

**Verification.** Focused transaction tests passed (11), shell package tests
passed (2), and `nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` passed. The
full CLI suite reported 12 passed and 3 existing failures; the broad shell
suite reported 773 passed, 24 existing failures, and 131 ignored; and the
broad module suite reported 247 passed, 9 existing failures, and 3 ignored.
Repository-wide formatting still reports only the pre-existing untouched
`crates/core/extension/module/src/package/profile.rs` drift; `git diff --check`
passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-28 — Make package mutations atomic, journaled, crash-recoverable, and failure-injection tested

`working tree` · area: shared package transaction durability

Hardened the shared `PackageTransaction` boundary used by the CLI and running
shell. Protected package state is now fsynced before commit, generated
workspaces are removed without following rejected candidate symlinks, and
unfinished journals are restored, durably marked aborted, and cleaned under the
package lock. Shell startup recovers an interrupted journal before loading the
installed graph, while CLI failure paths explicitly abort before exiting.
Immutable module-store staging now cleans failed candidates, renames before
making activation directories read-only, and fsyncs published objects,
snapshots, and generation pointers.

Added one-shot failure injection around journal writes, replacements, removals,
package writes, store writes, store activation, and commit preparation. The
transaction regressions cover normal rollback, public crash recovery, journal
failure, partial replacement/removal, package-state writes, store publication,
and activation-pointer failure.

**Verification.** Focused transaction tests passed (10), workspace `cargo
check` passed, and the final CLI suite reported 8 passed with 3 existing
failures. The module suite reported 246 passed, 9 existing failures, and 3
ignored; the shell suite reported 772 passed, 23 existing failures, and 131
ignored. Repository-wide formatting still reports the pre-existing untouched
`crates/core/extension/module/src/package/profile.rs` drift; `git diff --check`
passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-28 — Make uninstall path-safe before recursive deletion

`working tree` · area: package uninstall and transaction path safety

CLI and shell uninstall now route module-tree removal through the shared
`PackageTransaction` boundary. Transaction targets continue to require
contained canonical paths, and recursive removal performs a complete module
tree preflight that rejects traversal, symlink, and unsupported-file escapes
before `remove_dir_all` runs. Added regressions cover traversal refusal without
deleting an outside sentinel, nested symlink refusal, and successful removal of
a valid contained module tree.

**Verification.** Focused transaction tests passed (6), path tests passed (5),
shell package tests passed (2), dependent CLI/shell `cargo check` passed, and
scoped rustfmt plus `git diff --check` passed. The broad module suite reported
241 passed, 9 existing failures, and 3 ignored; the CLI suite reported 7 passed
and 4 existing failures. Repository-wide formatting still reports the existing
untouched `package/profile.rs` drift. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Process CoreRequest effects through a fair bounded scheduler

`working tree` · area: shell effect scheduling and runtime orchestration

Replaced the scattered recursive `CoreRequest` drains with one generation-aware
`EffectScheduler` that runs at the runtime frame boundary before rendering.
Effects carry their source module, runtime identity, activation generation, and
causal transaction context. Per-frame count/byte budgets, per-source count/byte
budgets, and per-transaction limits defer fair residual work; repeated causal
fingerprints break cycles, and sources that repeatedly exceed policy are
quarantined through the existing component isolation path. Render-generated
effects remain queued for the next frame so topology changes do not occur in a
render snapshot. Isolated Wayland/render helpers retain synchronous behavior by
using the same scheduler wrapper, while production uses the central frame
phase.

**Verification.** Focused scheduler tests passed (3), request-drain regressions
passed (2), the pointer-leave and dismissed-popover scheduler integration
regressions passed, and `nix develop --command cargo check --workspace`
passed. The final `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib`
run reported 760 passed, 23 failed, and 131 ignored; a parent-commit baseline
run reported 757 passed, the same 23 failures, and 131 ignored, so the failures
remain the existing component fixture, interaction, backend/graph, profiling,
and theme baseline. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` still reports only the
pre-existing formatting difference in untouched
`crates/core/extension/module/src/package/profile.rs`; `git diff --check`
passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Manage generation-aware file watches with bounded polling fallback

`working tree` · area: shell file watching and reload orchestration

Replaced the one-shot startup inotify watcher with a managed `WatchSet` that
tracks a deterministic, deduplicated set of graph, lock, profile, module,
theme, settings, frontend, contribution, resource, locale, and mounted source
inputs. Watch generations are rebound in place when the active graph/catalog or
profile changes, and filesystem messages carry their generation so delayed
events from retired activation inputs cannot trigger reloads. Missing input
paths resolve to existing ancestors so atomic replacement and first creation
remain observable.

The watcher now reports binding health and lifecycle failures, while frontend
and shell-settings metadata checks keep bounded 250ms/500ms polling even when
inotify is healthy. A stopped or unavailable worker immediately leaves the
polling path live and records a shell lifecycle diagnostic; the worker also
remains managed when no current directory can be bound. Last-known-good reload
and resource/catalog commit behavior remains unchanged.

**Verification.** Focused watcher tests passed (6), diagnostics library tests
passed (7), and `nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` passed. The
full shell library suite ran 757 passed, 23 failed, and 131 ignored; the
failures reproduce the existing dirty-tree baseline in unrelated component,
graph, backend-lifecycle, profiling, and fixture tests. `cargo fmt --all --
--check` reports only the pre-existing formatting difference in untouched
`crates/core/extension/module/src/package/profile.rs`; `git diff --check`
passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Reconcile graph deltas through the activation coordinator

`working tree` · area: shell graph activation and backend lifecycle

Completed Section 15's graph-reconciliation item. Backend module enable/disable
now retains its durable-write rollback until the staged activation either
commits or rejects, and both profile-owned and legacy/no-profile graph changes
prepare locale/resources, interfaces, frontend roots, and backend providers
through the same `ActivationPlan`/`RuntimeGeneration` commit boundary. Package
install/uninstall paths use that coordinator for live graph changes, and the
filesystem-change path reloads the installed graph/profile candidate before
activating only real normalized graph deltas. Graph/profile and module-root
directories are now part of the initial watch set so external graph changes
reach this reconciliation path as well.

The legacy coordinator derives roots from the candidate frontend catalog,
retires removed backend providers at commit, and restores a module-enable
decision when candidate preparation fails or is superseded. A regression test
asserts that a legacy graph delta publishes one committed runtime generation.

**Verification.** Focused legacy coordinator regression: 1 passed. Profile
activation tests: 10 passed, 2 ignored. `nix develop --command cargo check
--workspace` passed. The full shell library suite ran 750 passed, 23 failed,
and 131 ignored; the failures are the existing dirty-tree baseline (including
backend lifecycle, graph fixture, and unrelated component integration
failures), with no failure in the new coordinator regression or profile tests.
Workspace formatting check remains blocked by pre-existing formatting in
untouched `crates/core/extension/module/src/package/profile.rs` and
`crates/core/shell/src/shell/file_watch.rs`; all files changed for this item
were formatted directly. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — immutable activation plan and runtime-generation commit

`working tree` · area: profile activation and shell runtime coordination

Profile switching now builds one immutable `ActivationPlan` containing the
candidate graph, graph-owned interface catalog, effective capabilities,
settings, locale, resources, theme, frontend catalog, roots, and provider
candidates. Frontend and backend preparation resolve against that plan rather
than the mutable live interface registry. The readiness buffers remain
separate from the plan, and the commit publishes one `RuntimeGeneration` with
the staged service state before retiring replaced runtime objects. Resource
publication also uses candidate settings and defers font-theme effects until
the candidate commits. The active interface registry now supports an atomic
catalog replacement used by the commit boundary.

**Verification.** Focused profile tests passed (10 passed, 2 ignored), the
catalog snapshot regression passed, and `cargo check --workspace` passed under
`nix develop`. The full shell library suite ran 749 passed, 23 failed, and 131
ignored; its failures are the documented dirty-tree baseline and none is a
profile activation test. The full service library suite ran 46 passed, 1
failed, and 5 ignored; the failure is the existing optional feature-group
compatibility assertion.

No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Republish mesh.settings on prop writes; stop discarding theme/locale event effects

Section 15 finding #10 ("control-plane state and follow-up effects can
diverge," audited 2026-08-20). The finding's first sub-bullet (the undeclared
`"set-current"` command to a `mesh.theme` backend) was removed earlier today.
This closes its other two shell-seam sub-bullets. The finding's "declared
durable revisions" half — theme/locale writes being live-only in this package
— is explicitly owned by the Section 4/5 shared settings/profile transaction,
so backlog line 104 ("Route control-plane writes through declared durable
revisions…") stays open.

1. **`apply_set_module_prop` did not republish `mesh.settings`.**
   (`crates/core/shell/src/shell/profile.rs`) It persisted the override,
   rebuilt `self.settings_store`, and called `apply_settings_to_components`,
   then returned `Ok(VecDeque::new())` — so `mesh.settings` observers kept the
   pre-write revision until some unrelated change republished. It now returns
   `self.sync_settings_service_state()` (the same revisioned-snapshot helper
   every other settings mutation already uses).

2. **`mark_components_theme_changed` / `mark_components_locale_changed`
   discarded their broadcast effects.** (`crates/core/shell/src/shell/runtime/
   theme.rs`) Both ended with `let _ = self.broadcast_core_event(…)?;` and
   returned `Result<(), _>`, dropping every `CoreRequest` a script subscriber
   emitted in response to `ThemeChanged` / `LocaleChanged`. Both now return
   `Result<VecDeque<CoreRequest>, ShellRunError>` and all ~13 call sites
   thread the effects: sites with a `requests` accumulator `extend` it;
   `apply_set_theme` / `apply_set_icon_theme` / `apply_set_font_family` /
   `apply_set_locale` / `reload_theme_if_changed` build/return them;
   `commit_pending_profile_switch`'s post-commit refresh (which also records
   the new `profile_post_commit_*_refresh_failed` diagnostics) extends
   `requests` on `Ok`; `run()` setup extends `pending`; and
   `commit_resource_snapshot`'s font-token refresh — whose callers
   (`resolve_modules`, profile activation) do not thread effects up the stack
   — pushes them onto `self.deferred_requests` for the next loop drain rather
   than dropping them.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib`
(748 passed, 24 failed — identical failure set to the run before this change
and to the documented 23-32 flaky baseline; `malformed_theme_reload_retains_
last_known_good_snapshot` and `failed_bound_write_rolls_back…` confirmed
failing identically with the change stashed out). No new test: exercising
`apply_set_module_prop`'s publish needs a full Shell fixture with a mounted
component exposing a configurable prop, which the current harness does not
provide; the change substitutes an already-tested helper call for an empty
return, and the event-effect threading is mechanical accumulator plumbing
covered by the existing `theme::` suite. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Reject un-honorable profile entrypoints; diagnose post-commit refresh failures

Section 15 finding #1 ("profile activation is not one atomic runtime
generation," audited 2026-08-20). The bulk of this finding has been shipped
since the audit: `apply_switch_profile` -> `poll_pending_resource_preparation`
-> `continue_profile_switch` -> `commit_pending_profile_switch` already stages
a candidate graph, frontend catalog, locale, async resource job, theme, and
backends started beside the live ones with buffered initial state, then a
single commit point. Two concrete gaps from the finding were still open and
tractable without the full `ActiveSnapshot` redesign (which is the separate
Section 15 item 13 / "unconstrained feature direction"):

1. **`entrypoint` was silently ignored.** `ProfileRootInstance.entrypoint`
   (`crates/core/extension/module/src/package/profile.rs:58-59`) is read
   nowhere in root retention/selection — `discovery.rs` cold start and
   `continue_profile_switch`/`commit_pending_profile_switch` compare only
   `root.module`, and `package.rs:304` takes only the module-id half of the
   legacy `RootLayoutSelection.entrypoint` via `.split(':').next()`. A profile
   declaring `entrypoint: "sidebar"` would mount `main` anyway. The frontend
   catalog compiles exactly one primary component per module, so there is no
   second entrypoint for a non-default value to select; honoring the field is
   a catalog-level multi-entrypoint feature, not a comparison fix. Per the
   audit ("implement that field or reject every value it cannot honor"),
   `ShellProfile::validate()` now rejects any root whose `entrypoint != "main"`
   with a diagnostic explaining that multiple entrypoints per module are not
   implemented. `validate()` runs on every disk profile load
   (`from_json_str`, `from_path`, `ProfilePaths::load`), so cold start and
   live switch now reject identically instead of one silently degrading. All
   shipped compositions/profiles use `entrypoint: "main"` (verified across
   `config/` and `modules/`), so no shipped configuration regresses.

2. **Post-commit refresh failures were silent.** After the commit point,
   `mark_components_theme_changed`, `mark_components_locale_changed`, and
   `apply_settings_to_components` failures in `commit_pending_profile_switch`
   only `tracing::warn!` and continued. True rollback there needs the
   `ActiveSnapshot` redesign; short of that, each now also records a
   `profile_post_commit_{theme,locale,settings}_refresh_failed` lifecycle
   diagnostic on `@mesh/shell` so a partial post-commit state is observable
   through the normal diagnostics surface, not just the log.

Backlog item stays open: the immutable `ActivationPlan`/`RuntimeGeneration`
value with candidate-preview hidden surfaces and post-commit rollback
(Section 15 item 13) is not done. This closes the two standalone gaps the
finding called out beyond that redesign.

**Verification.** New
`profile_root_with_a_non_main_entrypoint_is_rejected_rather_than_silently_ignored`
in `crates/core/extension/module/src/package/profile.rs` tests. `nix develop
--command cargo test -p mesh-core-module` (239 passed, 9 failed — identical
9-failure set with the change stashed out, all in the pre-existing dirty-tree
baseline: content_store, shipped-fixture, luau-scan, icon-pack). `nix develop
--command cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib` (748 passed, 24 failed;
stashed baseline 746 passed, 26 failed — every failure in the change run also
present in the baseline run, no new failure, matches the documented 23-32
flaky baseline). The post-commit diagnostic path is not separately
shell-tested: exercising it needs a live profile switch with a component that
rejects theme/locale refresh, which the current harness cannot stage; the
change only adds a diagnostic beside an existing `warn!`. No performance
claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Confirm opaque/blur/region state already commits without pixel damage

The backlog carried an open item asking to commit opaque, blur, and other
surface-state changes even when pixel damage is empty (Section 14 finding #7,
audited 2026-08-20: "Region-only changes have no guaranteed commit"). Tracing
the current code found it already implemented, by the same 2026-08-23
presentation lifecycle work that closed the neighboring findings without
updating this backlog line:

- `WaylandSurfaceBackend::commit_surface_state`
  (`crates/core/presentation/src/wayland_surface/backend/present.rs:204-228`)
  stages input region, opaque region, blur region, and window geometry via
  `prepare_surface_state`, issues a `wl_surface.commit()` when any of them has
  pending work (`PreparedSurfaceState::has_commit_work`), and marks each as
  committed — independent of any pixel damage.
- The shell only skips `present_with_damage` when both invisible-transition
  and pixel damage are absent
  (`crates/core/shell/src/shell/runtime/render/mod.rs:1188`); the `else if
  visible` branch (`:1249-1271`) calls `commit_surface_state` on every no-pixel-
  damage visible frame, handling `Committed`/`Unchanged`/`NotReady`/
  `SurfaceMissing`/`Err` the same way the damage path does.

Coverage already exists: `state_only_commit_reports_pending_region_work_once`
(`crates/core/presentation/src/lib.rs:2358-2404`) drives an opaque-region
update through an idle configured surface and asserts exactly one commit is
recorded, plus a not-configured retry case.

No code changed; the backlog line was stale.

**Verification.** `cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation --lib` (112 passed, 11
ignored), `cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib render` (46 passed, 1
pre-existing unrelated failure —
`settings_surface_renders_backend_pages_and_advanced_controls`, about a
settings-UI `set_prop` payload assertion, not surface-state commit —
reproduced on an unmodified tree). No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Confirm the idempotent surface teardown supervisor is already routed

The backlog carried an open item asking to route close, dismiss, parent
destruction, role replacement, and connection loss through one idempotent
teardown supervisor (Section 14 finding #2, audited 2026-08-20). Tracing each
path found it already implemented, mostly by the 2026-08-23 presentation
lifecycle work that landed after the audit but never closed the backlog line:
`State::teardown_surface_with_focus`
(`crates/core/presentation/src/wayland_surface/state.rs:816`) is the single
idempotent core — it cancels per-surface input/text-input/focus-grab
ownership, removes both surface identity indexes, and releases auxiliary
protocol objects — and every path reaches it: explicit `destroy_surface`/
`destroy_popup` and role replacement (`backend/surfaces.rs:636-663`, called
from `configure()`'s must-recreate branch), compositor layer `closed` and
popup `done` (`wayland_surface/handlers.rs:194,1102`, via
`teardown_surface_tree_after_compositor_event`/
`teardown_surface_after_compositor_event`), and parent destruction
(`teardown_surface_tree` tears down popup children before the parent). Only
`mark_connection_lost` (`state.rs:931`) is a separate function, deliberately:
the socket is already dead, so it cannot attempt protocol requests and instead
clears every identity/index directly, then publishes a
`SurfaceLifecycleEvent::Lost` per surface, matching `Closed`/`Dismissed` for
compositor-originated paths. Shell-initiated paths (explicit destroy, role
replacement) do not publish a lifecycle event because the shell already
performs its own child bookkeeping synchronously at the call site
(`crates/core/shell/src/shell/runtime/request.rs:1913-1914`), not because
notification was skipped.

No code changed; the backlog line was stale.

**Verification.** `cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation --lib` (112 passed, 11
ignored), `cargo test -p mesh-core-shell surface_layout` (21 passed), and
`cargo check --workspace` all passed. No performance claim is made.

The Hyprland backend now handles socket2 output one line at a time instead of
