## 2026-08-26 — Theme control asked for a capability name nothing grants

Compiling the `mesh.theme` contract earlier the same day made theme methods
routable again, and uncovered the next gate:

```
INFO  @mesh/settings published event mesh.theme.set_theme
WARN  denied unauthorized service command dispatch
      source_module_id="@mesh/settings" interface="mesh.theme"
      command="set_theme" required_capability=service.theme.control
```

The warning names the capability the settings module already holds. It is not
the capability that was checked.

`dispatch_service_command_with_call_id` computes `required` from
`service_capabilities()` — `service.theme.control` — and uses it only for the
log line. The authorization itself goes through
`InterfaceProxy::can_call_contract_method`. A contract with no declared method
policy falls back to `can_control`, and `can_control` special-cased `theme` to
`Capability::new("theme.control")`. That name appears nowhere else in the
tree: not in `mesh-core-capability`'s known set, not in
`docs/spec/01-module-system.md` (which lists `service.theme.control` for all
three theme methods), not in any shipped manifest, not in
`config/module.json`. Every caller was denied, and the denial explained itself
using the grant it had refused to consult.

The neighbouring `locale` arm is correct — `locale.write` is a real capability
— so `theme` now falls through to the generic
`has_service_capability(caps, "theme", "control")` path. The read arm stays,
because theme reads really are the short `theme.read` grant.

This stayed invisible while the contract failed to compile: with no contract
to resolve, `dispatch_service_command` took the
`source_capabilities.is_granted(required)` branch and checked the right name.
Registering the contract moved every theme command onto the broken branch.

**Two test gaps let it through.**

`a_core_provided_command_is_applied_by_the_shell_itself` did dispatch
`mesh.theme.set_theme` with `service.theme.control`, but on a bare
`Shell::new()` with no installed graph, so `apply_set_theme` returned early on
its graph-authorized catalog check and the test had been failing on a later
assertion for long enough to read as baseline noise. It now installs a graph
theme and asserts the active theme actually changed, which makes it a real
end-to-end check of the reported failure.

`graph_with_theme_source` built its theme module's manifest in memory but
never wrote `module.json` to disk. `parse_theme_css_file` reads the manifest
beside the stylesheet for the pack's declared identity, so
`prepare_theme_for_graph` failed with `Io { path: ".../module.json" }`, and
`apply_set_theme` swallowed it as a `tracing::warn!` and a no-op. Every test
using that helper was therefore asserting against an unchanged theme. The
helper now materializes the manifest.

Making those tests work revealed why nobody had noticed: applying a theme
persists the appearance override, and `Shell::new()` resolves the settings
path from the repository, so the first green run rewrote the developer's own
`config/settings.json` to `@mesh/test-theme:test-light-present`. The three
tests that reach `apply_set_theme` now take `isolated_settings_file`, which
holds the settings env lock and points `MESH_SETTINGS_PATH` at a temp file.

**Verification.** `cargo test -p mesh-core-scripting`: 256 passed, 0 failed.
Against the same tree before the fix,
`a_core_provided_command_is_applied_by_the_shell_itself` returned `ok: false`
from dispatch; after, it returns `ok: true`, `status: "applied"`, and a
changed `shell.theme.active().id`. `cargo test -p mesh-core-shell` went from
28 failures on the unchanged tree to 23 across two consecutive runs, with zero
failures not already in the baseline set — per this file's standing note the
sets were diffed, not the counts. Fixed:
`a_core_provided_command_is_applied_by_the_shell_itself`,
`set_theme_forces_full_present_on_existing_components`,
`set_theme_loads_css_package_and_updates_runtime_setting`, plus the two flaky
scheduler deadline tests. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean; clippy reports
nothing on the changed lines. Live theme switching in a running compositor
remains unverified here — no Wayland compositor in this environment. No
performance claim is made.

**Still open, both pre-existing and untouched here.**
`malformed_theme_reload_retains_last_known_good_snapshot`: the legacy
`MESH_THEME_DIR` JSON reload path retains the last-known-good theme correctly
but records no `theme_reload_rejected` diagnostic.

`mesh-core-theme`'s registry is process-global, so shell theme tests are
order-dependent in both directions —
`explicit_color_scheme_is_reported_without_id_heuristics` and
`theme_service_state_lists_every_registered_theme` each pass or fail depending
on what an earlier test registered, on an unchanged tree. Both belong with the
graph-authorized-catalog item in the backlog, which would remove the global
registry these tests are reading.

## 2026-08-26 — Restore theme switching: one bad field type disabled mesh.theme

With themes finally listed, selecting one failed on every surface:

```
frontend event handler failed component_id=@mesh/settings::AppearancePage
  handler=onThemeSelect
  error=operation rejected: Unknown shell channel 'mesh.theme.set_theme'
```

The channel was fine. The whole interface was missing. `mesh.theme` declared
its `fingerprint` state field as `integer?`, but the contract type vocabulary
is `string`, `int`, `float`, `boolean`, `object`, `any`, plus PascalCase named
types. `CompiledContract::compile` rejected the contract, and
`InterfaceRegistry::register_contract` discards that error — `let _ =
self.try_register_contract(contract)` — so the shell started with a catalog of
four built-in interfaces instead of five and no word about the fifth.

Live component dispatch authorizes published events with
`authorize_event_with_catalog`, which resolves the interface in that catalog
and rejects what it cannot find as `UnknownChannel`. So `set_theme`,
`set_icon_theme`, and `set_font_family` were unroutable from every frontend —
settings, `@mesh/theme-selector`, and quick settings alike.

The existing tests missed it because `script_events_to_requests` routes through
`authorize_event`, the shell-operation registry path, which returns `Ok(None)`
for any non-`shell.` channel and so never consults a catalog at all.

`fingerprint` is now `int?`. Built-in contracts register through
`register_builtin_contract`, which logs an error naming the interface and the
reason and `debug_assert!`s, because a malformed contract compiled into the
shell is a bug in that file rather than a survivable input.

**Verification.** Two new tests, each confirmed to fail with the field type put
back: `built_in_interfaces_compile_and_expose_their_methods` asserts all five
built-in interfaces resolve and that `mesh.theme` exposes its three methods,
and `a_capable_module_may_publish_the_theme_service_methods` drives the live
`authorize_event_with_catalog` path a component actually uses. The fix also
repaired two pre-existing failures in `mesh-core-shell` —
`shell_registers_theme_provider_for_frontend_theme_proxy` and
`theme_revision_events_mirror_the_rendered_snapshot_and_token_delta` — taking
that suite from 28 failures to 26 with no new ones and 736 passing.
`cargo test -p mesh-core-service` holds at its existing single failure
(`optional_feature_groups_are_additive_but_required_groups_are_breaking`).
Startup emits no warnings or errors. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` passed.

**Left open.** Contracts supplied by modules through
`register_interfaces_from_graph` still take the silent `register_contract`
path, so a bad field type in a module's interface disables it just as quietly.
Filed under Service contracts.

## 2026-08-26 — Ship the theme choice the settings surface was meant to offer

The Appearance page offered exactly one colour theme. Seven themes existed —
`catppuccin-mocha`, `gruvbox-dark`, `mesh-default-dark`, `mesh-default-light`,
`nord`, `solarized-dark`, `tokyo-night` — but none of them reached the
selector, for two independent reasons.

**They were not where modules are found.** They lived in `config/themes/`,
while discovery scans `modules/`, `$MESH_HOME/modules`, and
`/usr/share/mesh/modules`. Icon packs, the exact analogue, already ship as
`modules/icon-packs/*`, so themes now ship as `modules/themes/*`.

**They were not shaped like theme modules.** Each manifest declared
`mesh.theme`, which is the frontend-only surface-theming section; loading one
fails with *"mesh.theme is only supported for frontend modules"*. A theme pack
contributes through `mesh.provides.themes` with a `modes` map, a
`default_mode`, and per-mode `mode_metadata`. Every manifest was rewritten to
that shape, one mode each (`light` for `mesh-default-light`, `dark` for the
rest).

Two loaders followed the files. `load_theme_package_metadata` read a theme's id
and label from the legacy `mesh.theme` section and now reads the first
`mesh.provides.themes` entry, accepting either a literal label or the
manifest's translation record. `theme_dir_path` pointed at `config/themes` and
now points at `modules/themes` (with `$MESH_HOME/modules/themes` outside a
checkout), which also moves the embedded default-theme `include_str!` and the
LSP's theme registry.

**Identity.** The graph keys its catalog by scoped identity
(`@mesh/nord:nord`), which is what the theme engine registers and what the
service publishes, so the settings UI compares like with like. Settings and
hand-written configuration name themes the readable way (`theme.active` is
`tokyo-night`), which resolved to nothing and left the shell on its recovery
theme. `ThemeCatalog::get` now falls back to a bare local id when exactly one
pack claims it, and resolves to nothing when two do rather than guessing an
owner.

**Verification.** A development run against Hyprland discovers 32
graph-authorized modules, up from 25, registers all seven themes, and emits no
warnings or errors; before the change it registered none. New tests:
`shipped_themes_are_discoverable_modules_that_contribute_theme_packs` walks
`modules/themes` and asserts each is a valid theme module contributing one pack
whose stylesheets exist, and that at least five ship;
`theme_catalog_resolves_a_bare_local_id_when_one_pack_claims_it` and
`theme_catalog_refuses_to_guess_an_owner_for_an_ambiguous_local_id` cover the
new resolution. `cargo test -p mesh-core-theme` 25 passed.
`cargo test -p mesh-core-module` holds at its existing 9 failures, name for
name, with one more test passing. `cargo test -p mesh-tools-lsp` holds at its
existing single failure
(`locale_completion_matches_the_runtime_host_api`, unrelated).
`cargo test -p mesh-core-shell` returned 731 passed / 29 failed, inside the
28-32 range that tree gives across runs. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` passed.
No performance claim is made.

**Not done.** `config/modules/` still holds stale legacy `package.json`
manifests (`@mesh/shell-theme` among them) that no discovery path reads. The
bootstrap loader still resolves the active theme by scanning the theme
directory rather than going through the graph catalog; the Themes backlog item
was narrowed to that remainder rather than deleted.

## 2026-08-26 — Keep live animations in the retained dirty roots

The debug-only `retained display-list generation changed caller lineage`
warning fired on essentially every frame of a running shell, not just in short
bursts as the previous entry claimed. Each firing means the display list was
handed one retained generation for two materially different trees, so its
generation fast path would have reused stale entries; only the debug check
forced the rebuild that kept the frame correct. Release builds have no such
backstop.

The render loop paints a surface twice within one frame — the second pass
exists so a measured size can be honoured. Probing both passes showed the
shape of the defect:

- pass one takes the full retained update, finds `style: 3`, and advances the
  generation;
- pass two takes the scoped update with an **empty** dirty-root set, reports
  nothing dirty, and leaves the generation where it was — while the tree it
  hands the display list has moved, because the pass re-resolved a running
  `background-color` transition on the two `.ws-btn` workspace buttons.

The cause is the baseline `apply_style_animations_with_previous` compares
against. `previous_visual_styles` is captured inside the same pass, so it only
reports a change when that pass also advanced the value. On the second pass of
a frame the animation resolves to what the pass-local baseline already holds,
so the node is reported clean even though it moved relative to what the
retained tree recorded. `collect_scoped_update_nodes` then skips it entirely:
it visits only nodes in `dirty_roots` or with a changed layout fingerprint, and
an animating colour is neither.

A node whose animation is still running is now dirty by definition.
`apply_node_keyframe_animation` reports whether the node still has a running,
unfinished keyframe animation, `transitions.active_unfinished` already
reported the same for transitions, and either keeps the node in the animation
dirty roots regardless of what the pass-local baseline says.

**Verification.** A 60-second development run against Hyprland emits 29 log
lines with zero warnings and zero errors, down from a warning on every frame.
The regression test
(`a_live_transition_stays_dirty_while_its_delay_pins_the_value`) drives the
exact condition — a transition inside its delay window, where the value is
pinned but the animation is unmistakably live — and fails with the fix
reverted. `cargo test -p mesh-core-shell` animation suite 35 passed, transition
suite 19 passed. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` passed.

**Correction to the previous entry.** That entry called the remaining warning
bounded at "15 firings" and attributed the stable numbers to a settled
transition; both were wrong, drawn from a run that happened to be idle. It also
reported `-p mesh-core-shell` as holding at "730 passed / 29 failed" as though
that were a fixed baseline. Repeated runs of the same tree give 28, 29, and 32
failures, so the suite is flaky and a single count is not a baseline. The
meaningful comparison is the failure *set*: identical, name for name, with this
change applied and reverted.

## 2026-08-26 — Restore backend startup and quiet the startup log

Two shipped backends died on every startup with `sandbox instruction budget
exceeded (limit 1000000)`, and the settings loader reported its own bookkeeping
key as a typo of itself.

**Instruction accounting.** `ResourceBudget::checkpoint` charged
`INSTRUCTION_CHECKPOINT = 1_000` per interrupt firing, as though Luau
interrupted every thousandth instruction. It does not: measured against
`mlua`, the interrupt fires once per loop back-edge and about three times per
function call — `for i=1,1000 do s=s+i end` fires 1001 times and 1000 calls
fire 3001 times. The 1,000,000 budget therefore bought roughly 1,000 loop
iterations or 333 calls per callback. `@mesh/upower-power` and
`@mesh/hyprland-wm` exhausted it inside `start()`, so `mesh.power` and
`mesh.wm` never came up. A checkpoint now costs one unit, and
`SandboxConfig::instruction_budget` is documented as bounding checkpoints
rather than opcodes.

**Reserved settings key.** `validate_settings_root` listed `revision` among
`ROOT_KEYS` for suggestions but had no match arm for it, so the revision stamp
that `save_if_revision` writes fell through to the unknown-key path and
produced `unknown key "revision"; did you mean "revision"?` twice per startup.
`namespace_names` also failed to filter it, exposing it as a module namespace.
Both now treat it as reserved, and a non-integer stamp is reported plainly.

**Log volume.** Per-item startup enumeration moved to `debug`: XDG icon themes
(26 lines, replaced by a count), per-module discovery (25 lines, already
summarized), per-file frontend compilation (14 lines), per-module script loads,
and backend script loads. The startup narrative — modules discovered, IPC
listening, each backend runtime started and switched live, components mounted —
stays at `info`.

**Verification.** A development startup against Hyprland went from 90 log lines
with 3 errors and 2 backend failures to 29 lines with none: all five providers
(`mesh.audio`, `mesh.brightness`, `mesh.power`, `mesh.device`, `mesh.wm`) reach
`backend runtime started`, and `hyprland-wm` reports live workspace and window
state. `cargo test -p mesh-core-config` 48 passed, `-p mesh-core-scripting` 255
passed with 26 ignored, `-p mesh-core-render` 252+6+1 passed.
`-p mesh-core-shell` holds at its existing 730 passed / 29 failed / 130
ignored, `-p mesh-core-presentation` at its existing 2 failures, and
`mesh-core-frontend`'s single failure
(`phase87_layout_display_source_semantics_survive_lowering`) reproduces with
the change stashed. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check` passed.
No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** The debug-only `retained display-list generation changed caller
lineage` warning still fires — 15 times in a 40-second run — while the
workspace buttons' `background-color` transition animates. Slot-by-slot
probing narrowed it to `PaintInput::material` on the two `.ws-btn` nodes:
animation sampling changes their computed colors without advancing
`RetainedTree::generation()`, so the display list receives one generation for
two different trees and only the debug check forces the correct rebuild. Filed
under Rendering and paint in the backlog rather than silenced.

## 2026-08-26 — Stop spanning layer surfaces from freezing after their first configure

A `wl_layer_surface` whose width spans its output puts the protocol's `0` on
the wire regardless of the resolved extent. `SurfacePolicySnapshot::diff`
compared the *resolved* `surface_size` instead, so the first frame in which a
spanning bar learned its real output width (a `(1, 256)` placeholder becoming
`(2880, 256)`) was classified `LayerConfigure`. `SurfaceEntry::apply_config`
then cleared `configured` and waited for a fresh configure event — one the
compositor had no reason to send, because every request it received was
byte-identical to the last. `surface_ready_to_present` stayed false forever
and `render_components` skipped the surface on every later frame.

The visible result was a shipped navigation bar frozen a few frames after
startup: `:hover` restyles resolved and invalidated correctly but never
reached a buffer, service-driven content such as the volume threshold icon
never repainted, and the clock stopped. Input was never at fault — pointer
hit-testing, hover pseudo-state, and handler dispatch all ran normally against
a tree whose pixels were no longer presented.

`layer_geometry_changed` now compares the per-axis *wire* size, with a
spanning axis contributing `None` on both sides, and `measurement_changed`
compares resolved `content_size`/`surface_size` rather than only their
`is_none()` transitions. A resolved-size change with an unchanged wire size is
therefore `MeasureAgain`: not a noop (the entry still adopts the new config
and the surface repaints at the new extent), but not a fresh configure either.

**Verification.** `cargo test -p mesh-core-surface-policy` (8 passed) covering
two new cases: a spanning axis resolving its output size diffs to
`MeasureAgain` and does not require a fresh configure, and a fixed-size layer
axis resizing still diffs to `LayerConfigure` and does. `cargo fmt --all --
--check` and `git diff --check` passed. `cargo test -p mesh-core-shell` went
from 30 to 29 failures against this working tree (730 passed, 130 ignored);
the remainder are the tree's existing in-flight failures.
`cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation` holds at its existing 2 failures.

Confirmed live against Hyprland on a 2880x1800 output: before the fix the bar
rendered a partial first frame (no workspace list, no window title) and never
changed again; after it, workspaces, window title, and clock are live, hovering
`.nav-button` paints the `--color-secondary-container` fill with the filled
glyph axis, and driving the sink volume through 15% / 50% / 85% moves the
navigation icon across `audio-volume-low` / `-medium` / `-high`.

## 2026-08-26 — Clean development-shell startup diagnostics

Fixed the shipped startup failures in the settings metadata, composition
editor, debug inspector, and navigation-bar locale bindings. Backend startup
now handles large `/proc/cpuinfo` and `/etc/os-release` payloads with linear
scans, avoids denied optional commands, and keeps expected Hyprland fallback
messages at debug level. Core-owned service providers no longer produce false
missing-provider warnings, and transitional test/migration-only Rust helpers
are explicitly classified so normal CLI builds remain quiet.

**Verification.** `cargo check -p mesh-tools-cli` completed without compiler
warnings; `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check` passed; the full
`mesh-core-scripting` library passed 253 tests with 26 ignored; focused device,
PipeWire, and shell lifecycle tests passed. A startup smoke run emitted only
the expected no-compositor and unavailable-battery environment warnings before
the sandbox rejected the IPC socket filesystem operation; no module compile,
template, locale, capability, or instruction-budget errors remained.

## 2026-08-25 — Validate manifest surface policy with canonical parsers

The manifest model now owns the canonical surface role, decoration, edge,
layer, and keyboard-mode parsers and normalized value lists. Graph diagnostics
use those parsers to report every invalid `mesh.surface` enum with its path,
value, and allowed canonical values, and role-specific field checks now use the
same parser so aliases cannot select a different policy. Surface settings
resolution reuses the manifest parser set rather than maintaining a second
acceptance table.

**Verification.** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`,
`cargo test -p mesh-core-module --lib package::tests::surfaces --no-fail-fast`
(7 passed), `cargo test -p mesh-core-surface-config` (31 passed), and
`cargo check --workspace` passed. The full `cargo test -p mesh-core-module
--lib` run reached 235 passed, 8 existing baseline failures, and 3 ignored;
the failures were the known content-store permission, script-only diagnostic,
and shipped-fixture families.

## 2026-08-25 — Keep promotable manifest-only in settings and ejection

`mesh-core-surface-config` now treats `mesh.surface.promotable` as an
author-only manifest capability: it is excluded from user settings validation,
shell discovery schema output, LSP settings documentation, and config
ejection. Stored `surface.promotable` values are diagnosed as unknown and
cannot change the manifest-derived capability or suppress inert role-field
diagnostics. Ejection still retains effective role-specific placement fields;
the capability remains owned by `module.json`.

**Verification.** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`,
`cargo test -p mesh-core-surface-config` (31 passed), and `cargo check
--workspace` passed. The focused shell settings run reached 35 passed, 9
failed, and 1 ignored; its failures are the existing unrelated fixture,
service, navigation, and real-surface failures. `cargo test -p mesh-tools-lsp`
reached 88 passed and 1 existing unrelated locale-completion failure in
`analyzer::tests::locale_completion_matches_the_runtime_host_api`.

## 2026-08-25 — Enforce the author-only promotable role guard

`mesh-core-surface-config` now owns the shared role-transition authorization
policy: stored role settings for non-promotable surfaces are rejected with a
diagnostic and cannot change the effective role. The frontend component keeps
the same guard at its live role mutation and settings-reload boundaries, while
the shell request path uses the shared policy for typed and raw IPC role
requests. Regression coverage covers non-promotable settings, promotable
settings, direct live changes, and a raw IPC promotion request.

**Verification.** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo test -p
mesh-core-surface-config` (30 passed), the focused shell role suite (8 passed),
the focused settings/live-role regression (1 passed), and `cargo check
--workspace` passed. The full `cargo test -p mesh-core-shell` run reached 680
passed, 77 failed, and 130 ignored; the failures are the existing unrelated
fixture/environment and profiling/real-surface failures, including the same
baseline families recorded in the current runtime and reload log entries.

## 2026-08-25 — Introduce a typed frame paint plan

`mesh-core-render` now publishes an immutable `FramePaintPlan` snapshot for
each retained display-list frame. The typed plan keeps resource/tree inputs,
ordered command topology and generation, cumulative transforms and ancestor
clips, effect regions, atomic replay spans/layer scopes, and sparse logical
damage together. Its damage contract converts logical edges to exact
floor/ceil device coverage and clipped buffer/Wayland rectangles through the
shared fractional-scale policy. Retained damage, command, effect, and paint
selection accessors now read from the plan snapshot, so painter-facing
selection does not reconstruct state from a filtered command stream.

Regression coverage proves plan snapshot immutability across topology changes,
transform retention, replay metadata, effect/damage accessors, and fractional
logical-to-device edge coverage.

**Verification.** The focused frame-plan regression passed; the full
`mesh-core-render` library passed 251 tests with 36 ignored; `cargo check -p
mesh-core-shell` passed. An initial parallel full-render run observed one
transient `skia_effect_linear_gradient_reuses_shader_for_moving_same_size_rects`
cache-count failure (2 vs 1); the isolated test and immediate full rerun both
passed. `git diff --check` and formatting validation passed.

## 2026-08-25 — Introduce per-module runtime sessions

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and sandbox

Added the public `RuntimeSession` boundary in `mesh-core-scripting`. A session
binds one module generation to its lazy sandboxed Luau realm, capability host
identity, shared resource broker, lifecycle state machine, Rust-owned
transactional state, supervisor cancellation, health, exponential backoff, and
explicit quarantine. Backend script contexts now use the session-owned realm
and publish load/start/running/stopping/stopped transitions through it.

**Verification.** The focused session and backend lifecycle regression passed;
the full `mesh-core-scripting` library passed 253 tests with 26 ignored;
`mesh-core-backend` passed 35 tests; workspace checking, formatting, and
`git diff --check` passed. The focused shell backend-lifecycle filter passed 27
tests and retained the three existing supervision/fixture failures documented
in the August 24 runtime-lifecycle entry.

## 2026-08-24 — Stage backend top-level host effects

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and sandbox

Backend source loading now executes in a startup-staged phase. Mutating
service publication, process execution and streams, provider event
subscription/publication, logging, and `self.storage` writes are rejected
until `start(self)` begins. Runtime callbacks enable the same handles after
startup, while top-level declarations and read-only host access remain
available. Regression tests prove that top-level stream, event, service
publication, and durable-storage operations cannot escape a failed load.

**Verification.** The focused top-level staging tests passed 4 tests; the full
backend scripting filter passed 94 tests; `mesh-core-backend` passed 32 tests;
workspace checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The full
`mesh-core-scripting` library reached 242 passed, 1 existing shared-VM
memory-retention failure, and 26 ignored; the failing test was reproduced in
isolation and is unrelated to backend startup staging. No performance claim is
made.

## 2026-08-24 — Swap fresh Luau environments on frontend reload

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and frontend reload

Frontend source reload now compiles and publishes a candidate catalog, creates
a fresh Luau context with a new generation identity, and swaps the replacement
root only after initialization succeeds. Removed globals and handlers cannot
survive the swap; a failed candidate restores the last-good catalog and runtime
while the explicit scoped `self.storage` document remains durable. Lifecycle
metadata now reports module, component, instance, diagnostics, and environment
generation identities separately.

**Verification.** The focused reload suite passed 5 tests, the lifecycle hook
regression passed, and the invalidation suite passed 58 tests with one existing
pixel-equivalence profiling failure. The full `mesh-core-scripting` library
passed 240 tests with 26 ignored, workspace checking and formatting passed, and
the full `mesh-core-shell` library had 676 passing tests, 77 unrelated
fixture/environment failures, and 130 ignored tests.

## 2026-08-24 — Publish initial provider state before readiness

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and provider activation

Backend startup now publishes the initial state produced by `start(self)`
before its `Started` lifecycle record. The shell's existing pending-provider
gate can therefore validate and stage the snapshot before atomically making
the provider active and publishing readiness; startup interface events remain
ordered after readiness.

**Verification.** The ordering regression passed; the full
`mesh-core-backend` library passed 32 tests, and the focused shell readiness
test passed. Workspace checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
The broader `mesh-core-shell` backend-lifecycle filter had 27 passing tests
and 3 failures in unrelated lifecycle/fixture assertions; those failures were
also reproduced individually.

## 2026-08-24 — Preserve backend self and host handles

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and sandbox

Backend runtime generations now cache one Lua `self` table. The table keeps its
storage proxy and lazily-created provider event channels stable across `start`,
poll, command, stream, and stop callbacks, so subscriptions and authored
handle identity are not lost between callbacks. A backend regression covers
self identity, storage-handle identity, persisted storage reads, and an event
subscription registered during `start` and fired during `on_poll`.

**Verification.** The focused stable-self regression passed; the full
`mesh-core-scripting` library passed 239 tests with 26 ignored;
`mesh-core-backend` passed 31 tests; workspace checking, formatting, and
`git diff --check` passed.

## 2026-08-24 — Make backend command dispatch typed, correlated, transactional, coalescable, generation-aware, and bounded

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and sandbox

Backend startup now installs an immutable interface command registry. Payload
arguments and declared results are validated at the runtime boundary, implicit
top-level helper functions are no longer callable as commands, command-result
conversion and handler failures roll back state and pending side effects, and
ordinary command failures settle through their correlated terminal result
without a provider lifecycle failure.

Production command ingress now uses a bounded Tokio queue and bounded batches,
with payload byte/depth limits, bounded coalescing keys that preserve distinct
targets, queue-full settlement, and stale-generation terminal results. Call
controls and command results carry runtime generations through shell routing,
and provider candidates pass their typed registry into the backend runtime.

**Verification.** The scripting backend filter (77 passed), full
`mesh-core-backend` library tests (31 passed), stale-generation regression,
shell service-contract filter (15 passed), workspace checking, formatting, and
`git diff --check` passed. The full `mesh-core-shell` library run had 674
passing tests, 75 fixture/environment failures, and 130 ignored tests; its
failures were concentrated in existing frontend catalog, real-surface, theme,
debug, graph, and state-shape assertions outside the focused dispatch tests.

## 2026-08-24 — Move default runtime storage to secure durable XDG state

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and sandbox

Default runtime storage now uses `$XDG_STATE_HOME/mesh/runtime-storage`, with
the user state directory fallback `$HOME/.local/state/mesh/runtime-storage`.
Storage roots, scope directories, lock files, documents, backups, and temporary
files are owner-only and reject symlinked or non-regular paths. Scope paths are
bounded by readable prefixes and SHA-256 identity suffixes, and documents
enforce bounded keys plus the existing per-context byte quota.

Writes now use an envelope with schema and monotonic revisions, an exclusive
writer lock, owner-only temporary files, file and directory synchronization,
backup replacement, stale-writer rejection, and recovery of valid backups or
fully written interrupted temporaries. Legacy plain JSON documents migrate on
their next write, and malformed or oversized documents are diagnosed and
preserved before recovery.

**Verification.** Storage tests (15 passed, 2 ignored), the full scripting
library (231 passed, 26 ignored), workspace checking, formatting, and
`git diff --check` passed. The broad workspace library test retained one
unrelated `mesh-core-config` failure in
`settings::tests::the_repository_settings_file_is_clean`: the repository
settings fixture contains an unknown root `revision` key.

## 2026-08-24 — Replace basename executable grants with canonical path/argument capability policy

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and sandbox

Backend `mesh.exec` and `mesh.exec_stream` authorization now uses structured
`exec.argv:<program>:<json-args>` grants. The host resolves each granted
executable to a canonical regular executable path, matches the complete
argument vector with exact, single-argument glob, or explicit any-argument
rules, and launches the authorized canonical path while preserving the
requested argv0 for multi-call binaries. The explicit high-risk
`exec.command` capability remains the unrestricted override; basename grants
and the shell-specific grant are no longer accepted. Shipped backend manifests,
root approvals, test fixtures, and the module-system specification now use the
structured policy, and `mesh.service.can_exec(program, args)` exposes the same
decision to backend scripts.

**Verification.** Capability tests (5), executable-policy tests (6), backend
runtime tests (86), the full scripting library (221 passed, 26 ignored), and
manifest tests (33) passed. Workspace checking and formatting passed. The
broader module library retained 8 unrelated baseline failures among 233
passing tests, and the shell service-contract filter retained one unrelated
invalid-result assertion failure among 14 passing tests; neither failure
exercises the executable policy. The CLI update-policy filter passed the new
structured-capability approval regression; its full 9-test filter retained one
unrelated provider state-fixture assertion failure.

## 2026-08-24 — Make event subscriber dispatch unsubscribe-safe

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and sandbox

Frontend/interface and backend self-event channels now allocate monotonic
subscription IDs and dispatch a sorted snapshot of those IDs. An unsubscribe
during dispatch cannot create a sequence hole that skips later subscribers;
removed callbacks are skipped and newly added callbacks wait for the next
emission. Each subscriber callback failure is reported independently through
the runtime warning path while remaining callbacks continue, and backend
provider events are still queued after a callback failure.

**Verification.** Focused frontend/interface event tests (11) and backend
self-event tests (2) passed. The full `mesh-core-scripting` library passed
212 tests with 26 ignored; `mesh-core-backend` passed 29 tests. Formatting and
`git diff --check` passed. The `mesh-core-shell service` filter retained the
repository's unrelated fixture baseline at 99 passed, 9 failed, and 14
ignored; the isolated service-observation fixture failures reproduce without
exercising the changed event dispatch paths.

**Left open.** Provider-owned event publication and stable backend `self`
handles remain separate backlog items.

## 2026-08-24 — Emit immutable compiled frontend revisions

`working tree` · area: frontend compiler and effect authorization

Frontend compilation now exposes immutable, content-derived
`CompiledFrontendRevision` artifacts, and the shell catalog stores compiled
roots through that shared copy-on-write boundary. Typed frontend effects carry
the catalog/runtime pair that produced them; frame publication binds effects to
its pair, while the normal shell adapter and live script-event path reject
missing or obsolete revisions before lowering requests.

**Verification.** ABI revision tests (3), compiler revision regression (1),
frontend host tests (5), the shell frame regression (1), focused crate checking,
formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The full frontend library retained
its known phase87 failure (81 passed, 1 failed, 20 ignored); the full shell
library retained the repository's broad fixture baseline (673 passed, 76
failed, 130 ignored), including catalog fixtures, service/real-surface,
backend, graph, theme, and pixel-equivalence failures.


## 2026-08-24 — Publish revisioned frontend frames

`working tree` · area: frontend host frame boundary

Frontend components now publish an immutable `FrontendFrame` after a completed
paint. The frame binds the immutable tree snapshot to the catalog, runtime,
tree, and service revisions, capability-safe service observations, typed
invalidation and diagnostics, child-surface/paint metadata, and accumulated
host or renderer-neutral effects. The shell component contract exposes the
last publication, and mixed tree/service revisions are rejected at construction
time. The follow-up for immutable compiled revisions and stale-effect
authorization remains open.

**Verification.** Focused frontend-host frame tests passed (4), the shell frame
publication regression passed, focused frontend-host/shell checking passed,
formatting and `git diff --check` passed. The full shell library retained its
existing baseline of 676 passed, 73 failed, and 130 ignored; failures were in
unrelated catalog fixtures, service/real-surface, backend, graph, theme, and
pixel-equivalence tests.

## 2026-08-24 — Reject local/module import alias collisions

`working tree` · area: component import namespace

Component script parsing now tracks every explicit import and `require()` alias
with its typed target. Local component and frontend-module aliases cannot share
one name; cross-kind collisions are rejected with a source-owned import
diagnostic before template lowering. The same namespace check covers imports
mixed with `require()` bindings, while ordinary duplicate aliases retain their
existing diagnostic.

**Verification.** The focused parser and compiler collision regressions passed;
the full component crate passed (88 tests), workspace checking passed, and
format/diff checks passed. The full frontend crate reached 80 passed, 1
existing phase87 layout/display/source-semantics failure, and 20 ignored. The
full shell library run reached 676 passed, 73 existing broad fixture failures,
and 130 ignored.

## 2026-08-24 — Dispatch frontend lifecycle hooks

`working tree` · area: frontend compiler and host

Frontend runtime creation now dispatches authored `mount` after `init`. Reloads
and catalog replacements dispatch `unmount` before runtime removal; module and
profile deactivation, same-surface profile replacement, shutdown, and
drop/rollback paths do likewise. Runtime teardown flushes storage and drains
side channels, and source-reload coverage verifies hook ordering.

**Verification.** Focused shell source-reload (4), lifecycle (1), and profile
(10) tests plus scripting lifecycle (8) tests passed. Full shell and scripting
libraries retain unrelated baseline failures: shell 672 passed, 72 failed, 130
ignored; scripting 201 passed, 1 failed, 26 ignored.

## 2026-08-24 — Guard frontend catalog rollback generations

`working tree` · area: frontend catalog publication

Frontend catalog rollback now compares the candidate generation while holding
the catalog write lock. If another replacement has published since the
candidate, the failed activation skips rollback and retains that newer
catalog. A concurrent replacement regression covers the stale rollback case.

**Verification.** The focused stale-rollback test passed; formatting,
`git diff --check`, and `cargo check --workspace` passed. The catalog slice
reached 7 passed and 3 existing fixture failures, with 2 ignored. The broad
shell library run reached 666 passed, 72 existing failures, and 130 ignored;
the failures remain in unrelated catalog fixtures, real-surface/service,
theme, backend, and graph tests.

## 2026-08-24 — Replace line-oriented Luau symbol discovery

`working tree` · area: Luau parser metadata and authoring consumers

Shared `full_moon` metadata now records statically resolvable interface event
subscriptions, backend service payload fields, and `on_command_*` handlers.
Frontend graph diagnostics and LSP backend shape completion consume that
metadata; the former line-by-line alias/member and indentation scans are gone.
Comments, strings, multiline calls, and multiline declarations no longer affect
these symbol results.

**Verification.** Focused component metadata, graph subscription, graph
diagnostic, and LSP backend-shape tests passed. The component library passed 84
tests; workspace checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The full
LSP library reached 87 passed and retained its existing
`locale_completion_matches_the_runtime_host_api` failure. The broader module
library reported content-store permission, script-only fixture, and
shipped-fixture failures.

## 2026-08-24 — Scope frontend local component aliases

`working tree` · area: frontend component import resolution

Compiler-produced local component records now use only owner-scoped keys that
carry the canonical owner source, import alias, and canonical target source.
Same-owner alias collisions compare typed import targets and retain both
canonical targets in the diagnostic; recursive owners can reuse an alias
without shadowing one another. Shell composition and catalog validation now
query the scoped index, while alias-only fallback remains limited to manually
assembled compatibility fixtures. Ambiguous scoped records fail closed rather
than selecting an arbitrary map entry.

**Verification.** The focused compiler suite passed (18 tests), the new
ambiguous-owner lookup regression passed, the relevant shell memoization test
passed, shell test-target checking and workspace checking passed, and format
and diff checks passed. The full frontend library retained its existing
`phase87_layout_display_source_semantics_survive_lowering` failure (80 passed,
1 failed, 20 ignored).

## 2026-08-24 — Unify frontend root invalidation dependencies

`working tree` · area: frontend catalog invalidation

Catalog changes now build one reverse dependency graph from both primary roots
and extension-point contribution roots. Root ownership, module imports, and
host contribution consumers are traversed together across the previous and
candidate catalog generations, so a module imported only by a contribution
invalidates its contributing module and every host surface that renders it.

**Verification.** The contribution-only dependency regression passed, the
three source-reload regressions passed, and the workspace check passed.
The broader invalidation slice reached 54 passed and 13 ignored tests but
retained the existing `phase98_pixel_equivalence_backend_update` failure; that
pixel-equivalence test does not exercise catalog invalidation.

## 2026-08-24 — Split frontend effects from shell policy

`working tree` · area: frontend host ABI

Added the renderer-neutral `mesh-core-frontend-abi` crate with typed surface,
service, locale, clipboard, and debug effects. Effects carry their module and
instance identity in an immutable capability scope; the shell-side adapter
authorizes that scope before lowering effects into the existing request and
Wayland vocabulary. Published frontend service and shell events now pass
through the typed effect path, preserving service caller identity and
capabilities while keeping shell authorization and backend policy at the
adapter boundary. The crate boundary documentation now records the new
dependency direction.

**Verification.** Focused ABI and host-adapter tests passed (4 tests); focused
shell effect-routing tests passed (9 tests); and `cargo check --workspace`
passed. Formatting and `git diff --check` also passed.

## 2026-08-24 — Preserve visibility transition endpoints

`working tree` · area: interaction/motion animation

Transition snapshots now retain the previously displayed discrete visibility
value, and visibility interpolation follows its edge timing: entrances become
visible at the start while exits remain visible until the transition endpoint.
Other discrete visibility values still switch at the midpoint. Regression tests
cover the animator path and direct interpolation behavior.

**Verification.** `mesh-core-animation` passed 26 tests plus doc tests;
`mesh-core-elements` animation-focused tests passed 19 with 1 ignored; focused
`mesh-core-shell` animation transition tests passed 2; and `cargo fmt --all --
--check` passed. The broad `mesh-core-shell --lib` run reached 654 passing, 72
failing, and 130 ignored tests; its failures are the repository's existing
module, surface, service, and fixture failures rather than focused animation
failures.

## 2026-08-24 — Consolidate interaction ownership transaction

`working tree` · area: interaction/input state

Added the renderer-neutral `InteractionState` and staged
`InteractionTransaction` in `mesh-core-interaction`. Focus, pointer capture,
press origin, gesture/touch owners, scroll owners, eligibility reconciliation,
revisioning, and typed changed-node invalidation now commit as one delta. The
shell routes pointer presses/releases, focus transfer and auto-focus, gesture
begin/end, touch ownership, scrollbar drags, wheel/touchpad scrolling, surface
hiding, and stale-tree cleanup through that owner state while retaining its
existing key snapshots for script payloads and render lookup.

**Verification.** `mesh-core-interaction` passed 34 tests (12 ignored), focused
shell activation passed 14, focused shell gesture tests passed 6, focused
element event tests passed 5, workspace checking passed, and formatting/diff
checks passed. The broader shell policy/navigation slices still report the
repository's existing real-surface/audio fixture failures; no new focused
ownership or activation failure was observed.

## 2026-08-24 — Use one affine transform/clip contract

`working tree` · area: interaction/rendering geometry

Added the shared affine matrix, inverse point mapping, transformed AABB, and
exact overflow clip stack to the element interaction contract. Hit testing,
focus geometry, scrolling and scrollbars, event dispatch, tooltip bounds, and
retained paint/damage/blur bounds now compose the same transform through
absolute layout coordinates and scroll containers. Transform origin is included
in retained render invalidation, and rotated clips use inverse membership while
paint culling keeps conservative AABBs. The shell damage path now consumes the
same transformed bounds.

**Verification.** Formatting and diff checks passed. The full interaction suite
passed (31, with 12 ignored), the full render suite passed (226, with 37
ignored), focused element contract tests passed (6), and focused shell damage
tests passed (4). The full elements suite reached 245 passing tests, 10
existing style/theme/prop fixture failures, and 57 ignored; those failures are
unchanged and unrelated to affine geometry.

## 2026-08-24 — Share interaction visibility and geometry policy

`working tree` · area: interaction/rendering policy

Added the shared `NodeEligibility` and `InteractionTarget` contract in the
element core. Paint, pointer, focus, gesture, scroll, tooltip, and semantic
walks now share visibility, disabled/inert propagation, geometry gating, and
target filtering. Interaction and rendering also use the same current
translation/axis-aligned scale geometry; accessibility keeps disabled controls
with their disabled state while excluding inert and aria-hidden subtrees.

**Verification.** Formatting and package checks for elements, interaction,
render, and shell passed. The full interaction suite passed (30, with 12
ignored), the full render suite passed (263 tests, with release-only ignored
benchmarks), and focused contract, event, accessibility, and paint-parity
tests passed. The full elements suite reached 243 passing tests, 10 existing
style/theme/prop fixture failures, and 57 ignored tests; those failures remain
outside this change.

**Left open.** Full affine rotation/clipping remains the next interaction
follow-up, and the separate disabled/inert activation backlog item remains
open for captured-target coverage.

## 2026-08-24 — Preserve additional Codex worktree changes

`working tree` · area: developer automation

The backlog runner now validates the primary one-item commit first, then
preserves any additional tracked or untracked worktree changes in a separate
recovery commit instead of aborting after a successful Codex turn. The default
clean-worktree guard remains in place; `--allow-dirty` explicitly permits
resuming work with existing changes. The development guide now points to the
tracked runner and its supported options.

**Verification.** `bash -n scripts/codex-backlog-runner.sh`, the runner dry-run,
and `git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Immutable frame snapshot boundary

`working tree` · area: UI element core frame publication

Added `FrameSnapshot`, which owns immutable node records, complete phase
stamps, stable mesh-key/runtime identities, normalized semantic projections,
and deterministic semantic diffs. Snapshot construction rejects duplicate
identities and non-finite geometry/accessibility values. The shell captures the
snapshot after layout and semantic normalization while retaining the mutable
tree for input-side work. Deterministic property-style generators cover
identity uniqueness, keyed reordering, semantic-field diffs, phase completion,
and invalid numeric inputs.

**Verification.** Focused frame tests: 4 passed. The full elements library:
238 passed, 10 existing style/theme/prop fixture failures, and 57 ignored.
Workspace checks for `mesh-core-shell`, `mesh-core-render`, and
`mesh-core-interaction` passed; `git diff --check` passed.

**Left open.** Shared visibility, transformed geometry, disabled/inert
eligibility, and target filtering across interaction and downstream consumers.

## 2026-08-24 — Broad shell validation note

`working tree` · area: validation

The full `mesh-core-shell` library run reached 651 passing tests, 72 failing
tests, and 130 ignored tests. The failures were in existing manifest/source
fixtures, service-script fixtures, dirty theme/config expectations, and real
surface baselines; no frame-snapshot assertion failed. The focused frame suite,
elements suite result, and dependent crate checks remain the validation gates
for this change.

## 2026-08-23 — Preserve retained style property masks

`working tree` · area: UI element core retained style resolution

`ComputedStyle` now carries shared explicit and inherited masks for inherited
text properties. Indexed and uncached declaration application record the same
authored mask, retained inheritance copies only properties without an explicit
declaration, and targeted restyle recomputes and preserves the masks. The
compiler uses the shared mask type, including shorthand font declarations,
instead of maintaining a separate bit vocabulary.

**Verification.** The retained transparent/default-value regression passes;
the resolver suite passes (48, with 37 ignored), compiler inherited-mask tests
pass (3, with 1 ignored), and workspace checking, formatting, and
`git diff --check` pass. The full elements library reaches 229 passing tests,
10 existing style/theme/prop fixture failures, and 57 ignored tests. The full
frontend library retains its unrelated phase-87 layout-display expectation
failure (74 passed, 1 failed, 20 ignored).

**Left open.** The next open backlog item is deriving accessibility focus from
canonical live focus state during snapshot generation.

## 2026-08-23 — Detect CSS custom-property cycles

`working tree` · area: UI element core style resolution

CSS custom-property resolution now carries a per-resolution dependency stack
through exact, embedded, typed, and animation value paths. Direct and indirect
cycles are bounded and reported as structured `StyleDiagnostic` records with
their dependency paths. Cyclic declarations are treated as invalid at computed
value time, preserving the prior computed property value, while explicit and
nested `var()` fallbacks remain usable and still retain the cycle diagnostic.

**Verification.** Focused diagnostics tests (12) pass; value-resolution tests
pass (19, with 17 ignored), and the resolver suite passes (48, with 37
ignored). The full elements library reaches 228 passing tests, 10 existing
style/theme/prop fixture failures, and 57 ignored tests. Workspace checking,
formatting, and `git diff --check` pass.

**Left open.** The next open backlog item is preserving explicit/inherited
property masks through retained style resolution and targeted restyle.

## 2026-08-23 — Generate the canonical element schema

`working tree` · area: UI element core contracts and compiler lowering

The element kind vocabulary now aliases the component source-tag vocabulary,
and one canonical schema generates source/runtime tag pairs, runtime type
definitions and fields, family-specific attributes, event sets, style hooks,
accessibility defaults, and source-specific input defaults. Compiler lowering,
accessibility construction, and input-default application consume that shared
mapping. Contract/type metadata now exposes explicit source and runtime tags;
unknown type and contract lookups no longer silently become `Box`, and element
validation reports structured unknown-tag diagnostics. Image lowering now
retains `image` rather than incorrectly lowering to `icon`.

**Verification.** Focused schema/diagnostic tests (5), compiler tag tests (4),
LSP element-knowledge tests (7), workspace checking, formatting, and
`git diff --check` pass. The full elements library reaches 225 passing tests,
10 existing style/theme/prop fixture failures, and 57 ignored tests. The
frontend attribute-focused run reaches 8 passing tests with its existing
visible-descendant-label failure; the full LSP run retains its existing locale
completion failure.

**Left open.** CSS custom-property cycle detection with structured diagnostics
and specified invalid-value fallback.

## 2026-08-23 — Unify stateful element input dispatch

`working tree` · area: UI element core interaction

The exported event dispatcher is now one stateful route, with `InputState` as
its compatibility alias. Pointer presses retain their origin and capture
release/move delivery; raw `pointerup` is distinct from primary-button
activation (`click`); focus moves only to eligible, non-disabled targets;
removed or invalid targets cancel safely; and every dispatch reports the nodes
whose interaction state requires restyle. Added parity regressions for
drag-off/drag-back, secondary buttons, disabled ancestors, keyboard focus,
removed origins, and invalidation output.

**Verification.** Focused element event tests (5), interaction tests (24
active, 12 ignored), dependent package checks, formatting, and
`git diff --check` pass. The full elements library reaches 221 passing tests,
10 existing style/theme/prop fixture failures, and 57 ignored tests.

**Left open.** The next open backlog item is the canonical schema for element
types, source/runtime tags, contracts, events, attributes, style hooks, and
accessibility defaults.

## 2026-08-23 — Make retained layout transactional

`working tree` · area: UI element core retained layout

Retained layout synchronization now propagates Taffy update failures instead
of continuing with a partially updated cache. Incremental and structural Taffy
failures invalidate retained state without zeroing live geometry, so the next
frame rebuilds from the current tree while the last-known-good rectangles
remain visible. Added an injected same-size failure/recovery regression.

**Verification.** The retained-layout regression and all 51 active layout
tests pass; the complete elements library reaches 218 passing tests, 10
existing style/theme/prop fixture failures, and 57 ignored tests. Package
checks for elements, frontend, and shell, formatting, and `git diff --check`
pass.

**Left open.** The next open backlog item is the unified stateful input
dispatcher with pointer capture, press-origin identity, activation semantics,
focus eligibility, and invalidation output.

## 2026-08-23 — Match compiler inheritance against element state

`working tree` · area: UI element core compiler styling

Compiler inherited-style masks now receive the resolved authored
`ElementState` and reuse the element resolver’s typed selector matcher, so
inactive pseudo-state rules no longer block inherited text properties. Initial
compiler style resolution uses the same authored state, while shell annotation
projects that state through the shared pseudo-state table before live
interaction updates. Bucketed and flat inherited-mask paths are covered by
state-true/state-false regressions.

**Verification.** Inherited-mask and end-to-end render regressions pass; the
pseudo-state element suite (6), shell pseudo-state suite (6), focused package
checks, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. The full frontend library
reaches 73 passing and 20 ignored tests with its unrelated existing
`render::tests::attributes::phase87_layout_display_source_semantics_survive_lowering`
failure (`Loading Ready` vs `Main grid`).

**Left open.** The next backlog item is transactional retained layout with
last-known-good geometry and retry after failure.

## 2026-08-23 — Unify typed pseudo-state contracts

`working tree` · area: UI element core style and interaction

The 18-state pseudo-state vocabulary now lives in one typed table with
canonical names, aliases, dependency bits, typed `ElementState` accessors, and
authored-attribute decoding. Style indexing and matching, shell runtime
annotation, retained fingerprints, selective invalidation, and dependency
collection consume that table. Compiler validation rejects unknown
pseudo-states with source-owned diagnostics; table-driven tests cover the full
state matrix and authored runtime projection.

**Verification.** Pseudo-state matrix tests, shell annotation/dependency tests,
compiler pseudo-state diagnostics tests, focused package checks, formatting,
and `git diff --check` pass. The full frontend library retains its unrelated
`render::tests::attributes::phase87_layout_display_source_semantics_survive_lowering`
failure (`Loading Ready` vs `Main grid`).

**Left open.** The next backlog item is compiler inheritance matching using
actual element state.

## 2026-08-23 — Preserve visible descendant accessible names

`working tree` · area: UI element core accessibility

Accessible-name normalization now follows the documented visible-text,
`label`, then `aria-label` precedence for component elements, with `alt` as a
fallback. Semantic child labels, including icon alternative text, participate
in parent-name composition. Hidden children no longer contribute stale names;
repeated normalization clears and rebuilds derived labels, so locale-driven
child text changes are reflected immediately.

**Verification.** Focused accessibility tests (4), compiler accessibility
tests (4 active), compiler component-label regression (1), AccessKit tests (3),
and shell restyle metrics tests (4) pass. `cargo fmt --all -- --check`,
`cargo check --workspace`, and `git diff --check` pass. The full
`mesh-core-elements --all-targets` run reaches 214 passing, 10 existing
style/theme/prop fixture failures, and 57 ignored tests; the accessibility
tests pass.

**Left open.** The next backlog item is the typed pseudo-state table for UI
element indexing, mutation, matching, invalidation, diagnostics, and tests.

## 2026-08-23 — Normalize the semantic accessibility snapshot

`working tree` · area: UI element core accessibility

Accessibility metadata is now normalized after component children are built and
again after shell runtime state, styles, and layout are final. The semantic
snapshot resolves roles, visible descendant names, descriptions, ARIA state
aliases, canonical focus, hidden/visible filtering, and authored relationships
including labelled/described-by references. AccessKit now consumes the
published snapshot, so hidden descendants and stale pre-child metadata cannot
leak into platform output; retained-tree fingerprints include the normalized
semantic fields.

**Verification.** Focused element accessibility tests (3), compiler
accessibility tests (4 active), compiler component-label regression (1),
AccessKit feature tests (3), shell restyle metrics tests (4), formatting,
workspace checking, and `git diff --check` pass. The workspace-wide test run
retains the existing `mesh-core-config` repository-settings failure
(`revision` is rejected) and the existing ten `mesh-core-elements` style/theme
fixture failures; the new accessibility tests pass.

**Left open.** The next backlog item is visible descendant text precedence with
hidden-child and locale behavior.

## 2026-08-23 — Propagate component source spans through diagnostics

`working tree` · area: component language diagnostics

Component block, template, props, script, style-body, import, and prop
declaration metadata now retain source byte spans. Template and parser-local
errors are rebased into the owning `.mesh` source; compiler validation errors
carry the offending node span; CLI-facing compiler errors include the byte
range; and LSP diagnostics convert the same UTF-8 byte ranges to UTF-16 LSP
positions. The LSP position-to-byte path is UTF-16 aware as well.

**Verification.** Component parser tests (including absolute AST/error-span
regressions), frontend compiler tests, LSP diagnostics tests, LSP definition
tests, `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `git diff --check`
pass. The full LSP library retains its existing `locale.current` label
expectation failure.

**Left open.** The next open backlog item is the UI element core semantic
snapshot normalization work.

## 2026-08-23 — Share Luau parser metadata across component consumers

`working tree` · area: component language Luau analysis

Component script imports, public fields, functions, service bindings, interface
proxies, element-reference aliases, and symbol spans now come from one
`full_moon` lexer/AST pass. Explicit imports are tokenized and masked while
preserving source offsets; incomplete editor buffers use the parser's fallible
AST, and template expression identifier collection uses the same parser. The
frontend compiler and LSP consume this metadata instead of maintaining
line-oriented script scans.

**Verification.** Component (79), frontend (70 active, 20 ignored), and
focused LSP parser/proxy/alias tests pass. Targeted checks for the component,
frontend, and LSP crates pass, as does formatting and `git diff --check`.
The full LSP library retains its existing `locale.current` label expectation
failure; workspace checking remains blocked by unavailable `xkbcommon` and
offline Skia dependencies.

**Left open.** Reliable source spans through component AST nodes, compiler
errors, CLI diagnostics, and LSP output remain the next component-language
item.

## 2026-08-23 — Normalize component prop definitions

`working tree` · area: component language prop definitions

`PropDef` construction now rejects duplicate fields, type-inapplicable metadata,
invalid range/step relationships, duplicate enum options, invalid units, and
malformed CSS-domain defaults. Units are normalized once, definition metadata is
validated through one shared contract check, and size/color/token values use
lightningcss value parsing instead of ad-hoc prefix checks.

**Verification.** Component (77), surface-config (28), and frontend (70 active,
20 ignored) tests pass. `cargo fmt --all`, `git diff --check`,
`cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo test --workspace --no-run` pass; workspace
checking retains the existing shell warnings.

**Left open.** Luau lexer/source metadata and full AST/compiler diagnostic span
propagation remain separate backlog items.

## 2026-08-23 — Make JSON-to-prop conversion scalar-only

`working tree` · area: component language prop conversion

JSON-to-prop conversion now returns a typed error for null, array, object, and
unrepresentable-number inputs instead of stringifying structured values into a
scalar `PropValue::String`. Settings, profile writes, contribution validation,
runtime/script assignments, CSS projection, and embedded component composition
all use the scalar-only boundary. Typed composition attributes remain typed long
enough for structured values to be rejected rather than converted to strings;
the obsolete nested-JSON conversion benchmark was removed and replaced with
scalar/structured regression coverage.

**Verification.** Component (71), surface-config (28), frontend (70 active,
20 ignored), shell prop-resolution (3), and shell structured-composition (1)
tests pass. `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and
`cargo test --workspace --no-run` pass. The full shell library reaches 649
passing, 71 existing fixture/runtime failures, and 130 ignored tests; the
scalar-prop and composition regressions pass.

**Left open.** `PropDef` constraint normalization, Luau lexer/source metadata,
and full AST/compiler diagnostic span propagation remain separate backlog items.

## 2026-08-23 — Validate component prop contracts

`working tree` · area: component language semantic validation

Added the post-parse component semantic pass that joins `<props>` declarations
with exact and embedded `prop()` style references, validates common CSS value
domains, and reports undefined, empty, and incompatible references with source
line/column context. Frontend compilation now validates local child component
attributes against exposed typed props and public script fields, rejects private
and unknown child inputs, and checks static enum, number, bool, and other typed
values. Added a tooling-only parser path so LSP completion retains syntax data
while strict compiler diagnostics remain active.

**Verification.** `nix develop -c cargo test -p mesh-core-component` (71 active,
1 ignored), `nix develop -c cargo test -p mesh-core-frontend` (70 active,
20 ignored), and `nix develop -c cargo check --workspace` pass. Formatting and
`git diff --check` pass. The LSP suite reaches 85 passing with its existing
locale-completion failure; the module suite reaches 234 passing, 3 ignored, and
7 known environment/migration failures (four content-store `PermissionDenied`
tests and three script-only fixture diagnostics).

**Left open.** Layered invalid-value fallback, scalar-only JSON prop ingress,
deeper prop constraint normalization, Luau parser/source metadata, and full AST
and compiler diagnostic span propagation remain separate backlog items.

## 2026-08-23 — Parse template braces with source spans

`working tree` · area: component language interpolation/control-flow parsing

Replaced ad-hoc brace preprocessing with a dedicated brace lexer/parser. Template
interpolations and `{#if}`/`{#for}` branches now validate against the Luau parser,
reject empty/unterminated/malformed expressions and invalid nesting, and lower
through the existing XML tree while retaining absolute spans for expression bodies,
attributes, conditions, loops, and control-flow blocks. Added focused regressions
for nested/keyed control flow, quoted braces, malformed input, and span ownership.

**Verification.** `nix develop -c cargo test -p mesh-core-component` (68 active,
1 ignored) and `nix develop -c cargo test -p mesh-core-frontend` (68 active,
20 ignored) pass; `nix develop -c cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and
`git diff --check` pass. The broad module suite still has the known script-only
fixture failures from the required-template parser migration and environment
`PermissionDenied` content-store failures.

**Left open.** Semantic prop/style/child validation and full diagnostic propagation
remain separate backlog items.

## 2026-08-23 — Preserve `.mesh` top-level block grammar

`working tree` · area: component language block parsing

Replaced lossy `HashMap` block extraction with an ordered top-level parser that
retains complete, opening-tag, content, closing-tag, and attribute byte spans
in `ComponentFile`. Unknown and stray top-level content, duplicate blocks,
unsupported block attributes/languages, malformed top-level tags, and missing
`<template>` blocks now produce diagnostics. Inline `<i18n>` catalogs remain an
explicit migration error because catalogs belong in `mesh.provides.i18n`.
Unadorned `<script>` remains Luau for existing authoring compatibility; a
provided `lang` attribute must be `luau`.

**Verification.** Component tests passed (63 active, 1 ignored), the frontend
compiler tests passed (68 active, 20 ignored), workspace checking passed, and
the elements suite reached 212 passing with 8 existing theme-color failures.
The LSP suite reached 85 passing with its existing locale-completion failure;
parser-induced fixture failures were updated for the required template block.

**Left open.** Interpolation/control-flow parsing and deeper component source
span propagation remain separate component-language backlog items.

## 2026-08-23 — Resolve recursive component imports by owner scope

`working tree` · area: component import resolution

Recursive `.mesh` imports now retain canonical local targets under the source
component that owns each alias. Separate branches may reuse an alias without
shadowing one another; same-owner target collisions carry the owner and both
targets in a compiler diagnostic. DFS tracks canonical paths on the active
import stack and reports a source-path cycle chain instead of silently stopping
at the first repeated file. The shell composition and style/runtime indexes use
the same owner-scoped records, including nested module imports and contribution
roots.

**Verification.** The focused frontend compiler suite passed (68 active, 20
ignored), including recursive same-alias/different-target, collision, and cycle
regressions. `mesh-core-shell` checked successfully; workspace all-target
checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The broad shell library
reached 670 passed, 46 existing fixture/runtime failures, and 130 ignored tests.

## 2026-08-23 — Secure module-relative component imports

`working tree` · area: component import resolution

The shared module path layer now resolves local component imports relative to
their owning `.mesh` file, normalizes and contains them under the canonical
module root, rejects absolute and symlinked paths, requires regular files, and
returns canonical identities. Frontend entrypoints, recursive compiler reads,
compiled watch paths, catalog fingerprints, and runtime watcher mtimes use the
same safe boundary. LSP local definitions now require an existing canonical
module root and resolve only existing contained component files.

The shipped cross-module `../../shared` helper was promoted to the
`@mesh/bubble-options` component module, with declared dependencies from the
two consumers.

**Verification.** Focused module path tests (2), compiler path tests (1), LSP
definition tests (4), shell symlink-fingerprint test (1), shell catalog
compilation regression (1), the full frontend library (64 active), and
`cargo check --workspace --all-targets` passed. The full module library reached
237 passed / 4 existing content-store permission failures; the full LSP library
reached 85 passed / 1 existing locale-completion failure.

## 2026-08-23 — Add the resource coverage advisor

`working tree` · area: resource coverage diagnostics

The foundational resource snapshot now exposes a read-only coverage advisor for
semantic icon vocabulary and font-script needs. It reports current-chain gaps,
candidate packs, selected-pack provenance, and explicit alternative chains. Icon
suggestions append missing packs; font suggestions can surface a first-wins
reorder, but every suggestion is marked for explicit application and the
advisor never mutates or silently reorders the user's chain.

The CLI exposes the same snapshot-backed preview through `resources coverage`,
with explicit `--font-script <module>:<role>:<script>` inputs. Font pack
explanations now carry normalized script coverage from pack and face metadata.

**Verification.** The focused coverage tests, full resource suite (23 tests),
icon suite (31 passed, 3 ignored), shell discovery resource tests (6 passed, 1
ignored), workspace check, formatting, and diff checks passed. The full LSP
library reached 84 passed and retained its existing locale-completion failure.

**Left open.** Applying a suggestion remains the normal profile/settings
prepare-and-commit operation; this advisor is intentionally preview-only.

## 2026-08-23 — Explain effective resources from one snapshot

`working tree` · area: resource diagnostics and tooling

The worker-built resource candidate now publishes one serializable effective
explanation for the runtime and tooling. It carries the committed revision,
host and module pack chains, asset metadata, and semantic icon resolutions with
owner, pack, candidate, fallback, tried-candidate, and prepared-asset
provenance. Structured missing-required and missing-optional diagnostics are
part of the same snapshot.

Runtime debug output, CLI resource views and config doctor, and LSP resource
completion now consume that shared model. LSP reports discovered resource
metadata without claiming render assets were prepared; runtime selection also
accepts active module icon packs surfaced by LSP.

**Verification.** `mesh-core-resources` passed 21 tests; `mesh-core-debug`
passed 3 unit tests and 1 integration test; LSP settings/resource discovery
passed 13 focused settings tests and 5 real-workspace tests. The full LSP
library reached 84 passed and 1 unrelated locale-completion failure. Shell/CLI
broad checking remained blocked by unavailable local Skia/xkbcommon build
inputs. Formatting and `git diff --check` passed.

**Left open.** The resource coverage advisor remains separate; text shaping
remains synchronous and outside the resource broker.

## 2026-08-23 — Prepare resource assets away from shell and render threads

`working tree` · area: resource preparation and paint-safe icon assets

Icon-pack glyph maps and fonts are now parsed, validated, fingerprinted, and
captured as immutable handles during cancellable resource preparation. A
prepared font remains usable after its source path is replaced or removed, and
the renderer no longer falls back to synchronous glyph or icon-file work when
the resource broker cannot admit a job. Semantic icon lookup now runs through
the same revision-aware bounded broker and returns a deterministic missing
placeholder until its worker result is published.

**Verification.** Resource and icon suites passed (21 and 31 tests); focused
shell resource regressions passed; the full render library passed (222 tests,
37 ignored); workspace checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
The full shell library reached 669 passed and 46 existing fixture/runtime
failures, with 130 ignored tests. No performance claim is made.
