## 2026-08-23 — Require backlog completion before Codex commits

`working tree` · area: developer automation

`codex-until-limit.sh` now runs backlog mode only. Its commit boundary requires
the selected item to be absent, exactly one unchecked backlog item to be
removed, and a `.planning/log/` record to be added. The outer loop refuses to
create a commit for partial turns; Codex is instructed to leave commit creation
to the outer loop.

**Verification.** `bash -n codex-until-limit.sh`, `git diff --check`, the
backlog dry-run, and rejection of the former feature mode passed.

## 2026-08-23 — Model deterministic multi-output membership

`working tree` · area: presentation output lifecycle

Presentation now retains every output associated with a live surface instead of
letting the latest `wl_surface::enter` replace earlier membership. Unordered or
stale `leave` and output-destruction events remove only the matching output;
the most recently entered surviving output remains the deterministic geometry
choice. Each actual membership/order revision advances the output generation
and forces a full redraw. The deterministic backend exposes the same lifecycle
for tests, including generation reset when a surface disappears.

**Verification.** `nix develop --offline -c cargo test -p
mesh-core-presentation --lib` passed 105 active tests with 11 ignored; the
focused shell surface-layout suite passed 21 tests; workspace checking,
formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The broad workspace library run
reached the existing `the_repository_settings_file_is_clean` failure, which
rejects the pre-existing `revision` key in `config/settings.json` after 43
configuration tests.

**Left open.** A live compositor multi-output/conformance matrix and connection
recreation remain separate follow-ups.

## 2026-08-23 — Model compositor scale invalidation in the deterministic backend

`working tree` · area: presentation lifecycle simulation

The deterministic presentation backend now accepts validated preferred-scale
events for live surfaces, exposes the updated scale, and marks retained paint
for a full redraw when the scale changes. Repeated values do not add another
redraw transition, and missing, closed, or connection-lost surfaces reject or
clear stale scale state. This gives shell tests the same scale/full-redraw
contract as the live Wayland backend.

**Verification.** The focused scale lifecycle regression passes; the complete
presentation library passes 103 active tests with 11 ignored, all 21 shell
surface-layout tests pass, workspace checking, formatting, and `git diff --check`
pass. The broad workspace library run reaches the existing
`the_repository_settings_file_is_clean` failure, which rejects the
pre-existing `revision` key in `config/settings.json` after 43 configuration
tests.

**Left open.** Deterministic multi-output membership, the live compositor
conformance matrix, and connection recreation remain separate follow-ups.

## 2026-08-23 — Separate SHM release backpressure from frame pacing

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation scheduling

The live Wayland presentation path now records when every SHM slot is owned by
the compositor as a distinct buffer-release gate. Dispatch refreshes that gate
after `wl_buffer.release`, so an occluded or throttled surface retains damage
and waits for an actual reusable slot instead of retrying when the frame
callback timeout expires. Shell readiness and parent/child rendering apply the
same gate. The deterministic backend now models frame-pending and
buffer-backpressured states independently.

**Verification.** The focused presentation lifecycle regression and shell
surface-layout regression pass. The complete presentation library passes 102
active tests with 11 ignored, all 21 shell surface-layout tests pass, workspace
checking passes, and formatting plus `git diff --check` pass. The broad
workspace library run reaches the existing
`the_repository_settings_file_is_clean` failure, which rejects the
pre-existing `revision` key in `config/settings.json` after 43 configuration
tests.

**Left open.** The live compositor lifecycle matrix and connection recreation
remain separate follow-ups.

## 2026-08-23 — Share resolved presentation extents across commit paths

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation geometry

Dynamic layer-shell surfaces can receive a zero configure dimension for an
output-spanning axis. Presentation now resolves that dimension against the
surface's actual output before SHM attach, viewport destination, input-region
derivation, and window-geometry staging; surface-size queries use the same
resolver. This keeps shell paint and compositor-facing logical geometry on one
extent, including padded bars and rails.

**Verification.** The focused presentation configuration suite passed 24 tests;
the complete `mesh-core-presentation` library passed 101 active tests with 11
ignored; the shell surface-layout suite passed 20 tests; workspace checking,
formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The broad workspace library run
reached the existing configuration failure in
`the_repository_settings_file_is_clean`, which rejects the pre-existing
`revision` key in `config/settings.json` after 43 configuration tests.

**Left open.** Remaining presentation work includes buffer-release
backpressure, the live compositor lifecycle matrix, and connection recreation.

## 2026-08-23 — Make surface configuration diffs role-aware

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation configuration

The typed presentation diff now compares only fields consumed by the active
role. Layer surfaces ignore toplevel-only fields, windows ignore layer-only
placement and namespace/blur fields, and live window size/title/identity,
input padding, and effective keyboard changes remain explicit. Downstream
display-style fixtures now provide the required normal font style as well.

**Verification.** The focused presentation config suite passed 23 tests; the

`working tree` · area: frontend render text shaping

The parsed `font-style` value now crosses display paint into cosmic-text
shaping and the renderer's layout cache identity. Ellipsis truncation,
selection geometry, and editable-input rendering use the same style-aware
path, while text batch signatures keep normal and italic content separate.

**Verification.** The focused cache and batch-signature regressions passed;

## 2026-08-23 — Add deterministic text-input-v3 lifecycle simulator

`working tree` · area: presentation text-input lifecycle

The deterministic presentation backend now models a one-seat text-input-v3
transaction lifecycle for live testing surfaces. Enter/leave ownership is
validated, preedit/commit/deletion payloads are buffered, and exactly one
`TextInputEdit` is published at the protocol `done` boundary. Leave cancels
pending protocol state without discarding the published surrounding-text
snapshot; surface teardown and connection loss clear both ownership and the
published snapshot.

`mesh-core-presentation` library suite passed 98 active tests with 11 ignored;

## 2026-08-23 — Align deterministic text-input state with teardown

`working tree` · area: presentation text-input lifecycle

The deterministic presentation backend now clears its retained
`TextInputState` whenever the corresponding surface or popup is closed,
dismissed, destroyed with its parent, or lost with the connection. The helper
matches surface ids so teardown cannot clear a snapshot belonging to another
live surface, keeping the testing lifecycle aligned with the real Wayland
backend.

**Verification.** `nix develop --offline -c cargo test -p
mesh-core-presentation --lib` passed 97 active tests with 11 ignored; focused
shell text-input tests passed 4 tests; the focused shell connection-loss test
passed; `nix develop --offline -c cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and

**Left open.** A live compositor lifecycle matrix and connection recreation

## 2026-08-23 — Project text-input-v3 preedit into component rendering

`working tree` · area: component text input rendering

The shell now retains compositor preedit state separately from each input's
committed value. A text-input transaction updates or clears that transient
state at the same `done` boundary as deletion and commit, while runtime tree
annotation projects non-empty preedit text at the validated UTF-8 cursor into
the rendered input value. Preedit-only transactions invalidate text rendering
without dispatching a committed-value change handler, and nullable empty
preedit clears the projection.

**Verification.** The focused shell text-input suite passed 4 tests, the
preedit annotation regression passed 1 test, `cargo check --workspace`,
formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The broad shell library run reached
668 passed tests, 46 existing fixture/runtime failures, and 130 ignored tests;
none were text-input failures.

**Left open.** The inline composition text is visible, but composition
decoration and cursor styling still need a renderer contract. Connection
recreation and a live compositor lifecycle matrix remain separate follow-up
work.

## 2026-08-23 — Wire Wayland text-input-v3 transactions

`working tree` · area: Wayland text input and component editing

The Wayland presentation backend now binds the optional
`zwp_text_input_manager_v3`, owns one text-input object per seat, tracks
surface enter/leave lifecycle, and buffers preedit, commit, and surrounding
deletion events until the protocol `done` boundary. The shell publishes a
validated UTF-8 byte-indexed surrounding-text snapshot for the focused input,
and routes each compositor transaction through the component boundary without
splitting its commit/delete change notification.

**Verification.** `cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation --lib` passed 90 tests
with 11 ignored; focused shell text-input tests passed 4 tests and the focused
Wayland routing test passed; `cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and
`git diff --check` passed. The full shell library run reached 665 passed tests,
48 existing fixture/runtime failures, and 130 ignored tests.

**Left open.** The typed transaction carries preedit text and cursor metadata,
but the component renderer does not yet draw that composition decoration.
Connection recreation and a live compositor protocol matrix remain separate
follow-up work.

## 2026-08-23 — Add UTF-8-safe surrounding deletion

`working tree` · area: component text editing contract

The typed input path now carries surrounding deletion as UTF-8 byte lengths,
matching the text-input-v3 protocol. Focused components retain a byte cursor,
clamp deletion requests to valid Unicode scalar boundaries, support keyboard
Delete alongside Backspace, and dispatch input/change handlers once per
effective edit. The presentation and shell seams route the deletion payload so
the future Wayland protocol adapter can deliver it without another ABI change.

**Verification.** Focused deletion, keyboard Delete, and Wayland routing tests
passed; the presentation library passed (88 active, 11 ignored); workspace
checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The full shell library run
passed 664 tests but retains 47 existing integration failures and 130 ignored
tests unrelated to this increment.

**Left open.** The actual `zwp_text_input_v3` manager/object lifecycle,

shaping also remain open.

`working tree` · area: Wayland keyboard and component text input

Wayland keyboard UTF-8 commits now travel as one typed text payload instead of

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation popup lifecycle

A failed child `configure_popup`, including a compositor-rejected reposition,

backend has a deterministic one-shot popup-configuration failure hook for
this lifecycle.

**Verification.** The targeted rejected-reposition regression passed; the

When a shell parent is successfully reconfigured, its compositor role may be

## 2026-08-23 — Retry missing popup compositor objects

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation popup lifecycle

Popup targets now clear their cached `last_popup_size` when a visible present
or state-only commit reports `SurfaceMissing`. State-only misses also request a
full present. Child reconciliation reissues `configure_popup` when that
creation gate is cleared even if the requested placement is unchanged, and
retained child paint-generation state cannot suppress the pending retry.

**Verification.** The targeted recreation regression passed, the complete child
surface test module passed (18 tests), the presentation library passed (86
active, 11 ignored), and workspace checking passed. Formatting and
`git diff --check` passed. The broad workspace library run
continues to stop at the existing repository-settings fixture, which rejects
the `revision` key in `config/settings.json`.

**Left open.** Safe popup recreation for rejected reposition or replaced
parents, the remaining semantic presentation state diff, full pointer-button
identity, IME/text-input-v3, connection recreation, and resource preparation/
text shaping remain follow-up work.

## 2026-08-23 — Preserve click-grab seat and serial identity

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation popup input identity

Wayland left-button presses now publish a dedicated identity-bearing input
event containing the originating `wl_seat` protocol id and press serial. Shell
routing preserves that identity while delivering the normal component pointer
input, records it only for the current dispatch generation, and consumes it
once when an inline click-grab popover is first configured. The accepted popup
configuration intentionally clears the credential, so later size and placement
updates cannot replay it. The backend now resolves and grabs the exact
originating seat; a missing, stale, zero, or unknown identity produces a
visible warning and the documented no-grab downgrade.

**Verification.** `nix develop --offline -c cargo test
-p mesh-core-presentation --lib` passed (86 active, 11 ignored), the focused
shell Wayland tests passed (4 active, 1 ignored), all 17 child-surface tests
passed, and `nix develop --offline -c cargo check --workspace` passed.
Formatting and `git diff --check` passed. The focused popover group passed 13
of 14 tests; its existing legacy dismissed-popover visibility assertion still
fails. The broad workspace library run stops at the existing
`the_repository_settings_file_is_clean` failure, which rejects the `revision`
key in `config/settings.json` after 43 configuration tests passed.

**Left open.** Safe popup recreation for unsupported reposition or replaced
parents, the remaining semantic presentation state diff, full pointer-button
identity, IME/text-input-v3, connection recreation, and resource preparation/
text shaping remain follow-up work.

## 2026-08-23 — Make v3 popup positioners reactive

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation popup placement

New popups now request an xdg-positioner reactive relationship when the
connection negotiated xdg-shell v3, allowing compositor parent/output geometry
changes to deliver reactive popup configures without an explicit client
reposition. Explicit reposition requests continue to use non-reactive
positioners and nonzero correlation tokens. Popup entries retain the latest
compositor-resolved position for initial, reactive, and reposition configures;
the capability snapshot exposes the v3 reactive-positioner gate separately.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation
--lib` passed (86 active, 11 ignored), `nix develop --command cargo check
--workspace` passed. Final formatting and `git diff --check` are the handoff
hygiene gates. The shell library run compiled but reported 656 passed, 47 failed, and
130 ignored across existing service/theme/module fixture expectations. The
broad workspace library run reached the pre-existing
`the_repository_settings_file_is_clean` failure, which rejects the `revision`
key in `config/settings.json` after 43 configuration tests passed.

**Left open.** Safe popup recreation for unsupported reposition or replaced
parents, click-grab seat/serial identity, the remaining semantic presentation
state diff, pointer-button identity, IME/text-input-v3, connection recreation,

## 2026-08-23 — Gate popup reposition and validate popup identity

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation popup lifecycle

Existing popup updates now require the live surface to already be an xdg popup
with the same parent id and parent object generation. Non-popup id collisions,
reparenting, missing or nested popup parents, and replaced parent objects return
typed surface-creation diagnostics instead of mutating the wrong compositor
role. Placement changes call `xdg_popup.reposition` only when the negotiated
xdg-shell version is at least 3; older versions keep initial popup creation but
return a typed unsupported-protocol error for reposition requests. Reposition
requests use nonzero monotonic tokens, and popup configure handling correlates
returned reposition tokens while retaining mismatches for diagnostics.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation
--lib` passed (86 active, 11 ignored), `nix develop --command cargo check
--workspace`, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The broad
`nix develop --command cargo test --workspace --lib` run reproduced the existing
configuration-suite failure at `the_repository_settings_file_is_clean`, which
rejects the pre-existing `revision` key in `config/settings.json` after 43
configuration tests passed. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Safe popup recreation for unsupported reposition or replaced
parents, the remaining semantic presentation state diff, pointer-button
identity, IME/text-input-v3, connection recreation, and resource preparation/
text shaping remain follow-up work.

## 2026-08-23 — Publish negotiated Wayland capability versions

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation capability negotiation

The Wayland presentation backend now records a typed, connection-local
capability snapshot for layer-shell, xdg-shell, viewporter, fractional scale,
KDE blur, activation, Hyprland focus grabs, and pointer gestures. Advertised
versions are clamped to the protocol surface supported by this build, absent
globals report version zero, and the snapshot has an explicit generation. The
public presentation engine exposes the snapshot for diagnostics and planning;
`xdg_popup.reposition` has a direct version-3 gate without changing popup
behavior yet.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation
--lib` passed (85 active, 11 ignored), `nix develop --command cargo check
--workspace`, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The broad
`nix develop --command cargo test --workspace --lib` run reproduced the
existing configuration-suite failure at `the_repository_settings_file_is_clean`,
which rejects the pre-existing `revision` key in `config/settings.json` after
43 configuration tests passed. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Consume negotiated versions for popup identity/reposition
safety, complete the remaining semantic presentation state diff, pointer-button
identity, IME/text-input-v3, connection recreation, and resource preparation/
text shaping.

## 2026-08-23 — Track output membership generations in presentation

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation output lifecycle

Live surface generation snapshots now carry a monotonic output revision.
`wl_surface::enter` and `wl_surface::leave` update the most-recent output
association, while `wl_output` geometry updates advance the revision for every
surface currently using that output. Output destruction clears stale
associations. Each affected surface is marked for a full redraw, and leave
events retain the current-output identity guard so an older queued leave cannot
erase a newer membership.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation
--lib` passed (84 active, 11 ignored), `nix develop --command cargo check
--workspace`, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The broad
`nix develop --command cargo test --workspace --lib` run reproduced the existing
configuration-suite failure at `the_repository_settings_file_is_clean`, which
rejects the pre-existing `revision` key in `config/settings.json` after 43
configuration tests passed. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Negotiated-capability generations and the remaining semantic
presentation state diff, plus pointer-button identity, IME/text-input-v3,
connection recreation, and resource preparation/text shaping.

## 2026-08-23 — Track SHM buffer generations in presentation snapshots

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation buffer lifecycle

Each SHM slot now receives a monotonic generation within its live compositor
object. The slot generation is carried by frame callback metadata, and the
surface generation snapshot publishes the slot whose contents were most
recently committed. Pool-size changes therefore cannot make a newly allocated
slot look like an older resource, while ordinary slot reuse remains visible as
the same buffer identity. Invalid frame references fail with a typed buffer
attach error before a callback is registered.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation
--lib` passed (83 active, 11 ignored), `nix develop --command cargo check
--workspace`, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The broad
`nix develop --command cargo test --workspace --lib` run reached the existing
configuration suite and failed only at `the_repository_settings_file_is_clean`,
which rejects the pre-existing `revision` key in `config/settings.json` after
43 configuration tests passed. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Output membership and negotiated-capability generations,
pointer-button identity, IME/text-input-v3, connection recreation, and resource
preparation/text shaping remain follow-up work.

## 2026-08-23 — Gate presentation callbacks by object and frame generations

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation lifecycle

Live presentation entries now retain monotonic object, configure, and frame
generations. Surface-role creation reserves a unique object generation across
replacements, accepted layer/window/popup configures advance the current
configure generation, and each SHM frame callback carries the surface id plus
the object and frame generations that created it. A callback is accepted only
when both generations still match the live entry, so a delayed callback from a
timed-out frame or a destroyed/replaced role cannot clear a newer frame gate.
The generation snapshot is exposed through the presentation engine for
diagnostic correlation, and the focused generation tests cover both stale
frame and stale-object callbacks.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test
-p mesh-core-presentation --lib` passed (81 active, 11 ignored), including the
new callback-generation regressions. `nix develop --command cargo check
--workspace`, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. No performance claim is
made.

**Left open.** Buffer/output/capability generations, popup identity/version
gating, pointer-button identity, IME/text-input-v3, connection recreation,
and resource preparation/text shaping remain follow-up work.

## 2026-08-23 — Commit presentation state without pixel damage

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation state commits

Opaque regions, blur regions, derived input regions, and xdg window geometry
now share a pending state preparation path. A visible render with no pixel
damage can issue a typed state-only commit, while SHM backpressure, copy, or
attach failure leaves staged state available for the next successful commit.
The testing backend models committed state and unconfigured retries. Shell
region caches are invalidated after successful surface configuration and
remapping so a recreated compositor object cannot inherit a warm cache.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test
-p mesh-core-presentation --lib` passed (79 active, 11 ignored), including the
state-only commit regression. Shell `surface_layout` passed 20 tests and
`child_surface` passed 20 tests with 1 ignored. `nix develop --command cargo
check --workspace`, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. The broad
`cargo test --workspace --lib` run still reaches the existing config suite and
fails only at `the_repository_settings_file_is_clean`, which rejects the
pre-existing `revision` key in `config/settings.json` after 43 config tests
passed. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Object/configure/frame generations, popup identity/version
gating, pointer-button identity, IME/text-input-v3, connection recreation, and
resource preparation/text shaping remain follow-up work.

## 2026-08-23 — Replace presentation fingerprints with typed config diffs

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation configuration

Wayland surface configuration now classifies changes as unchanged, live,
fresh-configure, or recreate work. The partial hash gate was removed, so the
comparison covers the full typed `SurfaceConfig` and effective keyboard mode.
Layer namespace/blur changes and xdg decoration negotiation now prepare a new
role object before destroying the old one; layer geometry still waits for a
fresh configure, while title, size-hint, keyboard, and padding changes stay on
the live path. Regression tests cover every change class and the previously
untracked creation-time fields.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test
-p mesh-core-presentation --lib` passed (78 active, 11 ignored), including the
new typed-diff cases. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Object/configure/frame generations, region-only semantic
commits, popup identity/version gating, pointer-button identity,
IME/text-input-v3, connection recreation, and resource preparation/text
shaping remain follow-up work.

## 2026-08-23 — Route presentation input ownership per seat

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation input lifecycle

Wayland presentation now stores protocol handles and mutable input ownership in
per-seat records. Pointer, keyboard, touch, gesture, and focus-grab callbacks
resolve their originating seat through protocol-object indexes; keyboard repeat
state and cursor updates operate independently for every seat. Internally
queued events retain their seat owner until the backend boundary, so capability
or surface teardown cannot discard or cancel another seat's event even when
both seats target the same surface. A focused regression covers that same-
surface cross-seat queue case.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation
--lib` passed (77 active, 12 ignored); the focused shell `surface_layout` slice
passed 20 tests and `child_surface` passed 20 tests with 1 ignored. Workspace
checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The broad
`cargo test --workspace --lib` run reached the existing config suite and failed
only at `the_repository_settings_file_is_clean`, which rejects the pre-existing
`revision` key in `config/settings.json` after 43 config tests passed. No
performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Pointer-button identity, IME/text-input-v3, connection
recreation, object/configure/frame generations, and the remaining semantic
presentation diff remain follow-up work.

## 2026-08-23 — Prevent normalized Escape from repeating

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation keyboard input

The keyboard normalizer emits the canonical `Esc` event name, while repeat
suppression previously recognized only `escape`. The non-repeating classifier
now accepts both spellings, so compositor Escape presses cannot schedule
repeat events regardless of whether the input arrives before or after name
normalization. Regression coverage verifies normalization and repeat setup.

**Verification.** The focused repeat and key-normalization tests passed;
`nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation --lib` passed (76
active, 12 ignored); `nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` and
formatting passed. The broad `cargo test --workspace --lib` run reached the
existing config suite and failed only at the repository-settings fixture,
which rejects the `revision` key in `config/settings.json` after 43 tests
passed. `git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Input ownership is still represented by single-seat globals;
pointer-button identity, IME/text-input-v3, and full per-seat routing remain
follow-up work.

## 2026-08-23 — Cancel input when seat capabilities disappear

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation seat lifecycle

Seat capability removal now cancels the state owned by the disappearing input
object before releasing it: pointer and gesture ownership emit synthetic leave
and cancelled-end events, keyboard focus and repeat state are cleared, and
active touch ids emit one deterministic cancellation per surface. Queued events
from the removed capability are discarded without disturbing the other input
classes. Full seat removal applies all three cancellations and releases the
focus-grab object as well.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test
-p mesh-core-presentation --lib` passed (76 active, 12 ignored); focused shell
`surface_layout` passed (20) and `child_surface` passed (20, 1 ignored);
`nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` passed with the existing shell
warnings. The broad `cargo test --workspace --lib` run reached the existing
config suite and failed only at the repository-settings fixture, which rejects
the `revision` key in `config/settings.json` after 43 tests passed. Formatting
and `git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Input ownership is still represented by single-seat globals, so
multiple seats cannot be routed independently yet. Object/configure/frame
generations, connection recreation, and text shaping/resource-broker
separation remain follow-up work.

## 2026-08-23 — Cancel single-seat input during surface teardown

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation input lifecycle

The shared surface teardown boundary now cancels input ownership before the
surface identity is removed. Pointer focus emits a synthetic `PointerLeave`;
keyboard focus and repeat state are cleared; active touch ids emit one
`TouchCancel`; and an active swipe, pinch, or hold emits its corresponding
cancelled end event. Queued events for the removed surface are discarded while
unrelated surface ownership and events remain intact. The guard preserves
idempotent teardown when the identity was already removed.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test
-p mesh-core-presentation --lib` passed (73 active, 12 ignored); the focused
shell `surface_layout` group passed (20) and `child_surface` group passed (20,
1 ignored); `nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` passed with the
existing shell warnings; formatting and `git diff --check` are run for this
working tree. The broad `cargo test --workspace --lib` run reached the
existing config suite and failed only at the repository-settings fixture,
which rejects the `revision` key in `config/settings.json` after 43 tests
passed.

**Left open.** Input ownership is still single-seat: seat/capability removal
needs equivalent cancellation and a per-seat state model. Connection
recreation supervision, object/configure/frame generations, and text
shaping/resource-broker separation remain follow-up work.

## 2026-08-23 — Surface connection loss becomes a typed lifecycle

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation lifecycle

Wayland dispatch, polling, and frame-flush paths now distinguish connection
I/O/EOF failures from ordinary presentation errors and return
`PresentationError::ConnectionLost`. The first loss idempotently drops all
retained surface identities and protocol-owned input handles, clears focus,
repeat, touch, and gesture ownership, preserves local cancellation events, and
publishes deterministic per-surface `SurfaceLifecycleEvent::Lost` records. The
shell consumes those records before returning the typed failure, so accepted
surface configuration is invalidated while the component remains mounted. The
testing backend can inject the same one-shot loss and verifies stable error and
event behavior.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation
--lib` passed (71 active, 12 ignored); the focused shell `surface_layout` group
passed (20); the connection-loss presentation and shell regressions passed;
`nix develop --command cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and
`git diff --check` passed. The broad `cargo test --workspace --lib` run remains
blocked by the pre-existing repository-settings fixture, which rejects the
`revision` key in `config/settings.json` after 43 config tests passed. Existing
shell dead-code/private-interface warnings remain. No performance claim is
made.

**Left open.** Ordinary surface/seat teardown still uses single-seat input
state and needs per-seat cancellation; the presentation backend still has no
connection-recreation supervisor. Object/configure/frame generations and text
shaping/resource-broker separation remain follow-up work.

## 2026-08-23 — Make Wayland presentation failures retryable

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation transaction boundary

BGRA copy helpers now validate checked source and SHM canvas lengths and return
typed copy failures instead of silently skipping rows. SHM slot damage is
restored when copying or buffer activation/attach fails, and attach failures are
returned as `PresentationError::BufferAttach` before the frame is marked
pending. A visible present for a missing compositor surface now returns the
distinct `SurfaceMissing` outcome; the shell retains its damage, invalidates the
accepted configuration cache, and retries role creation rather than consuming
pixels that were never shown.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation
--lib` passed (68 active, 12 ignored), `nix develop --command cargo check
--workspace` passed with the existing 25 shell warnings, and formatting passed.
The focused shell surface-layout group passed (17 tests). The broad workspace
lib run reached the existing config suite but failed its repository-settings
fixture because the checked-in `revision` key is reported as unknown. No
performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Failed configure/replacement and compositor-loss paths still need
typed lifecycle outcomes and one idempotent teardown supervisor; text shaping
remains synchronous and outside the shared render resource broker.

## 2026-08-23 — Bound Wayland SHM pool storage

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation SHM resources

SHM size-class configuration now uses checked rounding and validates maximum
dimensions, protocol-safe stride, and decoded byte length before calling
`SlotPool`. Each surface tracks the bytes retained by its compositor-owned
buffer slots, with 64 MiB per-buffer and 192 MiB per-surface limits. Shared
`SlotPool` growth is conservatively capped at 512 MiB, including its doubling
allocation strategy, so repeated surfaces cannot grow the mapped pool without
bound. Invalid and oversized configurations return typed presentation errors;
ordinary compositor backpressure still returns `NotReady`.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation --lib`
passed (66 passed, 12 ignored), `nix develop --command cargo check --workspace`
passed with the existing shell warnings, and formatting plus `git diff --check`
passed. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Present/attach failures still need transactional pending-damage
restoration and explicit frame acknowledgement; text shaping remains
synchronous and outside the shared render resource broker.

## 2026-08-23 — Make PixelBuffer storage and allocation bounded

`working tree` · area: frontend render and surface presentation boundary

`PixelBuffer` backing storage and dimensions are now private. Public callers
receive read-only dimensions and immutable byte slices, while render-internal
code receives only non-resizing mutable slices. `try_new` checks stride and
byte-length arithmetic, enforces a 512 MiB per-buffer limit, and uses fallible
reservation before initialization; dynamic shell surface and child-surface
allocation now reports rejected requests through the existing shell diagnostic.
The `PixelCanvasSession` contract is therefore storage-stable for its released
Skia surface, with regression coverage for allocation rejection and a live
canvas/raw-buffer sequence.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-render --lib`
passed (216 passed, 37 ignored), `nix develop --command cargo check --workspace`
passed with the existing shell warnings, and formatting plus `git diff --check`
passed. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Wayland SHM pool buffers still need the corresponding shared
byte/dimension budget and lifecycle accounting; text shaping remains
synchronous and outside the shared render resource broker.

## 2026-08-23 — Bound shaped text layout storage

`working tree` · area: frontend render text layout

The shaped-text layout cache now uses a byte-aware LRU with a 16 MiB resident
budget in addition to its 512-entry cap. Each cached `cosmic-text::Buffer`
gets a conservative storage charge from its owned strings, line capacity,
line/attribute overhead, layout runs, and shaped glyphs; inputs or estimates
that exceed safe bounds are left renderable but are not retained. Text cache
metrics expose current and maximum resident bytes, and byte-driven evictions
are counted as layout invalidations.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-render --lib`
passed (214 passed, 37 ignored), `nix develop --command cargo check --workspace`
passed with the existing shell warnings, and formatting plus `git diff --check`
passed. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Text shaping remains synchronous and outside the shared render
resource broker; `PixelBuffer` and SHM allocations remain for later work.

## 2026-08-23 — Bound the text glyph atlas by revision and bytes

`working tree` · area: frontend render text resources

The text renderer's Skia glyph atlas now keys entries by both the
`cosmic-text` glyph key and the active resource revision, so a replaced font
catalog cannot reuse an image produced by an older font system. Atlas entries
are admitted through a byte-aware LRU with an 8 MiB limit, and raster output is
rejected before Skia upload when dimensions, row size, or total storage would
overflow or exceed the configured limit. This keeps the existing synchronous
text paint path while making its cached derived images bounded and
generation-aware.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-render --lib`
passed (212 passed, 37 ignored), `mesh-core-elements --lib lru` passed (7),
`nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` passed, and formatting plus
`git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Text layout `Buffer` storage, `PixelBuffer`, and SHM resources
still need the shared generation-aware accounting boundary.

## 2026-08-23 — Bound derived render-resource bytes

`working tree` · area: frontend render resource budgeting

Added a byte-aware LRU primitive and applied it to decoded icon images,
raster variants, metadata lookups, icon-font bytes, glyph masks, and the Skia
icon uploads. Icon and glyph workers now reserve from one shared 32 MiB
handoff budget, so their count-bounded queues cannot multiply large pending
allocations. Checked pixel-size helpers reject oversized or overflowing icon
and glyph requests, and source bitmap dimensions are bounded before raster
allocation; oversized cache entries are
rejected without exceeding their cache budget.

**Verification.** `nix develop -c cargo test -p mesh-core-elements --lib
lru::tests` passed (7), `mesh-core-resources --lib` passed (21), and
`mesh-core-render --lib` passed (208 passed, 37 ignored). Workspace checking,
formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The full elements suite still has
the existing 8 theme-fixture expectation failures.

**Left open.** The shared budget is the first broker boundary; queue ownership
and linked-resource invalidation still need to move into the full generation-
aware resource broker. Text layout, text glyph atlas, PixelBuffer, and SHM
budgets remain separate work.

## 2026-08-22 — Add mode-aware Codex loop commits

`working tree` · area: development automation

`codex-until-limit.sh` now supports `feature`, `backlog`, and `refactor` modes.
Each successful turn is a commit boundary owned by the loop, while operational
logs and handoff files stay outside feature commits. Backlog mode requires the
selected unchecked item to be removed before committing. Refactor mode audits
the existing package sections, creates `docs/REFACTORING-RULES.md`, and then
asks for one scoped rules-guided increment per turn. Development guidance now
documents the modes and clean-worktree behavior.

**Verification.** `bash -n`, `git diff --check`, help/dry-run paths, and a
temporary fake-Codex integration passed for automatic feature commits,
refactor-rule commits, and rejection of incomplete backlog items.

## 2026-08-22 — Validate icon packs before atomic publication

`working tree` · area: resource preparation and icon registry publication

Selected icon-pack resources are now prepared on the resource worker. Bundled
fonts and glyph maps use contained bounded reads; fonts are parsed before
publication, glyph maps are strictly validated with entry limits, and mapping
targets must resolve declared font aliases and glyph names. Parsed glyph maps
are retained in immutable bindings so prepared resolution does not read module
files on the render path. Invalid resources reject the complete candidate, and
the obsolete incremental shell registration helpers were removed; the live
registry is updated only by its existing atomic replacement boundary.

**Verification.** `nix develop -c cargo test -p mesh-core-icon --lib` passed
(26 passed, 3 ignored); the resource-specific shell regression passed; focused
formatting and `cargo check -p mesh-core-icon -p mesh-core-shell` passed. The
broader shell icon slice reports 3 passed and 3 existing navigation integration
failures (audio click bubbling, brightness wheel dispatch, and layout bounds).

**Next.** Propagate resource revisions and fingerprints through negative lookup,
glyph, font, text-layout, and renderer caches.

## 2026-08-22 — Restore theme-token-backed animation playback

`working tree` · area: animation token resolution and tooltip playback

Theme token resolution now preserves canonical dotted names with meaningful
hyphens, so easing tokens such as
`animation.curves.bezier.emphasized-decelerate` resolve instead of dropping
transition declarations. Theme tooltip animation shorthands also add
`ms` when a numeric duration token is substituted into the time position.
Regression coverage covers the theme resolver, style transition lowering, and
shell animation/tooltip playback.

**Verification.** Focused theme, style, shell animation, and tooltip tests pass;
the shell animation/tooltip group reports 32 passed and 1 ignored. The broader
elements and shell suites still contain unrelated failures from the existing
dirty worktree (theme-fixture color expectations and service/module contract
tests).

## 2026-08-22 — Add typed icon mappings and resolution provenance

`working tree` · area: icon-pack contract and semantic resolution

Canonical icon-pack mappings now accept string shorthand or typed objects with
an explicit `target` and `multicolor` source-color policy. Pack source kind,
advisory coverage, and namespaced vocabulary metadata are retained in the
manifest model. Runtime bindings carry the typed policy through file and glyph
resolution, and every successful lookup records its owning module, pack,
candidate, and fallback stage for shared diagnostics and future explanations.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-icon` passed
(22 tests, 3 ignored); the combined focused resource/icon/diagnostics/locale/
config run passed (4, 22, 6, 17, and 44 tests respectively), and formatting
plus `git diff --check` passed. Shell checking remains stopped by the four
pre-existing `mesh-core-module` errors without a new mapping error.

**Next.** Validate complete packs off the render thread and publish them
atomically without partial registry state.

## 2026-08-22 — Implement canonical icon fallback and optional diagnostics

`working tree` · area: icon lookup and shell diagnostics

Semantic resolution now tries the canonical legacy vocabulary and retries the
whole ordered chain for dash-generalized names before hicolor and the built-in
missing icon. Required misses remain activation errors; optional misses are
resolved against the same effective registry and recorded as deduplicated
structured warnings.

**Verification.** The focused icon/resource/diagnostics suite passed (22 icon,
4 resource, and 6 diagnostics tests, with 3 icon tests ignored).

**Next.** Validate complete packs off the render thread and publish them
atomically without partial registry state.

## 2026-08-22 — Contain module resource asset reads

`working tree` · area: resource filesystem safety

Module contribution paths and bundled icon-pack font/glyph assets now use
descriptor-relative `O_NOFOLLOW` traversal, reject absolute and parent paths,
require regular files, and enforce bounded reads before candidate preparation
can retain a handle. Symlink, traversal, and oversized-input regressions cover
the resource handle contract.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-resources`
passed (4 tests), and the combined focused run also passed.

**Next.** Validate complete packs off the render thread and publish them
atomically without partial registry state.

## 2026-08-22 — Make resource pack order and ownership deterministic

`working tree` · area: icon registry identity and alias resolution

Prepared icon binding replacement now accepts only canonical lower-case pack
ids, rejects duplicate pack ids and module owners before mutating the live
registry, and keeps the prior candidate on validation failure. Mapping targets
resolve font aliases only within their owning icon pack rather than searching
all packs through unordered map iteration. Candidate preparation rejects
duplicate aliases within one pack. Profile graph chains retain declared order
and validate duplicate, missing, inactive, and wrong-kind entries for icon,
font, and language resources.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-icon` passed
(20 tests, 3 ignored); `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check`
passed. Shell checking remains stopped by the existing module-package errors
without reporting a new registry error.

**Next.** Open module resource assets through contained, no-follow, bounded
handles.

## 2026-08-22 — Prepare graph/profile resource snapshots atomically

`working tree` · area: host resources, icon packs, and profile activation

Composition resource selections now carry ordered icon-pack and font-pack
chains into `InstalledModuleGraph`; no-profile graphs derive deterministic
enabled-pack defaults, while profile chains reject duplicates, missing modules,
wrong kinds, and inactive entries. The shell prepares selected pack bindings
and contained icon/font contribution paths from that graph on a worker, then
atomically replaces graph-authorized icon bindings and retains a revisioned
resource snapshot. Discovery no longer publishes icon-pack or frontend
bindings for every module found on disk. Profile candidates carry the prepared
resource snapshot until activation commit, so failed preparation retains the
previous live registry.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-icon` passed
(19 tests, 3 ignored); `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check`
passed. Shell checking again reached the pre-existing `mesh-core-module`
errors (`ThemeModeMetadata`, missing `ShellProfile.revision`, and two inference
failures) without reporting a resource error.

**Next.** Preserve declared pack order and reject duplicate canonical IDs,
aliases, and ambiguous ownership deterministically.

## 2026-08-22 — Add manual, follow-system, and profile locale policies

`working tree` · area: localization policy and settings transactions

Locale settings now carry an explicit `manual` or `follow_system` policy.
Follow-system resolution normalizes POSIX environment values into BCP 47 tags
for runtime consumers while leaving the sparse durable settings unchanged.
Startup, graph settings registration, profile activation, settings reload, and
manual locale writes all resolve the same runtime settings boundary. The shell
locale contract and service snapshot publish the policy; CLI and LSP expose it,
and `locale set-system` shares the catalog preparation and revision-checked
commit path with manual selection. Profile writes remain scoped to the active
profile.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-config
-p mesh-core-locale` passed (44 and 17 tests); `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and
`git diff --check` passed. Shell/CLI/LSP checking reached the existing
`mesh-core-module` blockers (`ThemeModeMetadata`, missing `ShellProfile.revision`,
and two inference failures) without reporting a new policy error.

**Next.** Build icon and font resources only from prepared graph/profile
snapshots and atomically retain the last-known-good resource snapshot.

## 2026-08-22 — Drive locale tooling from the canonical graph

`working tree` · area: localization authoring and tooling

`InstalledModuleGraph` now owns the ordered, profile-aware locale catalog
read model used by the shell, CLI, and LSP. The CLI gained graph-backed
`locale list`, `active`, `set`, `which`, `missing`, `extract`, and `doctor`
commands; extraction and missing-key checks use parser-derived static keys,
while doctor also reports catalog entry and component parse failures. LSP
locale completion now reads graph contributions and module defaults instead of
scanning `config/i18n` directories. Inline `<i18n>` blocks are rejected with a
source-line migration diagnostic directing authors to `mesh.provides.i18n`.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-locale` passed
(16 tests); `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-component` passed
(59 passed, 1 ignored); `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check`
passed. CLI/LSP checking reached the known module-package blockers
(`ThemeModeMetadata`, missing `ShellProfile.revision`, and two inference
failures) without reporting a new error in this increment.

**Left open.** Manual, follow-system, and per-profile locale policy refinement.

## 2026-08-22 — Contain graph catalogs with bounded no-follow handles

`working tree` · area: localization catalog I/O

Graph-authorized catalog sources now retain their owning module root and a
validated relative path instead of relying on a free-form joined pathname.
Catalog reads traverse the root and relative components with `O_NOFOLLOW`,
reject traversal/absolute components and non-regular files, cap input at 2 MiB,
and validate UTF-8 before JSON parsing. The immutable snapshot preparation path
uses this handle, while the previous last-known-good commit behavior remains
unchanged.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-locale` passed
(15 tests), including escape, symlink, regular-file, UTF-8, and size-bound
regressions. Shell checking reached the existing module-package errors
(`ThemeModeMetadata`, `ShellProfile.revision`, and two inference failures) and
reported no new locale errors before that dependency stopped compilation.

**Left open.** Move catalog reading/parsing/compilation behind a non-shell
worker boundary before removing this backlog item.

## 2026-08-22 — Centralize runtime localized misses and metadata identity

`working tree` · area: localization consumption and diagnostics

Luau `mesh.i18n.t()` and template `t(...)` now resolve through one structured
missing-result path. Missing values remain visibly `!!key`, while the queued
record retains the owning module, key, fallback, field path, catalog source,
and snapshot revision for shell diagnostics. Manifest metadata uses the same
field-aware resolution record and shell diagnostics deduplicate active misses
by owner and key instead of emitting one generic runtime warning per render.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-locale` passed
(13 tests); the focused scripting miss regression passed; scripting library

still has one unrelated pre-existing service-ticket assertion failure, and

compilation.

## 2026-08-22 — Align locale scripting ABI and capability gates

`working tree` · area: localization runtime and authoring ABI

The canonical scripting surface is now consistent across the runtime, LSP,
and specification: module-scoped translation is `import("mesh.i18n", "t")`,
while locale state exposes `mesh.locale.current()` and the write request

## 2026-08-21 — Make optimistic service writes transactional

`working tree` · area: service contracts and shell state routing

Contract-bound optimistic state now records the correlated `CallId`, provider
generation, previous visible value, and desired value. Terminal failures,
timeouts, cancellation, throttled supersession, provider unavailability, and
provider replacement roll back only the transaction that still owns the field;
newer writes take ownership without allowing older completions to overwrite
them. Historical ownership chains unwind correctly when multiple writes fail,
and successful writes remain shell-visible until a matching provider snapshot
confirms them.

completed, failed, superseded, timed-out, or cancelled terminal outcome. The
shell updates the matching debug transaction instead of appending an
uncorrelated result, and queued calls can be cancelled or expire before their
Luau handler starts.

Verification: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check` pass. The
focused backend and shell tests could not start because the local registry
cache lacks `sha2` and network access is unavailable.

**Left open.** The Luau-facing ticket/await API and cancellation propagation
from a frontend caller remain part of the open service transaction backlog.

## 2026-08-21 — Isolate service snapshots from shared Luau globals

`working tree` · area: service contracts and scripting isolation

Frontend service payloads now use a per-context, Rust-owned store with
generation-stamped snapshots and capability filtering. Interface proxies read
copied fields from that store, reject state writes, and no longer expose
`__mesh_svc_*` payload or fingerprint tables through shared Lua globals. Shell
payload delivery now skips contexts without the matching read capability.

Verification: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check` pass. The
focused scripting regression could not start because the local registry cache
lacks `sha2` and network access is unavailable; `cargo test --offline` reports
the same missing dependency.

## 2026-08-21 — Verify signed module provenance before activation

`working tree` · area: module provenance and candidate planning

Detached `module.sig` records now sign one canonical module identity/version/
digest payload. Root graphs may provide PEM Ed25519 trust anchors under
`trustPolicy.keys`; install, staged update planning, lock verification, and
resolved graph activation all use the same verifier. Signature files are
excluded from module content digests, verified candidates receive the
`verified` tier, and unknown keys, malformed signatures, changed payloads, and
unsupported algorithms fail closed with `signature_invalid` diagnostics.

Verification: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check` pass. The
focused `cargo test -p mesh-core-module trust --locked` could not start because
the local registry cache lacks `sha2` and network access is unavailable.

## 2026-08-21 — Add trust-tier policy plumbing

`working tree` · area: module provenance and activation policy

Locks now retain a typed provenance tier and optional detached-signature
metadata. Root graphs may set `trustPolicy.minimum`; graph construction blocks
lower-tier modules before dependency/provider/frontend activation, while CLI
and shell installs refuse candidates below that policy and staged update review
surfaces trust-policy diagnostics even when revisions are unchanged. Existing
workspaces remain permissive by default. Cryptographic signature verification
and registry key distribution remain open follow-up work.

Verification: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check` pass. The
focused trust-policy regression could not start because `sha2` is absent from
the local registry cache; offline execution reports the same missing
dependency.

## 2026-08-21 — Isolate frontend source indexing failures

`working tree` · area: frontend component catalog

Frontend catalog construction now stays separate from the resolved module
graph. Primary `.mesh` roots and extension-point contributions compile as
independent incremental entries, unchanged entries are reused by manifest and
watched-source fingerprints, and a failed module or contribution is retained
as a scoped catalog diagnostic instead of aborting unrelated entries. Each
declared contribution source remains in the host watch set so an edit can
retry a previously invalid source without changing graph activation state.

Verification: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check` pass. The
focused catalog regression could not start because `sha2` is absent from the
local registry cache; offline execution reports the same missing dependency.

## 2026-08-21 — Explain candidate graph effects during dry runs

`working tree` · area: package planning and CLI review

Resolved module graphs now expose a deterministic diff for additions, removals,
updates, enable/disable transitions, provider changes, and effective profile
layout/slot changes. Update planning compares the installed graph with the
fully staged candidate graph, and the CLI prints that diff alongside existing
grant, contract-break, and dependency diagnostics before the transaction can
commit. `--dry-run` therefore reviews the same normalized graph the runtime will
activate without changing live state.

Verification: formatting and diff checks pass. The focused graph-diff test
could not start because `sha2` is absent from the local registry cache and
network access is unavailable.

## 2026-08-21 — Make the resolved graph the runtime inventory

`working tree` · area: module graph and shell discovery

Shell discovery now loads one validated `InstalledModuleGraph` candidate and
constructs runtime module instances, provider registrations, settings schemas,
and frontend catalog inputs from that graph. It no longer discovers arbitrary
module manifests and treats a missing graph as an allow-all filter. Graph load
failure retains the last-known-good runtime; startup with no valid graph
activates no module. Duplicate dependency validation in the shell was removed
so the package graph is the normalized compatibility authority.

Verification: formatting and diff checks pass. Cargo tests remain unavailable
because the local registry cache lacks `sha2` and network access is disabled.

## 2026-08-21 — Publish immutable module activation generations

`working tree` · area: module store and graph activation

Package install, update, uninstall, and rollback now publish validated module
trees into a shared SHA-256 content-addressed store. Objects and generation
snapshots are read-only after atomic publication; the lock generation activates
only after the matching snapshot exists. Installed-graph and shell discovery
consume the active immutable object paths when available, while editable module
trees remain the authoring and local-edit source. Package transaction recovery
protects the active-generation pointer.

Verification: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check` pass. Focused
Cargo tests could not start because `sha2` is absent from the local registry
cache and network access to crates.io is unavailable.

## 2026-08-21 — Recover saved Wi-Fi on Codex network waits

`working tree` · area: developer automation

The backlog runner now reacts to live Codex `waiting for network` errors when
NetworkManager is available: it enables Wi-Fi and attempts the most recently
used saved wireless profile, throttled to one attempt per 30 seconds. Recovery
is best-effort, does not handle credentials, and can be disabled with
`CODEX_WIFI_RECOVERY=0`.

## 2026-08-21 — Fall back from stale temporary-directory settings

`working tree` · area: developer automation

The backlog runner now skips a missing or unwritable `TMPDIR` and falls back to
`/tmp`, which keeps it usable after a Nix shell's temporary directory has been
removed. It reports the fallback and fails with an explicit diagnostic only
when neither location can create the capture directory.

## 2026-08-20 — Fail closed graph candidates and retain live activation

`working tree` · area: module graph activation and shell runtime reloads

Graph-backed frontend catalogs now compile and mount only enabled modules from
the validated installed graph. Discovery no longer registers module-owned
contracts or providers before graph validation, invalid profile roots fail
closed during startup, and graph replacement uses a prepare/commit boundary so
failed reloads keep the last-known-good graph and active frontend/backend
runtime. Package removal also validates the replacement graph before removing
the in-memory module or deactivating its live surface.

Verification: the new last-known-good graph reload regression passed; focused
catalog and frontend activation tests passed; `cargo check -p mesh-core-shell`
and formatting checks passed. The full shell library ran 674 passed, 6 failed,
and 130 ignored; the six failures are the known hover, raster, inspector,
backend-manifest, debug-layout-label, and theme-contract baseline failures
recorded by the Section 15 audit. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-20 — Recover from non-object settings documents

Section 01 foundation hardening is implemented. `SettingsStore::load_from` and
`SettingsStore::from_value` now turn successfully parsed non-object documents
into an empty usable store with declared shell defaults, while retaining one
error diagnostic at the document root. I/O and irrecoverable JSON parse errors
remain fatal.

Verification: `mesh-core-config --lib` passed 35 tests;
`mesh-core-surface-config --lib` passed 28 tests; `cargo check --workspace`
passed with existing warnings. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-20 — Use current context usage for runner rollover

`working tree` · area: developer automation

The Codex `--json` turn usage is cumulative session accounting, so treating it
as current context occupancy produced impossible negative remaining percentages
after long turns. The runner now reads the latest non-ephemeral rollout's
`last_token_usage.total_tokens` and `model_context_window`, honors an explicit
`CODEX_CONTEXT_WINDOW_TOKENS` override, and clamps the displayed percentage.
When the rollout signal is unavailable, it reports the limitation and starts a
fresh session conservatively instead of presenting a false context estimate.

## 2026-08-20 — Add explicit interrupted-turn recovery

`working tree` · area: developer automation

The backlog runner keeps its clean-tree guard as the default but now accepts
`--allow-dirty` for an explicitly requested retry after a canceled turn left
the feature edits in place. The option reports the existing status and allows
the next bounded turn to finish and commit that work; normal invocations still
refuse to risk incorporating unrelated changes.

## 2026-08-20 — Stream concise Codex runner progress

`working tree` · area: developer automation

The backlog runner now tees Codex JSONL into its capture file while filtering
live output to concise session, turn, command, error, and completion updates.
Large command `aggregated_output` and agent-message bodies are retained for
post-turn parsing but are no longer dumped to the terminal when a run is
cancelled or completes.

## 2026-08-20 — Fix unattended Codex permission mode

`working tree` · area: developer automation

The backlog runner now defaults to Codex's explicit unrestricted execution flag
for unattended turns. The previous `--sandbox workspace-write` plus
`--approve-for-me` combination is rejected by the installed CLI; the runner now
uses `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox`, with `CODEX_ALLOW_ALL=0` as a
workspace-write fallback and no incompatible flag combination.

## 2026-08-20 — Add guarded Codex backlog runner

`working tree` · area: developer automation

Added `scripts/codex-backlog-runner.sh` and its output schema. The runner
selects the next unchecked backlog item, gives Codex one bounded feature turn,
requires the item’s removal and planning log record, and refuses to continue
unless exactly one non-merge commit was created directly on `main`. It resumes
the same Codex session between features while its reported usage remains above
the configured rollover threshold, then starts a clean session at approximately
30% context remaining. The context window defaults to 1,050,000 tokens for the
GPT-5.6 family and is configurable for other models. No production MESH code
changed.

The 16 section `improvements.md` audits are now represented in the canonical
[`docs/BACKLOG.md`](../../docs/BACKLOG.md) as feature-level unchecked items,
grouped by section. The checklist includes each audit finding and explicit
feature-direction item; shipped Section 01 findings and audit regression
matrices remain recorded in their existing log/status documents. No production
code changed and no performance claim is made.

DTOs now derive serde serialization; profiling stage and repaint enum labels
retain their established wire names, and the `mesh.debug` payload carries
`DEBUG_TELEMETRY_SCHEMA_VERSION`. Narrow invalidation fields (`narrow_path`,
`affected_node_count`, and `component.script_narrow`) are serialized and covered
by a shell regression test. Overlay/controller fields remain explicitly
host-owned, while the module graph keeps its established nested wire shape via
a compatibility adapter.

Verification: `mesh-core-debug --lib` passed 2 tests; shell benchmark filtered

## 2026-08-20 — Remove the unused foundation EventBus

Section 01 improvement 5 is implemented by removing the unused
`mesh-core-events` workspace crate, shell field, and orphaned dependencies.
The live system already routes extension publications through the scripting
queue and typed shell/service router; no production caller used the foundation
bus. This removes the contract whose unknown-channel and send-failure errors
were unreachable and whose payloads were not actually typed.

under `nix develop`; the lockfile contains no `mesh-core-events` package. No

## 2026-08-20 — Key diagnostics by module instance and issue lifecycle

Section 01 improvement 4 is implemented. Diagnostics now keep stable issue
records keyed by module, instance, and issue code with severity, current
details, first/last seen timestamps, occurrence counts, and active/resolved
state. Health is derived from active issues, so warnings cannot downgrade an
active error and `healthy()` cannot hide unrelated faults. Collector
registrations replace the same identity, explicit unregister removes stale
profile/component state, and module snapshots aggregate deterministically while
retaining instance detail. Backend `Running` transitions resolve prior
lifecycle issues.

Verification: `mesh-core-diagnostics --lib` passed 6 tests; shell service
contract tests passed 12; backend-lifecycle tests passed 22 with 1 existing
dirty-tree failure; and debug tests passed 24 with 2 existing dirty-tree
failures. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-20 — Bound and normalize settings fields

Section 01 improvement 3 is implemented. The shared settings declarations now
support inclusive numeric bounds and canonicalizing enum parsers. Blur passes
are restricted to 1–3, negative blur radii fall back to their field default,
and values such as a whitespace-padded tooltip position are stored canonically.
Validation drops only the rejected field, so valid sibling settings survive;
the stale `fonts.packs` diagnostic-path test now matches the precise nested
location. The LSP settings schema derives the new range hints from the same
runtime declarations.

Verification: `mesh-core-config --lib` passed 34 tests; `mesh-core-surface-config
--lib` passed 28; `mesh-tools-lsp --lib` passed 84; and the shell blur settings
integration test passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-20 — Centralize shell operation authorization

Section 01 improvement 2 is implemented. A typed shell operation registry now
defines the recognized `shell.*` operations, validates their payloads, checks
required capabilities and caller constraints, and gives unknown, malformed,
unauthorized, and dropped requests distinct diagnostic outcomes. Raw shell
events, `mesh.locale.set`, and popover helpers all use the same authorization
function; malformed position margins are rejected instead of being coerced to
zero. Generic interface service commands remain routed through their existing
contract registry after the shell-operation boundary.

Verification: `mesh-core-scripting --lib` passed 186 tests with 27 ignored;
`mesh-core-shell --lib` passed 667 tests with 6 existing dirty-tree failures

## 2026-08-20 — Resolve capability approvals before module activation

Section 01 improvement 1 is implemented. The capability foundation now has a
closed catalog and immutable `EffectiveCapabilities` resolver backed by
explicit `mesh.capabilityApprovals` in the root module graph. Required
capabilities must be approved, optional capabilities are denied unless
explicitly approved, and unknown or malformed declarations fail closed.

Shell frontend and backend activation paths use the resolved grants, and both
the shell package service and CLI persist required approvals during successful
installation. Update planning reads persisted approvals and refuses newly
required capabilities until they are approved. Existing shipped module graph
configuration now records the required approvals.

Verification: `mesh-core-capability` 5 passed; `mesh-core-module` 198 passed,
3 ignored; CLI binary tests 9 passed. The shell library ran 666 passed, 7
existing dirty-tree failures, and 130 ignored; those failures match the
pre-existing baseline recorded below. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-15 — make optimized development runs and production paints explicit

`working tree` · area: developer workflow and render diagnostics

**Measured.** The pre-existing 2026-08-08 A/B measurement used a release-equivalent
Appearance workload with 556 nodes: paint-only frames were 1.977–2.204ms with
the proof versus 1.603–1.698ms when skipped (~19%), and resource-list scroll
frames were 2.613–2.715ms versus 2.279–2.365ms (~13%). This change applies the
previously measured no-proof production path permanently; the focused proof
regressions still pass in test builds.

**Verified.** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`; `cargo check -p mesh-tools-cli
--bin mesh-shell`; and three focused `mesh-core-shell` proof tests passed under
`nix develop`.

## 2026-08-08 — Fix the settings surface opening and closing twice

`working tree` · area: Wayland surface configure lifecycle

Two defects in the render loop's configure path, both observed live against
Hyprland with the layer list and Hyprland's `openlayer`/`closelayer` events as
the measurement.

**A configure could carry a size the protocol had to invent.** `render_layout`
writes `(0, 0)` for a component with no content measurement, and
`observe_surface_size` drops that measurement whenever the available size
changes — so a zero reached the configure on any frame between a configure and
the paint that re-measures. It also survived into the loop's second pass,
because `render` only re-runs `render_layout` while the surface config is dirty
and a component whose content measures exactly what it was laid out against
leaves it clean. On the wire zero is not "unknown" but "span the output", so
`layer_protocol_size` clamped it — for the floating settings surface, to 1x1 at
its anchor corner, logged as an error every time. The visible result was the
surface collapsing to a sliver and popping back to full size on each show and
hide: settings appearing to open twice, and to close, reopen, and close.

Configures are now held back rather than sent with a zero no anchor backs
(`layer_configure_size_is_resolved`, which mirrors `layer_surface_request_size`:
a top/bottom bar spans horizontally, a docked rail spans vertically, a window is
exempt because its size is a hint). `config_changed` stays true, so the real
geometry goes out as soon as it exists. The corrective second pass also
re-dirties the surface config now, so it re-derives the size from the
measurement the paint in between produced instead of resending the first pass's
zeros — which is also what left `@mesh/blur-test` and `@mesh/opacity-test`
permanently mapped at the 1px clamp.

**A hidden layer surface could never be shown again.** Hiding attaches a null
buffer, which returns the surface to the unconfigured state on both sides: the
backend clears `configured` and the hidden frame drops `last_surface_config`.
The render loop then waited on `surface_ready_to_present` before it would send a
configure — waiting for an event only that request can provoke. Every
`ShowSurface` after the first silently did nothing; reproduced on the live shell
before the fix (IPC show, no configure, no `openlayer`, nothing on screen). The
readiness gate now applies only to a surface whose configure has already been
sent. The present itself was always safe: it reports `NotReady` and retains the
damage.

Verification: five layer open/close cycles on the live shell, each exactly one
`openlayer`/`closelayer` pair and one configure at the measured 920x900, plus
promote-to-window, hide/show as a window, and demote back — zero protocol clamps
across the session, against three clamps and a dead reopen before. Two shell
regression tests added (reopen after unmap; no configure carries an unbacked
zero) plus a unit test for the spanning/docked exemptions.
`unconfigured_surface_keeps_pending_frame_until_configure_retry` now renders one
frame first, so it waits on an answer to a configure that was actually sent —
its old setup encoded the state the second defect made unreachable. Full
`mesh-core-shell --lib`: 666 passed, 6 failed, 128 ignored; the same six fixture
failures fail on a clean checkout of the parent commit.

`working tree` · area: theming and shell defaults

The tracked development settings, missing-settings fallback, embedded fallback
theme, and shell-theme backend now all select `tokyo-night`. Core and Luau dark
mode detection also recognize Tokyo Night as dark even though its id does not
contain `dark`.

Verification: formatting and diff checks passed; `mesh-core-theme` (12 tests),

regression passed. The broader shell filter had one unrelated debug-fixture
failure, and the config suite had one unrelated diagnostic-order failure.

## 2026-08-08 — Add the Linux device-information provider

`working tree` · area: settings and backend providers

Added the typed `mesh.device` interface and the `@mesh/device-info` Linux
backend. The provider reads hostname, `/etc/os-release`, kernel and machine
architecture, desktop session, CPU model, physical memory, uptime, and the
