## 2026-08-08 — Convert padded compositor geometry before navigation repaints

`working tree` · area: Wayland surface sizing and interaction repaint

The first pointer-cache fix was insufficient because the render loop itself
could read the compositor-facing layer size (for example 2880×256) and pass it
to `surface_size_changed` before the next retained interaction repaint. That
size includes the navigation bar's 200px tooltip reserve; the component's
content remains 2880×56. A shared conversion now subtracts the configured
surface padding before updating parent layout caches, dynamic-size resolution,
or parent input fallback. Child popup targets retain their own padded geometry.

Added explicit 40×40 navigation-button assertions after pointer press/release
and coverage for padded-parent conversion. Verification: all 17 navigation
interaction tests, 14 surface-layout tests, and 15 child-surface tests passed;
the rebuilt live shell reported the expected 2880×256 compositor buffer. The
full shell baseline still has the five unrelated fixture failures recorded in
the preceding entry.

## 2026-08-08 — Keep navigation-bar geometry stable during pointer input

`working tree` · area: Wayland input routing and surface sizing

Spanning top/bottom layer surfaces expose width `0` in the shell-side surface
record, while the compositor-facing paint buffer includes the transparent
tooltip reserve. Pointer dispatch could cache that padded buffer as the
parent's known size, so the next navigation interaction repaint used the wrong
layout geometry and could collapse the retained bar. Parent input now preserves
the component's content size in the cache; child popup targets continue to keep
their own compositor size.

Added a regression for a spanning parent click with a 1920×56 content surface
and 1920×256 padded buffer. Verification: the focused surface-layout suite
passed (13 tests), child-surface suite passed (15 tests), navigation settings
click passed, and `cargo fmt -- --check` plus `git diff --check` passed. The
broader shell suite passed 656 tests with five existing failures in unrelated
composition/manifest/debug and shipped-navigation fixture expectations.

## 2026-08-08 — Preserve contribution styles across interaction repaints

`working tree` · area: surface rendering and retained restyle

Navigation items mounted through customizable extension-point slots are
alternate compiled roots, not local imports. The retained interaction restyle
cache now includes the authored styles for the contributions actually hosted by
the surface, including contributions supplied by another module. The
navigation control cluster also returns to intrinsic shrink-wrapped width;
fixed width was masking the layout regression and left hover dispositions
wrong.

Added a regression for contribution hover selectors. The focused shell test is
currently blocked before compilation because the environment has no native
`xkbcommon.pc`; formatter, diff validation, and the frontend compiler suite
pass.

## 2026-08-07 — Audio controls migrate to the shared navigation VM

`working tree` · area: production popovers and input capture

The last shipped standalone popover, `@mesh/audio-popover`, is now a
`component` module consumed by `@mesh/navigation-bar`. The volume trigger owns
the open/close hover bridge, while the audio component exposes pointer-enter,
pointer-leave, drag-start, drag-end, and keyboard-dismiss events. Its slider
continues to use core's active-slider pointer capture, so leaving the popup
while dragging does not close or lose the control. The old standalone surface
manifest and debug benchmark target were removed from the production path.

The shipped module graph now classifies audio as an enabled component, and the
navigation regression proves hover opens one promoted child popup anchored to
the volume button. Existing standalone legacy-popover fixtures remain only for
coverage of the compatibility path.

Verification: audio real-surface integration passed (9 tests), debug inspector
integration passed (8 tests), benchmark shell tests passed (16 tests), and the
shipped module-graph regression passed. The navigation interaction module had
16 passing tests and one pre-existing focus-diagnostic failure:
`navigation_bar_pointer_click_opens_settings_and_updates_focus_diagnostic`.

## 2026-08-07 — Nested escaping overlays derive child surfaces

`working tree` · area: child-surface derivation

Automatic `Overflow` promotion previously inspected only direct children of the
surface root. Nested absolutely positioned overlays could therefore extend
beyond the parent SHM buffer and disappear at the buffer edge. The derivation
now walks nested descendants, uses their transformed surface-space bounds for
the popup request, skips explicit `<popover>` subtrees that already own a
promoted surface, and respects `overflow: hidden|auto|scroll` clipping
ancestors.

Verification: the shell component unit module passed (26 tests, 1 ignored),
the child-surface suite passed (15 tests), and the popover suite passed (14
tests). `cargo fmt --all` completed successfully.

## 2026-08-07 — Promoted popovers measure before their first popup configure

`working tree` · area: popup sizing and shell render loop

Legacy separate-module popovers used a protocol-safe `(1, 1)`
`PopupPlacement` size while waiting for their first content measurement. That
placeholder also became the layout bound, so an intrinsic cross-axis could
measure to one pixel and remain pinned there. The shell now derives a generous
bound from the configured parent surface and passes it through a new intrinsic
`SurfaceExtent`: the bound is available to layout, but its axes remain unknown
to the synthetic surface root and can shrink to measured content before the
`xdg_popup` is configured.

The regression covers a content-measured promoted popup with a 1920×56 parent
and asserts that its first paint buffer uses the parent bound rather than 1×1.

Verification: under `nix develop`, the focused popover suite passed (14 tests),
including the new first-measurement regression; `cargo fmt --all -- --check`
passed.

## 2026-08-07 — Wayland presentation is frame-batched

`working tree` · area: presentation

The Wayland backend stages each surface's SHM commit during presentation and
flushes/dispatches the shared connection once through `PresentationEngine::finish_frame()`
at the shell-frame boundary. The testing backend regression proves that two
surface presents share one explicit completion boundary.

Verification: `presents_share_one_explicit_frame_completion` passed and
`cargo check -p mesh-core-presentation` passed.

## 2026-08-07 — Phase26 icon-cache proof is resource-pack independent

`working tree` · area: shell profiling tests

The Phase26 real-surface proof no longer assumes that semantic icons use the
process-wide SVG/bitmap raster cache. Font-backed icon packs legitimately report
glyph-cache activity instead, so the proof now accepts raster or glyph cache
activity and reuse while preserving the same five scenarios.

Verification: the focused proof passed; the default-parallel
`mesh-core-shell --lib` run passed the Phase26 proof (644 passed, 5 unrelated
pre-existing failures, 124 ignored). The existing formatter check passed.

## 2026-08-07 — Composition and configuration writes are capability-gated contracts

`working tree` · area: settings, module system, capabilities

Stage 1 removed the hardcoded `@mesh/settings` host from `catalog.rs`, but the
same literal survived in `service.rs`, where it was doing more damage:

```rust
"shell.set-provider" if event.source_module_id == "@mesh/settings" => ...
"shell.set-module-enabled" if event.source_module_id == "@mesh/settings" => ...
"shell.set-module-prop" if event.source_module_id == "@mesh/settings" => ...
```

Seven reserved `shell.*` channels were gated on the publishing module's **name**.
A replacement settings frontend could be mounted — Stage 1 made that structurally
possible — and then silently do nothing, because every write it issued was
dropped by a string comparison. The channels were also a contract in nothing but
name: no argument types, no capability, and no diagnostic when a payload was
wrong, since a non-matching arm just fell through to `None`.

**Writes are now methods on core-provided interfaces.** `mesh.settings` gains
`set_prop`/`unset_prop`, `mesh.theme` gains `set_theme`/`set_icon_theme`/
`set_font_family`, and a new `mesh.packages` contract carries
`set_module_enabled`/`set_provider`/`switch_profile`. These are declared like any
other contract, so they flow through the *existing* generic path: a published
`mesh.packages.set_provider` is split into interface and command, checked against
`service.packages.control`, and refused with a diagnostic naming the missing
capability. No module id is consulted anywhere in that path.

**The shell answers its own interfaces.** `dispatch_service_command` previously
knew only Luau backends, so a core interface would have returned
`service_unavailable`. It now translates a core-provided command into the
`CoreRequest` that performs it and applies it directly, pushing follow-up
requests onto `deferred_requests` — the queue a profile switch already used.
Argument extraction is strict: a payload that does not match the declared
contract yields `None` and is reported as unsupported, rather than applied with a
defaulted value.

**Two things this exposed.** `theme.write` was declared a High-privilege
capability and enforced nowhere; `shell.set-theme` was ungated, so any module
could restyle the entire shell. Theme writes now require `service.theme.control`,
and `theme.write` is deleted from the vocabulary rather than left as a second
name for the same thing. Regression:
`setting_the_theme_now_requires_a_capability`.

Conversely, `shell.set-locale` **was** correctly gated — at the host-API
boundary, where `mesh.locale.set` enforces `locale.write` before publishing.
Deleting it broke three locale tests and it was restored. A second,
service-shaped route in would have meant two capability names for one write, so
`mesh.locale` deliberately gains no method.

**Scope.** `shell.open-debug-source` is still gated on
`@mesh/debug-inspector`. That id appears at six sites in the shell (surface
identity, benchmark scenarios, profiling), so converting one of them in
isolation would have been cosmetic; it stays with the open backlog item for
moving built-in debug behavior behind generic providers.

The settings frontend's **reads** were already a revisioned `mesh.settings`
broadcast and did not change. What did not close: `publish_resolved_props` still
injects each component's namespace as a raw `settings` prop, so the backlog item
was narrowed to that rather than deleted.

Verification: `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` fails exactly the same 8
tests as `c4c29292`, by name. Eight `surface::icon::tests::*` failures appeared
in one intermediate run and not the next; they pass 14/14 in isolation and are
the known parallel raster-cache contention, not a regression. New regressions:
`a_core_provided_command_is_applied_by_the_shell_itself` (asserts real state
change, not just an `ok` response), `a_core_provided_command_without_its_capability_is_denied`,
`a_core_provided_command_with_a_malformed_payload_changes_nothing`, and
`the_settings_module_id_grants_nothing_by_itself`.


## 2026-08-07 — Compositions are modules; extension points are contracts

`working tree` · area: module system, installation, settings

Five staged changes making a shell composition installable, updatable, and
forkable, and removing the last module id hardcoded into the shell. Design:
[`todos/pending/2026-08-07-composition-modules-and-extension-points.md`](../todos/pending/2026-08-07-composition-modules-and-extension-points.md).

**Extension points replace module-keyed slots.** `provides_slots` produced slot
ids like `@mesh/settings:custom-settings`, so every contributor named the host
and replacing the settings frontend silently dropped every contributed page.
Worse, it was structurally unusable from a manifest: the canonical loader left
`slot_contributions` empty, so the only way to fill a slot was
`const SETTINGS_HOST: &str = "@mesh/settings"` in `catalog.rs` — core policy in
the wiring layer, the `if service == "audio"` bug in a different domain.
Extension points are now versioned contracts declared by interface modules
(`mesh.extensionPoints`), hosted explicitly (`mesh.hosts`), and contributed to
by contract name (`mesh.provides.extensionPoints`). `SlotDefinition`,
`SlotContribution`, `provides_slots`, `slot_contributions`, and
`mesh.entrypoints.settings_ui` are deleted outright, not aliased. A contribution
now creates **no dependency edge on its host** — the edge terminates at the
contract — which is why `detects_cycles_through_slot_hosts` was replaced by
`host_and_contributor_are_not_a_dependency_cycle`: the adapter pattern was
previously an unloadable cycle.

**Composition modules.** `kind: "composition"` carries `mesh.compose`
(structurally a `ShellProfile` plus `slots`) and `mesh.extends`. A profile
becomes an instance: `schemaVersion: 2` adds an optional `from`, and resolution
layers base composition → derived composition → profile deltas. Two merge rules
are deliberate rather than incidental: ordered resource chains **replace** (an
icon chain is an ordered fallback list; interleaving two orderings is
meaningless), and a user override for a root the composition dropped is
**retained and reported** (`orphaned_profile_override`) rather than discarded,
since dropping it loses the user's work on every upstream rename. A composition
declares no capabilities — `composition_declares_capability` is a hard error —
or it becomes the privileged layer replaceable modules exist to avoid.

**Lock v2.** `mesh.lock` gained `version`, a `sha256` content digest over the
installed tree, `requestedBy`, and generations archived under `lock-history/`.
The digest is what makes `spec/02` §1's "updates preserve edits" enforceable
rather than aspirational; previously local edits were undetectable. Lock writes
are now transactional: `persist_git_provenance`'s warn-and-continue was right
for a one-off install and wrong for a composition, where the lock *is* the
rollback record.

**Update transaction.** `mesh update` resolves candidate revisions via
`git show <rev>:module.json` (version without checkout), diffs
`InterfaceContract`s as data to classify breakage without executing module code,
diffs capabilities for elevated/high re-approval, and refuses to overwrite
edited trees. `--dry-run`, `--keep`, `--replace`, `mesh rollback`,
`mesh uninstall` (refuses while `requestedBy` is non-empty), and
`mesh lock verify` ship with it. Rollback re-fetches by revision rather than
archiving trees, since git is already content-addressed.

**Settings-UI ladder.** composition override > module page > generated
fallback. The generated fallback is kept — without it a third-party module has
zero settings UI until someone writes an adapter, which centralizes worse than
the hardcoding being removed. A suppressed page reads as absent so the host
falls back to generated rows rather than showing nothing.

Also: version resolution over the enabled closure with a one-version-per-module-id
rule (the id is also the settings namespace and surface instance key, so two
copies would fork the settings store), `mesh.uses.sources` for fetching
uninstalled dependencies, `mesh profile prune`, and the LSP manifest schema
updated in step with every manifest change.

Verification: `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast -- --test-threads=1` under
`nix develop`. Failures are exactly the pre-existing baseline, each confirmed by
re-running the same suites at HEAD with the working tree stashed:
`mesh-core-config` 1 (`an_unknown_shell_section_is_reported_without_losing_its_siblings`),
`mesh-core-module` 1 (`shipped_module_graph_loads_repo_module_fixture`, 4 vs 5
frontends), `mesh-core-shell` 5 (two real-surface, one interaction, two debug).
Everything else green, including `mesh-tools-lsp` (91) after the schema update.
New coverage: 13 extension-point tests (including the host-rename gate), 10
composition tests (including composition-backed vs hand-written equivalence),
6 resolution tests, 7 lock tests, 8 contract-diff tests, and 9 CLI tests with a
real two-revision git fixture.

unless smooth behavior is requested.

## 2026-08-04 — The navigation bar was measuring a 1px stand-in surface

`working tree` · area: shell render loop, surface measurement, presentation

Reported as "the navigation bar renders half its size, the upper part is out of
bounds", and before that (2026-08-03, on KDE) as the shell exiting with `all 3
SHM buffers are busy for 1920x201 surface` with the bar off-screen. Both are the
same defect, and it reproduces on Hyprland: `hyprctl layers` showed the shipped
bar as a **1920x201** layer surface painting nothing, where 201 is 1px of
content plus the 200px tooltip reserve.

### What actually happened

A surface's first frame has no size on either axis — the compositor has not
configured it and nothing has been measured. The shell resolved that to `1` (a
buffer needs real pixels), and `finalize_tree` stamped it onto the synthetic
`surface` root as a **definite** `Px(1)` box. `.nav-shell` declares
`height: 56px`; laid out in a 1px surface it comes out 1px tall, and
`measure_content_size` faithfully reports the collapse. `render_layout` then
sends that 1 to the compositor as the bar's height — and a *nonzero* height is
no longer a dynamic axis, so `resolve_dynamic_surface_size` never consults the
compositor's real size again. The bar stays 1px of content in a 1920x201 buffer
for the life of the process, and repeatedly presenting that surface is what
saturated the three SHM buffers on KDE.

Instrumented paint on the live shell: content extent `1x1` then `1920x1`, child
style `height: Px(56.0)`, child layout height `1.0`, `measured_size=(1920, 1)`.
An isolated Taffy repro confirmed the mechanism and the shape of the fix — a
definite `Px(1)` root collapses the child to 1x1; an `auto` root axis measures
56 whatever the available space is.

### The fix

An axis with no size is not a small size. `SurfaceExtent.content` now carries
`0` for an axis the shell could not resolve, `paint` records which axes those
are, and `finalize_tree` gives the surface root `Dimension::Auto` there instead
of `Px(stand-in)`. The stand-in still sizes the buffer and the damage rects; it
just never becomes a layout box. Content measurement measures content again.

Kept from the first pass at this bug (which fixed real things but not the
cause): the first unmeasured layer configure is deferred to the immediate
re-render, so the compositor sees one configure instead of two, and a saturated
SHM pool returns `PresentStatus::NotReady` — ordinary Wayland backpressure —
instead of a fatal `BufferAlloc` that killed the process.

### Verification

Live on Hyprland: the navigation layer is **1920x256** (56 content + 200
reserve) from exactly one configure, the bar paints in full (workspaces, title,
clock, control cluster), and 25s of running produced no busy-buffer error.

`unmeasured_navigation_bar_measures_its_own_height_not_the_placeholder` paints
the shipped `@mesh/navigation-bar` with `SurfaceExtent::padded((0,0),(1,201))`
— exactly what `render_components` passes before the first configure — and
asserts 56. Reverting the `Auto` branch fails it 1 vs 56.

`mesh-core-shell` is 628 passed / 9 failed / 125 ignored: the eight documented
deterministic failures plus the documented parallel-only phase26 raster-cache
one, with the new test the only delta. `mesh-core-presentation` 64/64,
`mesh-core-elements` 210/210.

Session record: [`../debug/kde-navigation-shm-buffers.md`](../debug/kde-navigation-shm-buffers.md).

## 2026-08-04 — Red test baseline triage: 23 failures to 8, two real bugs

`working tree` · area: shell component runtime, scripting state, test suite

The 2026-08-03 red baseline was 23 failing tests across three crates. It is now
8, all in `mesh-core-shell`. `mesh-core-elements` (210 passed),
`mesh-core-frontend` (64 passed), and every other workspace crate are green;
`mesh-core-shell` is 627 passed / 8 failed / 125 ignored single-threaded,
against 614 / 21 / 125 before.

Two of the failures were **real defects**, not stale expectations.

### A dragged slider stopped repainting after the first move

`slider_drag_repaints_across_multiple_pointer_moves` was correct and the
product was wrong. A handlerless slider drag takes the targeted
interaction-restyle path, whose changed-node set
(`collect_interaction_changed_node_ids`) diffs hover, focus, focus-visible,
active, and checked. A slider value is none of those — it is *painted content*.
So the second and later pointer moves of a drag found an empty changed set,
`empty_restyle_avoided` short-circuited the frame, and the display list kept
the previous knob position. The widget tree was correct throughout: it recorded
`value="90.91"` then `value="27.27"` while the painted bytes stayed
**identical** (0 of 38,400 differed), and forcing a full tree rebuild painted
the moved knob, which is what isolated the invalidation path as the culprit.

Fixed by tracking `previous_slider_values` and diffing it into the changed-node
set. Unlike the `checked` diff it is **not** gated on
`cached_restyle_state_dependencies` — a slider repaints whether or not any
selector mentions it. All 18 slider tests pass.

### Host service state never reached the Luau `_ENV`

`raw_service_state_update_schedules_repaint_without_proxy_tracking` rendered
`{last_service_update.name}` as `""` when the state held `"audio"`. Template
expressions are compiled to Luau closures over the context's `_ENV`, but
`seed_service_state` and `apply_service_update_*` wrote only to the Rust
`ScriptState`. Any template expression naming a host-injected global therefore
evaluated against a nil Luau global. A probe confirmed the split precisely: a
script-authored `localobj.name` rendered `LOCAL` while `last_service_update.name`,
`last_service_update`, and `audio.percent` all rendered empty from the same
build, with the correct values visible in `ScriptState` at build time.

Fixed with `seed_service_context`, which seeds the trace through
`seed_context_global` (state *and* `_ENV`), plus an `_ENV` refresh on update.
`set_with_fingerprint_lazy` now returns whether it installed the value, so the
refresh costs one JSON-to-Lua conversion only when the trace actually changed —
unchanged fan-out probes still allocate nothing. Service *payload* globals keep
their existing proxy design (`__mesh_svc_*` reached via `require`); only the
host-owned trace changed.

### Stale expectations corrected

- Two `activation.rs` tests had their input variants crossed: the keydown test
  sent two `KeyReleased`s so `onKeyDown` never ran, and the option-activation
  test sent only `KeyPressed` when choice activation lands in
  `handle_key_released`. Both now send a real press/release cycle.
- `indexed_theme_defaults_...` indexed declarations positionally;
  `ComponentDefaults` is insertion-ordered (a `Vec`), so the assertions now
  match on the declaration.
- `shipped_navigation_style_expected_diagnostics_do_not_block_tokens` asserted a
  hover lift that moved to `settings-button.mesh` on 2026-07-30; that component
  joined the fixture set.
- `for_node_wrapper_carries_no_padding_or_gap` looked for the synthetic `{#for}`
  `column` wrapper that layout-transparent control flow removed. Renamed to
  `for_node_children_expand_into_the_parent_without_a_wrapper` and now asserts
  the stronger guarantee: no wrapper exists and loop bodies are direct children.
- `targeted_default_merge_only_updates_affected_subtrees` tested element tag
  defaults that `apply_runtime_attribute_state` no longer owns (they resolve in
  style resolution). Rewritten around the `hidden` attribute it does own,
  keeping the subtree-scoping guarantee.
- Two theme tests asserted a total delivered-event count of 1; a theme reload
  also broadcasts `mesh.settings` now. They count `mesh.theme` events via a new
  `recorded_updates_for` helper.
- `backend_command_result_records_debug_method_result` never registered a
  backend runtime, so the (correct) active-provider gate dropped the result.
- `handlerless_slider_drag_...` asserted a key-derived `NodeId` against a
  hand-built tree, whose nodes carry freshly minted ids.
- Navigation lookups matched the whole `class` attribute, so
  `class="nav-shell"` becoming `class="nav-shell nav-shell-blur"` hid the node;
  they use the existing `first_node_with_class_token`. The audio trigger's
  `aria-label` became the dynamic `"Volume…"`, matched by a new
  `first_node_with_attr_prefix`.

### One failure was cascade, not signal

`SETTINGS_ENV_LOCK` guards `()`, but a panic under it poisoned the mutex and
two unrelated theme tests then failed on `PoisonError` rather than their own
assertions. `settings_env_lock()` recovers the guard, so one genuine failure
reports once.

**Verification:** `cargo test --workspace --lib` under `nix develop`. Every
crate passes except `mesh-core-shell`; its 8 remaining failures are listed in
`docs/BACKLOG.md`. `cargo fmt --all` clean; no new clippy warnings.

## 2026-08-03 — Codebase refactor, part 5: shell and tools

`working tree` · area: shell composition, component runtime, CLI/LSP

This closes the refactor. **No file in the workspace exceeds 2000 lines.**

**Files split:**

| Was | Became |
| --- | --- |
| `shell/tests.rs` (9411) | `tests/{mod,common,scheduler,service_delivery,service_state,debug,benchmarks,profiling,commands,input,popover,window_role,child_surface,theme,service_contract,backend_lifecycle,graph,surface_layout,discovery}` |
| `component/runtime_tree.rs` (3727) | `runtime_tree/{mod,tree,fingerprint,node_id,annotate,service_deps}` + `runtime_tree/tests/{mod,tree,fingerprint,node_id,annotate,service_deps,benchmarks}` |
| `component/shell_component.rs` (3526) | `shell_component/{mod,internals,damage,child_surface,tests}` |
| `component/tests/interaction/navigation.rs` (3978) | `interaction/{gestures,activation,keybinds,navigation}` |
| `component/tests/integration/real_surfaces.rs` (3270) | `real_surfaces/{mod,settings,layout,transitions,audio,brightness,raster,hover,commands,inspector,bubbles}` |
| `component/rendering.rs` (2425) | `rendering/{mod,annotate,tests,interaction_changed_key_tests}` |
| `runtime/render.rs` (2023) | `render/{mod,child,popup,tests,performance_tests}` |

**A trait impl cannot be divided.** `shell_component/mod.rs` stays at 1720
lines because `impl ShellComponent for FrontendSurfaceComponent` is one
1651-line item. Splitting it would mean turning trait methods into thin
forwarders over inherent methods elsewhere — a design change, not a file move,
so it was not done. `render/mod.rs` and `rendering/mod.rs` keep their inherent
impls for the same reason, but those *can* be split by method, and
`render/child.rs` takes the child-surface half.

**Visibility follows the old scope, not the new nesting.** Items a flat file
exported to `component` keep `pub(in crate::shell::component)`; items it kept
private but that now cross a file boundary take `pub(super)`, which is the
new module's own subtree. Nothing became more visible than it was.

**Duplicate helper removed.** `first_node_with_class_token` was defined twice,
once in `navigation.rs` and once in `real_surfaces.rs`; it and
`rect_matches_bounds` now live in `component/tests/common.rs`. Three helpers
part 2 orphaned (`make_panel_ctx`, `contains_interactive_tags`, `count_tag`)
had no callers and were deleted, as was the unused `unique_test_file` and the
`Display` re-export part 2 left behind.

**Comment volume.** Shell 2376 → 2370, tools 652 → 647. Far smaller than
parts 1-3 (-12%) and deliberately so: this crate's comments are protocol,
invalidation, and compositor-behavior reasoning that the code does not state.
What went was one duplicated comment pair, dead identifiers from the frozen
`.planning/archive/` ticket system (`T-102-05`, "Phase 2", "Phase 12"), a
stale "spec 03 phase 1" pointer replaced with the real doc path, five passages
narrating what the code replaced rather than what it does, and four LSP
comments restating the line below them.

**Found, not fixed.** Two items filed in `docs/BACKLOG.md` instead of changed
here, both behavior questions rather than refactors:

- The LSP still serves `package.json` as a MESH manifest and accepts
  `package.json`/`mesh.toml` as module roots, against the canonical
  `module.json`-only rule.
- Parts 1-3 orphaned 23 items that now warn as dead code — 20 in
  `mesh-core-render` from removing the recursive painter, three substring
  extractors in `installed_graph/scan.rs` that the AST scan replaced. They are
  either public API or deletable; that is a decision, not a move.

**Verification.** `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` reports no errors.
`cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib` reports 610 passed / 20 failed / 125
ignored — identical counts to the pre-refactor baseline captured on the same
checkout, and the same 20 failures by name (only their module paths changed).
Per-file test counts are unchanged in every split file: shell tests 177,
navigation 60, real_surfaces 42, runtime_tree 49, shell_component 25,
rendering 22, render 13. `mesh-tools-lsp` and `mesh-tools-cli` suites pass.
`cargo fmt --all` and `git diff --check` pass. No performance claim is
recorded for this structural work.

The refactor is complete: foundation, platform, surface-config, UI, frontend,
runtime, extension, presentation, shell, and tools are all done.

## 2026-08-03 — Codebase refactor, part 4: presentation

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation

**File split** (no file in the crate now exceeds 2000 lines; the largest is
`wayland_surface/handlers.rs` at 1158, unchanged):

| Was | Became |
| --- | --- |
| `presentation/wayland_surface/backend.rs` (3465) | `backend/{mod,config,protocol,entry,shm,damage,surfaces,present,events}` and `backend/tests/{mod,config,damage,shm,scale,entry,benchmarks}` |

`WaylandSurfaceBackend`'s 1100-line inherent impl is split three ways:
`surfaces` (creation, configuration, popups, teardown, output geometry),
`present` (the SHM/blur/input-region commit and the surface queries), and
`events` (dispatch, poll, blocking wait). `SurfaceEntry` and its Wayland role
enum moved to `entry`; the damage arithmetic and the SHM pool sizing are now
separately testable modules.

Items the old flat file kept private but that now cross a module boundary carry
`pub(super)`, which is `backend`-wide — exactly the old file's scope. Items the
old file marked `pub(super)` (reached from sibling `state.rs`/`handlers.rs`)
carry `pub(in crate::wayland_surface)`, again the old scope. Nothing became
more visible than it was.

**Comment volume.** 685 → 656 lines (-4%). The presentation crate's comments
are mostly load-bearing protocol reasoning, so the cut is smaller than parts 1-3
and deliberately so. What went: dead identifiers from the frozen
`.planning/archive/` milestone system (`BLUR-01..04`, `CR-01`, `WR-01`, `D-01`,
`T-102-01/02/06`, "Pitfall 1") and two references to a `CONTEXT.md` that no
longer exists — the surrounding explanations were kept, only the unresolvable
pointers removed. A duplicated 15-line doc block on `layer_protocol_size` (the
superseded first copy) and the numbered step commentary in `wait_for_events`
also went.

**Found and fixed.** The `present_with_damage` SHM comment still opened with
"SHM copy region must always be a union — fold all rects", contradicting the
next sentence and the code, which retains disjoint per-buffer damage. That line
was a leftover from before disjoint damage landed; removed.

**Verification.** `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` is clean apart from
the one unused-import warning in `component/tests/interaction.rs` that part 2
recorded as pre-existing. `cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation` reports 59
passed / 12 ignored, identical to the pre-split baseline, and the crate's
`#[test]`/`#[ignore]` attribute counts (71 / 5) are unchanged. `cargo fmt --all`
and `git diff --check` pass. No performance claim is recorded for this
structural work.

Remaining sections: shell, tools.

## 2026-08-03 — Codebase refactor, part 3: runtime and extension crates

`working tree` · area: extension module/service, runtime scripting/backend

**Files split** (no file in either section now exceeds 2000 lines; the largest
is `runtime/backend/src/lib.rs` at 1657, unchanged):

| Was | Became |
| --- | --- |
| `extension/module/package/tests.rs` (5053) | `tests/{mod,common,manifest,loading,shipped,graph,contributions,interfaces,surfaces,diagnostics,luau_scan}` |
| `extension/module/package/installed_graph.rs` (2867) | `installed_graph/{mod,graph,health,scan,diagnostics,contributions,load}` |
| `runtime/scripting/context/tests.rs` (4155) | `tests/{mod,common,imports,lifecycle,storage,state,sync,events,interface_proxy,shared_vm,refs,template}` |
| `runtime/scripting/context/runtime.rs` (2753) | `runtime/{mod,vm,context,template,state,lifecycle,host_api,sync,benchmarks,helpers}` |

`ScriptContext`'s 2339-line inherent impl is now split across those modules.
Splitting an impl across files forces its fields and cross-module methods to
carry a visibility marker; they use `pub(super)`, which is exactly the old
file's scope, and `pub(in crate::context)` where the original said `pub(super)`
from one level up. The same applies to `installed_graph`, which uses
`pub(in crate::package)` for items its sibling `tests` module reaches.

**`benchmarks.rs` is `#[cfg(test)]` as a module.** All 19 of the extracted
`_for_benchmark`/`_for_test` methods carried the attribute individually; gating
the module once removes 19 attributes and keeps the non-test lib build honest.

**Comment volume.** Runtime and extension: 1124 → 989 lines (-12%). The
reductions are concentrated in `manifest/model.rs` (127 → 94),
`context/runtime/context.rs` (66 → 49), `package/luau_scan.rs` (57 → 45), and
`context/state.rs` (58 → 44). Two kinds of text went: historical narrative
about what the code replaced (`luau_scan.rs`'s account of the substring
scanners it superseded — that history belongs here, not in the source), and
restated signatures. Stale references to `mesh.toml`, `package.json`, and
`icons.toml` in doc comments were corrected to the canonical `module.json`.

**Found, not fixed.** `mesh.surfaceLayout` is still accepted as a compatibility
input aliasing `mesh.surface`, which the manifest rule says should instead be a
migration diagnostic. No shipped module uses it. Filed in `docs/BACKLOG.md`
rather than changed here — it is a behavior change, not a refactor.

**Verification.** `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` is clean apart from
the one unused-import warning in `component/tests/interaction.rs` that part 2
recorded as pre-existing. Test counts are identical to the pre-split baseline
in every affected crate: `mesh-core-module` 158 passed / 3 ignored,
`mesh-core-scripting` 176 / 27, `mesh-core-service` 22 / 5, `mesh-core-backend`
25 / 0. `cargo fmt --all` and `git diff --check` pass. No performance claim is
recorded for this structural work.

Remaining sections: presentation and shell, tools.

## 2026-08-02 — Generated prop controls target live instances

`working tree` · area: component settings

The generated settings UI now exposes each frontend module's live root-instance
ids and lets a prop row cycle between “All instances” and a concrete instance.
The selected scope reads its effective inherited value, writes through typed
`CoreRequest` variants to `props.instances.<instance-id>`, and resets sparsely
back to the global value. The shell rejects instance ids that do not identify a
live instance of the requested module.

Focused verification under `nix develop` passes the global and instance sparse
update tests, global and instance event-decoding tests, and the real settings
surface regression (5 tests total). Custom `settings_ui` mounting remains open.

## 2026-08-02 — Popups and overlays verified complete

`working tree` · area: popovers and component runtime

The stale popup/overlay umbrella item is closed. In-tree `<popover>` content is
already promoted to transient `xdg_popup` child surfaces with custom content,
content sizing, anchoring, pointer and keyboard routing, hover-bridge dismissal,
one-open-per-trigger exclusivity, and compositor-dismiss synchronization. The
remaining automatic-overflow and legacy-popover migration work stays tracked as
separate backlog items.

The completion audit exposed two composition regressions and removed an
intentional debug-dump test that had been left in the suite. Handler props now
preserve arguments bound by the child (for example `onselect(item.id)`) after
resolving the parent's callback, and Luau template-expression invalidation now
accumulates changed public members across multiple synchronizations until the
template evaluation boundary. The language popover consequently dispatches its
selected locale and repaints the trigger flag immediately; its event payload is
also the primary source for that flag.

The focused scripting cache regression passes, and the shell popover suite
passes with 41 tests plus one ignored release-only benchmark.

## 2026-08-02 — Widget-tree paint uses the display-list command builder

`working tree` · area: rendering

The compatibility `render_tree*` entrypoints now build a transient display list
and replay its commands, matching the retained production path. The parallel
recursive `WidgetNode` painter and its private traversal helpers were deleted,
so clipping, z-order, filters, controls, and effects have one authoritative
lowering path. This exposed and corrected a stale parity expectation: CSS
`filter` now emits the retained push/pop layer scope through either entrypoint.

The complete `mesh-core-render` library suite passes: 200 tests passed and 37
release-only benchmarks were ignored.

## 2026-08-02 — Stored frontend props are validated and ejectable

`working tree` · area: component settings

Frontend global and per-instance prop overrides now validate against the
primary component's canonical `<props>` declarations during startup, live
reload, embedded-component initialization, and `config doctor`. Unknown,
unexposed, wrong-typed, and constraint-violating values produce located
diagnostics and are removed from the runtime-facing namespace so the declared
default wins; the stored document remains untouched for repair.

`mesh-shell config eject <module-id>` now compiles the frontend declaration and
materializes effective exposed global props beside the effective surface
placement. Existing valid globals remain effective, declared defaults fill the
previously sparse block, instance overrides are preserved, and internal props
stay absent. The surface-config tests (28), focused shell prop tests (12 passed,
2 release-only benchmarks ignored), workspace checks, and an isolated real CLI
eject of `@mesh/navigation-bar` pass.

## 2026-08-02 — Settings renders the complete per-module graph

`working tree` · area: settings and module diagnostics

Installed-module rows in the advanced settings page now have an expandable
details control. The view reports required and optional interfaces with their
active providers, provided interfaces, module dependencies, required and
optional capabilities, icon/font/theme resources, native binary availability,
i18n catalogs, keybinds, and combined graph health and diagnostics. Empty
categories stay explicit rather than disappearing.

The settings frontend now accepts the canonical `interface=provider` strings
emitted by `mesh.debug.module_graph` as well as object-shaped values, fixing a
fixture-masked mismatch that could leave the active-provider section empty in
the running shell. The full frontend-compiler and module suites pass (63 + 158
tests; 20 + 3 release-only tests ignored), along with focused production graph
serialization and real settings-surface tests.

## 2026-08-02 — Core service calls join generic interface routing

`working tree` · area: module runtime and profiling

The audio contract now declares `play_sound(path)`. Startup sound dispatch
builds an ordinary capability-bearing `ServiceCommand` from `@mesh/shell`, so
contract validation, active-provider selection, method-call diagnostics, and
backend profiling all run through the same dispatcher used by module calls;
the shell no longer reaches into the `mesh.audio` handler map directly.

The backend-update benchmark also selects a running provider and matching
profiling sample by `(interface, provider)` rather than naming `mesh.audio`.
Its frontend cost is the largest rendered surface sample in the session, so
the scenario works for network, power, window-management, and future backend
interfaces without adding branches. Focused sound-routing, six backend
benchmark, and shipped-graph tests pass, along with `cargo check` for the shell.

The backlog item remains open for core-owned `mesh.debug`, `mesh.theme`, and
`mesh.locale` state paths that still need replaceable generic providers.

## 2026-08-02 — Frontend props generate settings controls

`working tree` · area: component settings

Installed frontend modules now project the exposed props of their primary
`.mesh` component into the module graph as a typed settings schema. The
settings frontend renders the resulting controls and reset actions, seeds them
from effective values, and sends typed set/unset requests. The shell restricts
those requests to `@mesh/settings`, validates names, types, and constraints
against the owning prop declarations, persists sparse profile-scoped global
overrides, reapplies live component settings, and requests a render.

The original backlog item is narrowed rather than closed: arbitrary
`settings_ui` components are recorded but are not mounted, and generated
controls do not yet target individual component instances.

The complete component, frontend-compiler, and module suites pass, as do the
focused shell tests for event authorization, typed requests, sparse override
updates, graph projection, and the real settings surface. The full shell suite
still has 23 unrelated existing failures (593 passed, 125 ignored), including
isolated option activation, navigation layout, and backend-debug assertions;
the same failures reproduce when run individually outside the changed paths.

## 2026-08-02 — External interface contracts reach runtime tooling

`working tree` · area: module contracts and LSP

Canonical manifest loading now resolves module-relative `contract.json` files
through the same validated package-loader path used by installed graph
construction. The LSP indexes the resulting `InterfaceContract` as the
authoritative interface shape and exposes exact Luau state types, method
arguments and returns, and event payloads; backend-source inference remains a
fallback only when no declared contract exists. Contract-backed proxy
completion also matches the shipped runtime surface instead of offering the
obsolete `on_change` helper.

The complete `mesh-core-module` and `mesh-tools-lsp` test suites pass (158 + 83
unit tests, 6 LSP integration tests; 3 release-only module benchmarks ignored).

## 2026-08-01 — Typed embedded handler targets

`working tree` · area: component composition

`WidgetNode` event handlers and pre-bound handler calls now retain a typed
`HandlerTarget`: the local script handler is distinct from the optional embedded
component instance that owns it. Compiler namespacing, interaction lookup,
scheduled handlers, keybind subscribers, pressed-handler snapshots, and shell
dispatch pass that target without constructing or reparsing the former
`__mesh_embed__::instance::handler` encoding. Runtime-provided legacy strings
remain accepted at the compiler and test compatibility edges and are immediately
decoded into the typed representation.

This closes the remaining magic-string protocol at the component composition
boundary. Focused compiler, interaction, shell dispatch, `bind:this`, child-event,
and shipped navigation regressions pass; the complete shell test target also
compiles.

## 2026-08-01 — Typed component binding and popover channels

`working tree` · area: component composition

The compiler-to-shell boundary now carries public component values, per-prop
bindings, and `bind:this` in a typed `ComponentCompositionProps` record instead
of encoding binding metadata as `__mesh_binding_*` and `__mesh_bind_this`
attributes. Public prop projection no longer needs to identify and filter
reserved names, and component memo fingerprints include each typed channel.

Promoted-popover wrappers likewise carry a typed, copy-on-write authored-node
flag rather than `_mesh_promoted_popover`. The flag survives component memo
reuse and remains distinct from module-authored attributes. Embedded handler
targets still use the serialized `__mesh_embed__::` form and remain open in the
backlog.

## 2026-08-02 — Keyed embedded component loops

`working tree` · area: component composition

`{#for item in items key={item.id}}` now evaluates the key in each iteration's
locals and carries that stable identity through component occurrence allocation
and memo lookup. Reordering a keyed list therefore retains embedded component
VM state and cached render results. Unkeyed loops deliberately retain their
existing positional identity, and retained native-widget reconciliation is
still the remaining backlog work.

**Verified.** Focused component-parser and shell composition regressions pass,
including a reorder that reuses both keyed component memo entries.

## 2026-08-02 — Keyed loop runtime reconciliation

`working tree` · area: component composition, interaction state

Keyed loop placement now flows into the retained tree's stable runtime node
keys as well as embedded-component instance keys. Native inputs, focus,
hover, checked state, scroll offsets, and other runtime maps therefore follow
their keyed item when a list is reordered. Duplicate key values log a warning
and fall back to positional identity for the colliding occurrence, preventing
an unsafe retained-node alias.

This completes the keyed-list backlog item. The focused shell regressions cover
both component memo reuse and native input state surviving a reorder; the shell
crate also passes `cargo check` in the Nix development environment.

## 2026-08-02 — Layout-transparent control flow

`working tree` · area: component composition, layout

`{#if}` and `{#for}` now build fragments: their selected or iterated children
are inserted directly into the surrounding component/root/element child list.
The compiler no longer injects an unstyleable synthetic `column`, so a loop or
conditional inside a `row` retains row layout and inherits from the real parent.
The selective static-template path continues to use its positional fast path;
dynamic control flow already takes the normal structural rebuild path.

**Verified.** A shell regression proves static, conditional, and loop text are
five direct children of one `row`, with no generated column wrapper. The shell
crate compiles in the Nix development environment.

## 2026-08-02 — Git module installation and provenance locking

`mesh-shell install` now accepts a Git URL with an optional `#ref`. It clones
into a private staging directory, checks out the requested ref, records the
resolved commit in `config/mesh.lock`, validates the installed graph, and then
uses the same capability review and kind-aware profile activation as local-path
installs. Failed clones, ref checkouts, manifest validation, and graph checks
leave no staged module directory behind.

The CLI parser and lock serialization have focused unit coverage. Full CLI
verification is currently blocked by the checkout's missing Nix linker-wrapper
store path; `rustfmt --edition 2024` and `git diff --check` pass.

## 2026-08-02 — Dotted template expressions avoid redundant traversal work

`working tree` · area: component composition and template evaluation

Dotted expressions now split their path without allocating a temporary vector,
look up the root directly, and traverse it by reference. Stores that cannot
lend values still clone only the resolved leaf rather than every intermediate
JSON object. A focused regression asserts that the borrowed path performs just
the root lookup, preserving the existing no-owned-clone behavior.

`rustfmt --edition 2024` on the changed compiler files and `git diff --check`
pass. `cargo test -p mesh-core-frontend eval_expr_` is blocked before tests run:
the configured Rust linker wrapper references a missing Nix store path. No
runtime performance claim is recorded until the release benchmark can run.

## 2026-08-03 — Surface reserve and input region are one decision

`working tree` · area: presentation, shell surfaces, input

The shell blocked clicks in a ~200px strip under the navigation bar again. Not
a new mistake — the twentieth instance of one structural one.

**Why it kept coming back.** MESH deliberately configures parent surfaces
*larger* than their content: `tooltip_overlay_extra_for_content` adds 200
logical pixels below a bar (and widens narrow surfaces to 352) so tooltips can
paint outside the content box, and popovers pad their buffer for descendant
shadow/filter overshoot. Those pixels are transparent, so unless the surface's
input region excludes them the compositor routes every click over them to MESH.
Inflation happened in `render_components`; confinement happened much later in
`present_surface_target`, as a separate `update_input_region` call guarded by
the `last_region_state` cache. That is fail-*open*: every way the second half
could not run — a surface entry recreated for a role swap, a window destroyed
on hide and rebuilt with `input_region_rect: None` while the shell still
believed the region was applied, a `update_input_region` dropped because the
backend had no entry yet, a present that returned before the region flushed —
reinstates the dead zone, and none of it is visible in tests because the
testing presentation backend discarded `update_input_region` entirely.

**Fix.** The reserve now travels with the size that created it.
`SurfacePadding { left, top, right, bottom }` is a field of `SurfaceConfig` and
`PopupConfig`, produced together with the inflated size by the single function
`surface_geometry_with_overlay_reserve` (returning `(w, h, padding)`, so a
caller cannot inflate a surface without saying which part is reserve).
`SurfaceEntry` derives the input region from its padding on every commit and
compares against the last committed value, so it is re-published automatically
after any entry recreation. `PresentationEngine::update_input_region` is gone;
there is no setter to forget. Both shell call sites were removed.

**Verified.** `mesh-core-presentation`: 64 pass (5 new, covering trailing
padding, ring padding, zero padding, oversized padding degrading to
whole-surface input rather than an unclickable zero-area region, and
padding participating in the config fingerprint so `apply_config` adopts it).
`mesh-core-shell`: 4 new tests — a bar surface whose input region is the
content rect, the generic invariant that every configured surface's
`size - padding` equals its content size, an uninflated window taking input
everywhere, and a padded child popup confining input to the visible popover.
Mutation check: reverting the padding to `SurfacePadding::default()` fails two
of the four. Failure set for `mesh-core-shell` is byte-identical to `main`
(20 pre-existing failures, unrelated: theme reload, slider drag, real-surface
geometry).

**Also found, not fixed.** `ShellComponent::paint` receives the *padded*
extent as `width`/`height` and falls back to it as the content size when the
surface has not been measured yet, so `last_surface_size` records the reserve
as content for one frame (probed: `content_input=(1920, 201)` on frame 0 of the
shipped navigation bar, correct from frame 1). That no longer reaches the
compositor, but it lays the first frame out against an inflated height. Making
`paint`'s fallback prefer `last_surface_size` pins the old size for direct
callers; the real fix is separate content/padded arguments. In `docs/BACKLOG.md`.

**Pre-existing breakage noticed.** `mesh-core-elements` tests did not compile
(`legacy_deep_clone` missing `loop_identity`); fixed in passing, which reveals
two genuinely failing style tests
(`indexed_theme_defaults_reuse_lowered_declarations_per_revision`,
`shipped_navigation_style_expected_diagnostics_do_not_block_tokens`) and one in
`mesh-core-frontend` (`for_node_wrapper_carries_no_padding_or_gap`). Untouched;
in `docs/BACKLOG.md`.

## 2026-08-03 — Quick settings opened as a 1px sliver

**Symptom.** Hovering the navigation bar's settings button drew a 1px-wide
vertical strip directly under the button instead of the quick-settings panel,
and the strip lingered through the hover-bridge/exit delay after the pointer
left. Measured on the live shell (Hyprland, HDMI-A-2): an opaque `#282828`
column at monitor-local `x=1877`, `y=56..329` — 1 x ~274, i.e. the panel's real
height at a width of one pixel.

**Cause.** `modules/frontend/quick-settings/src/main.mesh` styled its
`<popover>` root `.qs-shell` with `width: fit-content; height: fit-content`.
`fit-content` parses to `Dimension::Content`, which `taffy_dimension` maps to
Taffy `auto`; the synthetic surface root is a column flex container with
`align_items: Stretch` and `width: Px(available)`, so an `auto` cross size is
stretched to the available width. A promoted popover has no available width to
speak of on its first paint: `ActivatePopover` seeds `PopupPlacement.size` at
`(1, 1)`, `declared_or_measured_size()` is `(0, 0)` until something is measured,
and `paint` falls back to the surface extent — so the first layout ran at 1px,
`measure_content_size` reported 1, that became `measured_size`, and every
later frame re-measured against the same 1px. A fixed point at one pixel.
Height was never affected: `auto` on the main axis is content-driven.

**Fix.** Use `width: fit; height: fit`, the same pair the two other shipped
popover roots (`@mesh/theme-selector`, `@mesh/language-popover`) already carry
with a comment saying `<popover>` needs the fit value explicitly. `fit` is
`Dimension::Fit`, which `resolve_fit_sizing` resolves *after* layout by growing
the node to its descendants' union bounding box — so it does not depend on the
available width the popup was measured against.

**Verified.** Live, against the running shell via module hot reload: hover the
gear, the full 300px panel renders (sliders, theme and language chips); move
away, and a pixel diff of the column against the idle frame shows nothing left
at `x=1877`. Before the change the same diff flagged that column across 45% of
rows in `y=0..600`.

**Not fixed.** (1) `fit-content` silently degrading to stretch is a trap any
module can fall into — the layout report already records
`"content dimension mapped through Taffy measurement"` for it, but nothing
surfaces that as an authoring diagnostic. (2) Independently, a content-measured
popup's first layout runs against the `(1, 1)` placeholder rather than anything
resembling its output; `fit` survives that, ordinary intrinsic sizing does not.
Both in `docs/BACKLOG.md`. (3) The panel header renders "Quick" where the
template says "Quick Settings" — separate, unrelated to popup sizing, not
investigated.

## 2026-08-03 — `paint` takes the content extent and the padded extent separately

`working tree` · area: shell surfaces, layout

Follow-on to the reserve/input-region record above, which left this behind as
"also found, not fixed".

**The defect.** `ShellComponent::paint(theme, width, height, buffer, scale)`
took one extent that meant two things. The caller passed the *padded* surface —
content plus the tooltip overlay reserve — because that is the buffer being
painted into. But `paint` also used it as the fallback content size whenever
`requested_layout_size()` was still `(0, 0)`, which is the first frame of every
surface, before anything has been measured. So the shipped 1920x56 navigation
bar laid its first frame out at 1920x256 and recorded 1920x256 in
`last_surface_size`. Everything downstream that reads content size — CSS
percentage resolution, `content_input_size`, the shell's own size cache — saw
the reserve as content for that frame.

**Fix.** One argument that carries both halves rather than two positional
`u32`s that can be handed the wrong number:

```rust
pub struct SurfaceExtent { pub content: (u32, u32), pub padded: (u32, u32) }
SurfaceExtent::unpadded(w, h)          // buffer is exactly the content
SurfaceExtent::padded(content, padded) // padded clamped up to content
```

`paint`'s unmeasured fallback reads `extent.content_*`; the paint buffer reads
`extent.padded_*`. `render.rs` builds the extent from the same
`surface_geometry_with_overlay_reserve` that produces the `SurfacePadding`, so
content, padded size, and input region now all come from one call. Popups pass
`unpadded`: their `placement.size` is already the measured content.

Same shape as the `SurfacePadding` change it follows: the reserve is not a
separate number a caller can forget to account for, it is a named field of the
value that carries the size.

**Verified.** `cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib`: 614 passed / 21 failed /
125 ignored across three runs, and the failing set is identical name-for-name to
`main` measured in the same checkout by stashing this work (theme reload, slider
drag, real-surface geometry, debug snapshots — all in the red baseline already
listed in `docs/BACKLOG.md`). One earlier run reported 22 failed with the same
21 names, so something in that baseline is also intermittent; not chased here.
New regression
`first_paint_lays_out_against_content_extent_not_the_tooltip_reserve` paints an
unmeasured component at `SurfaceExtent::padded((1920, 56), (1920, 256))` and
asserts both the recorded content size and that a `height: 100%` root resolves
to 56, not 256. Mutation check: pointing the fallback back at `padded_*` fails
it with `left: Some((1920, 256))` — the exact figure probed on the live shell
yesterday. `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo fmt --all`, and
`git diff --check` are clean.

379 call sites were rewritten mechanically (`.paint(&theme, w, h, …)` →
`.paint(&theme, SurfaceExtent::unpadded(w, h), …)`); all but one are tests, and
the one production caller is `Shell::render_components`.

## 2026-08-07 — Window resize repaints per configure

`working tree` · area: Shell render loop / xdg_toplevel

Interactively resizing a `role: "window"` surface stretched the whole surface —
text, borders, background fills — until the drag was released. Cause: the
compositor grabs the pointer during an interactive resize, so the window gets no
input events, and an `xdg_toplevel` configure carrying a new size did not dirty
the component. `render_components` therefore skipped the surface at its
`wants_render()` gate, no new buffer was committed, and the compositor scaled the
last committed buffer to the box it was dragging. `window_configured_size` was
only read *behind* that gate (`render/mod.rs`), so it could never open it.

Fix: deliver the compositor-configured size ahead of the gate, next to the
existing `surface_window_states_changed` delivery, so a resize configure runs
`observe_surface_size` → `STYLE|LAYOUT|PAINT|METRICS` invalidation and the
surface re-measures and re-presents once per configure. Nothing in the
presentation layer changed; the viewport destination was already consistent with
the buffer it was attached to.

Verification: `cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib` → 642 passed / 6 failed /
124 ignored, byte-identical failure set to the same run with the change stashed
(pre-existing hover, raster, navigation-focus, profiling-baseline, and two debug
tests). `cargo check -p mesh-core-shell` clean apart from pre-existing warnings.

`working tree` · area: component settings contract and retained renderer cleanup

Frontend component runtimes no longer receive the resolved module settings

`mesh.settings` service contract. Settings reloads no longer recreate the

The old PixelBuffer icon/glyph entrypoints and recursive widget painter helpers
are now test-only compatibility code; production rendering uses the retained

emits the corresponding dead-code warnings. The authoring scan's remaining

Verified: `mesh-core-render` 201 passed / 0 failed / 37 ignored;

## 2026-08-08 — Runtime-generation index for component memoization

`working tree` · area: shell component memoization

Component memo entries no longer scan every runtime and retain a generation
tuple for each descendant. A parent/child runtime index now stores an
aggregate subtree stamp; runtime creation, handler/service/locale/settings
updates, render hooks, catalog pruning, and source reloads update the affected
path or rebuild the index. A changed descendant still invalidates every
enclosing memo entry, while a new or removed runtime changes the same aggregate
stamp.

**Measured.** Release under `nix develop` (rustc/cargo 1.94.0), three runs of
100,000 probes over a 4,096-descendant runtime shape. The legacy descendant
scan took 75.405–79.928ms; aggregate index lookup took 1.340–1.655ms, a
45.56–59.63x microbenchmark improvement. The checked
`runtime_generation_index_beats_descendant_scan` gate passed all three runs.
This is an isolated lookup benchmark; end-to-end frame impact is not claimed.

**Verified.** The runtime-index correctness test and all 11 active
component-memo integration tests pass. `cargo check -p mesh-core-shell`,
`cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` pass; existing shell
warnings remain unchanged.

## 2026-08-08 — Package lifecycle operations moved into `mesh.packages`

The package-service backlog item is complete. `mesh.packages.install` and
`mesh.packages.uninstall` are now declared typed core-provider methods and map
through the generic capability-gated service-command path into `CoreRequest`.
The shell implementation stages local directories or Git sources, validates
manifest capabilities, updates the installed graph and `mesh.lock`, manages
frontend/composition profile activation, and refuses unsafe removal of active
profile references or providers unless forced. The existing CLI remains a
client-compatible path for the same on-disk graph and lock semantics.

Verification: `cargo check -p mesh-core-module` passed; shell library
compilation passed with `PKG_CONFIG_PATH=/tmp/mesh-pkgconfig` and
`RUSTFLAGS='-C link-arg=-fuse-ld=bfd'` because the environment lacks the native
`xkbcommon`, freetype, and fontconfig libraries required to link shell tests.
`cargo fmt --all -- --check` passed. No performance claim or benchmark applies.

Follow-up: `cargo test -p mesh-core-module` passed (195 passed, 3 ignored).

## 2026-08-08 — Two-level component customization direction

`working tree` · area: module system and component composition

The implementation plan for non-coder visual customization now uses named,
author-declared slots containing ordered public component placements. It reuses
versioned extension-point contracts and alternate-root isolation rather than
serializing arbitrary elements, scripts, handlers, or styles into a second UI
language. Precise changes remain source edits to `.mesh`, Luau, and CSS.

The planned visual editor is an ordinary frontend module over a generic
`mesh.composition` interface. Core supplies catalog introspection, validation,
sparse profile persistence, transactional activation, and mounting only. Read
and control receive narrower composition capabilities; installing code remains
behind the stronger existing package capability. The full phased design and
acceptance gates are in
[`../todos/pending/2026-08-08-node-slot-customization.md`](../todos/pending/2026-08-08-node-slot-customization.md).

## 2026-08-08 — Author-declared node slots and replaceable composition editor

The two-level customization design shipped. Component authors can declare
stable named customizable slots backed by existing versioned extension points
and author defaults. Profile schema 3 stores only ordered public component
placements, stable placement ids, and literal exposed-prop overrides; arbitrary
elements, scripts, handlers, CSS, and surface policy remain source-level.

Core now validates and resolves placement modules through the installed graph,
mounts alternate roots under stable slot identities, and exposes the generic
capability-gated `mesh.composition` state/apply/reset provider with optimistic
generation checks. The shell contains no editor-specific or navbar-specific
branch. The shipped navigation bar uses start, center, and end slots, and the
ordinary `@mesh/composition-editor` frontend module supplies the visual UI and
generated scalar prop controls.

Verification: `mesh-core-module` passed 196 tests with 3 ignored; focused slot
parser/compiler, composition request, shipped frontend compilation, LSP, and
real navigation layout/raster/hover/keyboard/pointer tests passed. The complete
shell library run reached 655 passed and 125 ignored, with only three unrelated
existing debug/theme fixture failures. Formatting and diff checks passed. No
performance claim is made.

Follow-up verification after adding the schema-2 migration and invalid
placement regressions: `mesh-core-module` now passes 198 tests with 3 ignored.

## 2026-08-08 — Settings surface open/close lifecycle

The settings launcher no longer races its embedded Quick Settings popup with
the full Settings surface: opening Settings blocks a second hover popup until
the launcher is left, while later hovers still work. Shell visibility requests
are idempotent, so duplicate `ShowSurface` requests cannot restart entrance
animation and duplicate `HideSurface` requests cannot force an in-progress
exit to complete early.

Verification: the 18-test navigation interaction suite, 8 window-role tests,
15 surface-layout tests, and focused Settings close/re-show regressions passed.
No performance claim applies.

## 2026-08-20 — End-to-end audit of Luau runtime and sandbox

The Section 11 review covered `mesh-core-runtime`, `mesh-core-scripting`, and
`mesh-core-backend`, including sandbox policy, per-context isolation, host
capabilities, frontend/backend lifecycle, storage, streams, polling, commands,
event publication, cleanup, and diagnostics. The process-tree review was
supplemented by independent logic/order, direct code-error, and
security/resource reviews. No production code was changed.

The audit found disconnected sandbox enforcement, shared service globals,
capability bypasses, abort-based cleanup gaps, generation/readiness races,
unbounded or blocking process/queue paths, weak durable-storage defaults, and
non-transactional command/event dispatch. The complete findings and the
unconstrained feature direction are recorded in
[`sections/11-luau-runtime-and-sandbox/improvements.md`](sections/11-luau-runtime-and-sandbox/improvements.md).

Verification: `mesh-core-scripting` 181 passed / 27 ignored;
`mesh-core-backend` 25 passed; the filtered `mesh-core-shell service` suite
102 passed / 14 ignored; and `cargo check` passed for the three Section 11
packages, all inside `nix develop`. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-20 — End-to-end audit of rendering and paint

The Section 12 review covered the retained render-object path, display-list
entries and subtree spans, dirty-to-damage selection, partial replay, software/
Skia lowering, transforms, clipping, blur/layers, text and icon caches, scaling,
diagnostics, SHM copy, and Wayland presentation. The requested agent
orchestration interface was unavailable in this session, so the review used
independent manual process-tree, logic/order, direct code-error, and
cache/resource/diagnostic passes. No production code was changed.

The audit found incomplete affine transform propagation, asymmetric border
lowering, per-primitive rather than node-group opacity/blend compositing,
order/topology generations that can leave presentation regions stale, dirty
fingerprint gaps, incomplete text shaping/resource invalidation, an implicit
compositor-only backdrop policy, independent fractional-scale rounding, and
release metrics that discard batch data. The concrete feature direction,
implementation dependencies, and regression matrix are recorded in
[`sections/12-rendering-and-paint/improvements.md`](sections/12-rendering-and-paint/improvements.md).

Verification: `mesh-core-render --lib` 201 passed / 0 failed / 37 ignored;
`paint_perf_scenarios` 6 passed / 0 failed; and `cargo check -p
mesh-core-render` passed under `nix develop`. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-20 — End-to-end audit of rendering and paint

`working tree` · area: `mesh-core-render` and the render-to-presentation seam

The requested Luna xhigh process-tree pass mapped widget/style/layout state
through render-object invalidation, retained display-list construction and
damage selection, paint/resource paths, PixelBuffer output, SHM upload, and
Wayland frame feedback. Independent logic/order, direct code-error, and
render-boundary/resource reviews were launched. No production code was
changed.

The audit found a safe API that can invalidate a live Skia pixel pointer,
transforms that are not lowered consistently through descendants and related
geometry, incomplete retained invalidation fingerprints, opacity/border/radius
paint loss, inconsistent physical text scaling, stale or incomplete resource
caches, blocking/unbounded paint-path resource work, partial-present and blur
contract gaps, unstable equal-z ordering, and weak layer/diagnostic/backend
contracts. The full process tree, ranked findings, unconstrained render-frame
direction, implementation order, and regression matrix are in
[`sections/12-rendering-and-paint/improvements.md`](sections/12-rendering-and-paint/improvements.md).

Verification: `mesh-core-render --lib` passed 201 tests with 37 ignored;
the six focused paint-performance scenarios passed; and
`cargo check -p mesh-core-render -p mesh-core-presentation` passed, all inside
`nix develop`. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-20 — Section 12 audit record correction

The immediately preceding Section 12 entry was written during delegated
review completion and described the passes as manual. Four Luna xhigh review
passes were in fact used: process-tree, logic/order, direct code-error, and
render-boundary/resource. Their returned findings are integrated in the linked
report; this addendum preserves the append-only history without rewriting the
earlier entry.

## 2026-08-20 — End-to-end audit of surface policy and configuration

`working tree` · area: `mesh-core-surface-config`, shell surface lifecycle,
and the presentation `SurfaceConfig` seam

The Section 13 process tree traced manifest declarations and sparse settings
through validation, precedence, props resolution, CSS measurement, shell
configure/reload/role transitions, Wayland role selection, and compositor
feedback. Four Luna xhigh passes were launched for process mapping, logic/order,
direct code errors, and schema/lifecycle boundaries; their workers timed out,
so the final report uses the local source audit and focused verification as the
evidence base. No production code was changed.

The audit found that settings-driven role changes bypass the author
`promotable` guard, `promotable` itself is accepted but ignored by user
resolution, invalid manifest enums silently default, presentation fingerprints
omit blur and window decorations, settings role reloads bypass the explicit
transactional transition path, inert field diagnostics/ejection are
inconsistent, and zero/measurement/physical geometry is split across layers.
The complete findings, unconstrained policy-compiler direction, implementation
order, and regression matrix are in
[`sections/13-surface-policy-and-configuration/improvements.md`](sections/13-surface-policy-and-configuration/improvements.md).

Verification: `mesh-core-surface-config` 28 tests passed;
shell surface-layout tests 17 passed; shell window-role tests 8 passed; and
the surface-config/shell/presentation cargo check passed under `nix develop`.
No performance claim is made.
