## 2026-08-20 — Per-widget promotion

`working tree` · area: shell, frontend runtime, and presentation

Embedded widgets can now be promoted independently of their parent surface.
The shell keeps the retained node and shared component VM, removes the widget
from parent flow, reconciles it as an independent `xdg_toplevel`, and routes
child input and paint through the same runtime. Luau shell operations expose
promote, demote, and explicit role selection by owning surface and retained node
key; closing the window demotes it back into the parent.

Verification: focused child-surface tests passed (20 passed, 1 ignored),
scripting operation tests passed (6), presentation tests passed (64 passed,
12 ignored), and `cargo check --workspace` passed. The broad shell suite passed
671 tests with the six known clean-baseline fixture failures; workspace library
testing stopped at eight known `mesh-core-elements` theme fixture failures.

## 2026-08-20 — Validate module namespaces through owner schemas

`working tree` · area: settings store, installed module graph, and shell reloads

`SettingsStore` now stages owner-registered namespace schemas and commits their
validated runtime projection atomically. The installed graph contributes one
complete schema snapshot per module, combining core surface/resource fields with
module-owned settings declarations before graph consumers read settings. Invalid
registered values produce path-aware diagnostics and are omitted from runtime
reads, while raw unknown or uninstalled namespaces remain in the persisted
document for lifecycle repair. Graph activation, profile candidates, package
changes, and settings reloads all replace schema snapshots transactionally.

Verification: `cargo test -p mesh-core-config --lib` passed 37 tests;
`cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib graph:: -- --test-threads=1` passed 4;
`cargo check -p mesh-tools-lsp -p mesh-tools-cli` passed; and the broad shell
library suite passed 672 tests with 6 unrelated baseline failures and 130
ignored. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check` passed. No
performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-20 — Make settings writes unique and crash-durable

`working tree` · area: settings persistence

`SettingsStore::save` now creates a unique same-directory temporary file with
`create_new`, owner-only Unix permissions, and cleanup on failures. It flushes
the complete file before atomic rename and syncs the containing directory after
the rename, while newly created parent directories use owner-only permissions.
Concurrent writers therefore cannot collide on a fixed `.json.tmp` path and
the final persisted JSON is a complete document after a crash-consistent commit.

Verification: `cargo test -p mesh-core-config --lib` passed 39 tests;
`cargo check -p mesh-core-shell -p mesh-tools-cli -p mesh-tools-lsp` passed;
the broad shell library suite passed 672 tests with 6 unrelated baseline
failures and 130 ignored; `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check`
passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-20 — Remove leading dots from root diagnostics

`working tree` · area: settings diagnostics

`SettingsDiagnostic::location` now joins the namespace and key path only when
both are present. Root-level settings validation therefore reports
`unknown_root_key` instead of `.unknown_root_key`, while namespace and
namespace-root locations retain their existing forms. Formatter and settings
regression coverage exercises the actual misspelled-root path.

Verification: `cargo test -p mesh-core-config --lib` passed 40 tests;
`cargo check -p mesh-core-shell -p mesh-tools-cli -p mesh-tools-lsp` passed;
the broad shell library suite passed 672 tests with 6 unrelated baseline
failures and 130 ignored; `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check`
passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-20 — Treat empty XDG directories as absent

`working tree` · area: XDG path resolution

Default config lookup, host icon discovery, and the shell IPC socket resolver
now ignore empty `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_DATA_HOME`, `XDG_DATA_DIRS`, and
`XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` values before constructing paths. They therefore use the
existing `$HOME` or `/tmp` fallbacks instead of turning an empty environment
value into a relative path such as `mesh.sock` or `icons`.

Verification: `cargo test -p mesh-core-config --lib` passed 41 tests;
`cargo test -p mesh-core-resources --lib` passed 2; the focused shell XDG test
passed; `cargo check -p mesh-core-shell -p mesh-tools-cli -p mesh-tools-lsp`
passed; and the broad shell library suite passed 673 tests with 6 unrelated
baseline failures and 130 ignored. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and
`git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-20 — Report allocation profiling only when installed

`working tree` · area: allocation profiling availability

The allocation profiler no longer treats the `allocation-profiling` crate
feature as proof that the embedding binary installed the counting allocator.
The wrapper marks the runtime availability flag when it handles an allocator
operation, so profiling snapshots advertise allocation counters only for an
actual counting-allocator build; feature-enabled binaries that use the normal
allocator now report the mode as unavailable.

Verification: `mesh-core-debug` feature and integration tests passed; the
feature-enabled shell allocation regression passed; `cargo check -p
mesh-tools-cli --features allocation-profiling` passed; the broad shell
library suite passed 674 tests with the 6 existing unrelated baseline failures
and 130 ignored; and `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check` passed.
No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-20 — Enforce package identity and path safety

`mesh-core-module` now centralizes typed module-ID and contained-relative-path
validation. Package reads and writes reject symlinked files or parents, module
trees reject symlinks and special files before installation or digesting, and
root graph, lock, profile, installer, update, uninstall, and rollback paths
resolve through the validated module root. Recursive module discovery and
source scanners use canonical visited sets and skip links, preventing cycles or
content outside the module root from being returned.

Verification: `cargo test -p mesh-core-module package::` passed 173 tests with
3 ignored; `cargo test -p mesh-tools-cli` passed 9 tests; `cargo check -p
mesh-core-shell -p mesh-tools-cli` passed; `cargo fmt --all` and `git diff
--check` passed. The broad `cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib` run passed
673 tests with 130 ignored and reproduced 6 existing unrelated failures in
real-surface, backend-lifecycle, and debug tests. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-21 — Share package mutations through a recoverable transaction engine

`working tree` · area: package installation and lifecycle mutations

`mesh-core-module::package::PackageTransaction` now provides the sole durable
package mutation boundary used by the CLI and shell. Install, update, uninstall,
and rollback acquire one advisory OS lock, recover any unfinished journal, stage
candidate trees outside the live module directory, snapshot root/lock/history/
profile/module state before mutation, and either commit the journal or restore
the complete snapshot. Updates materialize Git revisions in the staging area;
rollback restores the exact selected lock generation and removes modules absent
from that generation. Lock persistence also has an exact-generation path for
rollback.

Verification: the transaction tests passed 3/3; `cargo test -p
mesh-core-module package:: --lib` passed 176 tests with 3 ignored; `cargo test
-p mesh-tools-cli` passed 9 tests; `cargo check -p mesh-tools-cli -p
mesh-core-shell`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed.
The workspace run completed with the existing unrelated failures in
`mesh-core-elements` (8 style-fixture/theme expectations), `mesh-core-shell` (6
real-surface/backend/debug tests), and `mesh-tools-lsp` (the shipped root
manifest's existing `capabilityApprovals` warning); package and CLI targets
passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-21 — Enforce module and interface compatibility before activation

`working tree` · area: module dependency resolution and provider selection

Required module edges now resolve through the installed closure and block only
the requesting module when a dependency is missing, version-incompatible, or
blocked transitively. Optional module edges remain active as degraded metadata
with explicit diagnostics. Manifest validation rejects malformed module,
interface, and provider version ranges before graph resolution. Interface
consumers and providers are matched against declared contract and offered
versions before providers or contributions are activated; incompatible
required consumers are blocked while optional consumers degrade.

Verification: `cargo test -p mesh-core-module package::resolution --lib` passed

`cargo test -p mesh-core-module --lib` passed 209 tests with 3 ignored;
`cargo test -p mesh-core-service --lib` passed 30 with 5 ignored; formatting and
`git diff --check` passed. `cargo test --workspace --lib` reached the existing
unrelated `mesh-core-elements` style-fixture/theme failures (8 failed); the
affected module and service crates passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-21 — Preserve composition and lock provenance

`ShellProfile` now accepts sparse inherited-root overrides, retaining the
instance-key module identity while applying active-state deltas. Composition
resolution validates and carries the selected version, includes composition and
`extends` modules in the activation dependency closure, and projects only
active, non-orphaned root node slots into the runtime graph. Install and
uninstall lock writes now record selected composition metadata and rebuild
`requestedBy` from the installed required-dependency closure.

## 2026-08-21 — Plan updates from fully staged candidate graphs

`working tree` · area: module update planning and candidate activation

The CLI update path now resolves Git refs with read-only `ls-remote`/`rev-parse`
operations, materializes changed and unchanged module trees under the package
transaction workspace, copies the active root/profile inputs there, and builds
the complete candidate graph before replacing any live checkout. Candidate
manifests therefore include external interface contract files and the graph
validates required dependency/provider/profile closure. Capability policy is
resolved against the staged graph and persisted approvals; newly required
grants remain an explicit refusal while successful modules retain their exact
effective grant decisions in the plan. Commit consumes the already-staged trees
instead of checking them out again.

passed 8 tests, including the external-contract and live-tree-preservation
regression; `cargo test -p mesh-core-module package:: --lib` passed 187 tests

`mesh-core-config` failure:
`settings::tests::a_non_object_document_loads_defaults_and_retains_a_root_diagnostic`
failed because its temporary test path was missing. No performance claim is

## 2026-08-21 — Route production manifest loading through the canonical loader

`working tree` · area: module manifest loading and migration diagnostics

The normalized runtime adapter is now named `load_canonical_manifest` and
delegates exclusively to the typed package `module.json` loader. Shell discovery,
LSP indexing, and authoring/test consumers use that adapter; legacy JSON/TOML
readers are test-only, and legacy files or old-shaped `module.json` inputs return
the package loader's explicit migration diagnostics instead of runnable
compatibility manifests.

`mesh-core-module` library passed 216 tests with 3 ignored; the LSP library
passed 84 tests; shell discovery passed 1 test with 1 ignored; workspace

## 2026-08-21 — Make module lifecycle and health authoritative

`working tree` · area: module graph, runtime lifecycle, and service availability

Module instances now retain independent graph-derived and runtime-derived health
records, with authoritative effective health exposed through lifecycle state,
debug inspection, recovery, unload, and quarantine transitions. Backend status
updates drive module lifecycle and health, restart deadlines carry provider and
generation identity so stale wakeups cannot resurrect superseded runtimes, and
terminal provider failures update the cached service state and deliver both an
unavailable update and a replayable `health` interface event. Candidate runtime
health remains private until activation, while newly mounted consumers replay the
latest health transition.

Verification: `cargo test -p mesh-core-module --lib` passed 216 tests with 3
ignored; `cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib --no-fail-fast` passed 677 tests
with 130 ignored and retained 6 existing baseline failures; `cargo check
-p mesh-core-shell`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, and `git diff --check` passed.
The workspace library run retained the same shell failures plus existing
`mesh-core-elements` failures. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-21 — Align lock schema and graph identity metadata

`working tree` · area: module installation locks and graph validation

`mesh.lock` is now authoritative at schema v3, with an explicit v2 migration,
deterministic direct dependency requirements, reverse requester metadata, active
composition provenance, and source/revision/content-digest provenance populated
by install, uninstall, and update paths. Duplicate active standalone interfaces,
extension-point declarations, and same-module contribution identities are
rejected using sorted deterministic validation before activation.

## 2026-08-21 — Prevent false exhaustion detection in the standalone Codex loop

`working tree` · area: developer automation

The standalone `codex-until-limit.sh` loop no longer scans arbitrary JSONL
command output for words such as `quota`, which previously stopped a healthy
turn when the backlog mentioned ordinary quota budgets. It now recognizes only
structured exhaustion errors, uses the persisted account-window status, and
reads current context occupancy from the latest rollout token-count record and
runtime-reported model window. Missing context telemetry causes a conservative
fresh session instead of an invalid cumulative-token estimate.

Verification: `bash -n codex-until-limit.sh`, `git diff --check`, and `jq`
fixtures for ordinary quota text versus a genuine usage-limit error passed.

## 2026-08-21 — Compose graph theme layers with provenance

`working tree` · area: theme cascade and graph-selected snapshots

The runtime now prepares a four-layer theme snapshot: recovery defaults,
selected graph theme pack/mode, enabled frontend-owned contributions scoped to
their module IDs, and sparse shell user token overrides. The theme engine keeps
per-value provenance for the winning layer, validates scalar overrides and
module targets, and profile/settings/reload paths use the same graph composer.
Theme settings now accept an optional mode and scalar token map.

Verification: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check` pass. Focused
Cargo tests remain blocked because the local registry cache lacks `sha2` and
network access is unavailable. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-22 — Complete bounded off-thread locale catalog preparation

`working tree` · area: localization catalog lifecycle

The localization catalog backlog item is complete. Graph sources now use
contained, no-follow handles with a 2 MiB UTF-8 bound and regular-file checks;
the shell routes owned source handles and module defaults through a named
worker-thread preparation API. The worker returns an immutable candidate, so
existing last-known-good snapshots remain unchanged until the caller commits
the result atomically. Invalid graph paths now fail preparation instead of
being silently skipped.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-locale` passed
(16 tests), including the worker candidate, traversal, symlink, regular-file,
UTF-8, and size-bound regressions. `git diff --check` passed. Shell checking

`working tree` · area: CSS parsing, theme lowering, and style matching

The restricted selector AST and parser now live in the theme foundation and
are reused by component style lowering. Theme CSS retains supported compound,
class, ID, and state rules as executable declarations; the element resolver
matches global and module-scoped theme rules through the same matcher as local
component styles. Theme declaration scanning now respects quotes/functions and
rejects unterminated comments, malformed values, unmatched syntax, and trailing
CSS instead of silently storing dead rules.

## 2026-08-21 — Persist and fingerprint theme selections

`working tree` · area: theme persistence, reload observation, and revisions

Theme selection now persists the active graph identity and clears stale mode
state through the settings store. Theme watch state carries a stable content
fingerprint, selected mode, and snapshot revision; graph sources fingerprint
through their contained safe handle. Reload checks fingerprints even when file
mtimes are unchanged and keeps polling after selection changes so a new source
directory is observable despite the startup inotify set. Theme service state
publishes the mode, fingerprint, and revision with the rendered selection.

Cargo tests remain blocked because the local registry cache lacks `sha2` and

## 2026-08-21 — Unify theme and component keyframes and token dependencies

uses that result for cached style values and carries computed custom
properties through initial construction, full restyles, targeted restyles, and

stop representation as component keyframes, with inherited custom properties
available while lowering each stop.

## 2026-08-22 — Unify the refreshable host resource catalog

`working tree` · area: XDG icon discovery, host fonts, and resource snapshots

The host resource catalog now owns ordered XDG data, icon, and font roots,
preserves source-declared theme inheritance order, and publishes immutable
refreshes through an `Arc` snapshot without invalidating in-flight consumers.
Icon theme discovery and lookup use those catalog roots instead of rebuilding
their own environment search. The shared catalog font database also supplies
family metadata and file paths, removing the per-pack `fc-match` subprocess.

and font bindings, so both paths use the same host revision and font database.

while preserving precedence.

## 2026-08-22 — Add bounded cancellation to resource preparation

`working tree` · area: resource preparation lifecycle

Resource candidates now carry a cooperative `ResourcePreparationToken` through
module and host asset reads, glyph-map parsing, bundled font validation, font
registry construction, and shell icon-pack preparation. Reads are chunked at
64 KiB and parser units check the token so a superseded candidate can stop
without returning partial bytes or publishing partial icon/font state. The
existing preparation entry points retain their behavior by using a fresh token,
and the live registries still commit only after the complete candidate succeeds.

passed), focused XDG glyph-map tests (7 passed), focused shell discovery tests
(4 passed, 1 ignored), and `cargo check --workspace` passed. The shell-wide
library suite reported 654 passed, 44 existing integration/fixture failures,
and 130 ignored; the workspace library run still stops at the known config
fixture failure caused by top-level `revision` metadata in `settings.json`. No
performance claim is made.

owner so profile switches can cancel superseded work; remove remaining XDG/SVG
decode and raster preparation from shell/render-thread paths before closing the
host-resource backlog item.

## 2026-08-22 — Guard resource candidates by generation

`working tree` · area: resource preparation lifecycle

Added a shared `ResourcePreparationCoordinator` with monotonic generation
leases. Starting a lease cancels the prior token, stale leases cannot retire a
newer generation, and shell resource candidates carry their generation through
to commit. The shell rejects a candidate that is cancelled or superseded
before publishing icon bindings, font state, and the resource snapshot.

Verification: `cargo check -p mesh-core-resources -p mesh-core-shell` passed
with the existing shell warnings; resource tests passed (19); shell discovery
tests passed (4, 1 ignored); formatting and `git diff --check` passed. No
performance claim is made.

**Next.** Move profile-switch resource preparation from the synchronous worker
join into a resumable asynchronous job state so a later profile request can
exercise the coordinator while work is in flight; then remove remaining
XDG/SVG decode and raster preparation from shell/render-thread paths.

## 2026-08-22 — Expose pollable resource preparation jobs

`working tree` · area: resource preparation lifecycle

The shell resource worker is now represented by a `ResourcePreparationJob`
that owns its generation lease and supports cancellation, non-blocking
`try_wait`, blocking `wait`, current-generation checks, and safe retirement.
Dropping an unfinished job requests cooperative cancellation. Existing
discovery and profile callers retain their behavior through `wait`, while a
future profile transition can keep the job and poll it between shell turns.

Verification: resource tests passed (19); shell discovery tests passed (5, 1
ignored), including cancellation/polling without blocking; workspace check,
formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The existing shell warnings remain.
No performance claim is made.

**Next.** Store this job in the pending profile candidate and advance profile
preparation from the shell loop instead of joining synchronously; then remove
remaining XDG/SVG decode and raster preparation from shell/render-thread paths.

## 2026-08-22 — Poll resource preparation during profile switching

`working tree` · area: profile transition resource lifecycle

Profile switching now stores the `ResourcePreparationJob` in a pending
candidate instead of joining it on the shell thread. The runtime polls that
candidate between shell turns and uses a bounded 1 ms wake-up while resource
work is active; once the job completes, frontend/backend candidate preparation
continues through the existing transactional profile commit path. A newer
resource-only profile request cancels and rejects the older candidate, while
profile edits and module/provider changes remain guarded for the full
transition. The generation lease transfers into the prepared snapshot and is
retired on commit, rejection, cancellation, or worker failure, including a
worker-panic regression path.

Verification: resource tests passed (19); shell discovery tests passed (6, 1
ignored), including the non-blocking cancellation and worker-panic lease
cleanup tests; shell profile tests passed (4); `cargo check --workspace`,
formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The existing shell warnings remain.
No performance claim is made.

**Next.** Move the remaining XDG/SVG decode and raster preparation away from
shell/render-thread paths; frontend and backend candidate preparation after
the resource stage remains shell-loop work.

## 2026-08-23 — Move cacheable file-icon raster misses off the paint path

`working tree` · area: frontend render icon resources

Cacheable bitmap and SVG file-icon misses now use a dedicated render worker
with a bounded request queue. The worker performs image decode or SVG parse and
rasterization, then publishes a revision/freshness-keyed `RasterVariant` back
to the render cache. Paint uses the built-in missing-icon placeholder while a
job is pending; the shell scheduler polls worker completion and invalidates
component paint so the real icon appears without blocking the shell/render
thread. Existing external-resource SVG handling remains synchronous because
those files intentionally bypass the stable raster cache.

Verification: render icon tests passed (15, 2 ignored), including the worker
cache handoff; resource tests passed (19); shell discovery tests passed (6, 1
ignored); `cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and `git diff --check`
passed. The focused real-surface navigation raster test remains blocked by the
known repository fixture layout failure (`right control cluster` exceeds its
1280px test surface), unrelated to the icon worker. Existing shell warnings
remain. No performance claim is made.

**Next.** Extend preparation to external-resource SVGs and font-glyph raster
requests, then continue moving remaining resource decode work away from
shell/render-thread paths.

## 2026-08-23 — Move font-glyph preparation off the paint path

`working tree` · area: frontend render font resources

Font-pack glyph misses now use a bounded `mesh-glyph-raster` worker for font
byte loading and swash alpha-mask rasterization. Results are handed back into
the revision/fingerprint-keyed glyph cache; the render thread still owns Skia
A8 image creation/upload and the final canvas draw. A pending miss uses the
built-in missing-icon fallback, while the shell polls both icon and glyph
queues and requests repaint when either completes. Idle scheduler polling no
longer initializes the icon worker.

Verification: glyph tests passed (4); render icon tests passed (15, 2
ignored); resource tests passed (19); shell discovery tests passed (6, 1
ignored); `cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
The focused real-surface navigation raster test remains blocked by the known
repository fixture layout failure (`right control cluster` exceeds its 1280px
test surface), unrelated to this worker handoff. Existing shell warnings
remain. No performance claim is made.

**Next.** Move external-resource SVG preparation away from the
shell/render-thread paths, then continue with frontend/backend candidate
preparation after the resource stage.

## 2026-08-23 — Move external-resource SVG preparation off the paint path

`working tree` · area: frontend render icon resources

External-resource SVGs now use the bounded icon worker for SVG text reads,
external-reference classification, linked-resource loading, and rasterization.
Cacheable SVGs continue to publish into the revision/freshness-keyed raster
cache; externally referenced SVGs publish one-shot variants through a separate
ready-result handoff and never become persistent cache entries. The paint path
keeps the existing missing-icon placeholder while either kind of worker result
is pending. Opaque-region lookup no longer rereads SVG text just to decide
whether a cached variant may be used.

Verification: render library tests passed (205, 37 ignored), including the
external-SVG worker handoff; resource tests passed (19); shell discovery tests
passed (6, 1 ignored); `cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and `git
diff --check` passed. The focused real-surface navigation raster test remains
blocked by the known repository fixture layout failure (`right control
cluster` reaches x=1564 on a 1280px surface), unrelated to this worker path.
Existing shell warnings remain. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** The bounded queues still use revision/freshness keys rather
than a shared generation-aware broker with explicit cancellation; linked-file
changes that occur during an in-flight one-shot render are conservatively
handled on the next request rather than invalidated by a dedicated watcher.

**Next.** Unify icon/glyph preparation under the resource broker’s generation
and cancellation contract, then continue frontend/backend candidate
preparation after the resource stage.

## 2026-08-23 — Cancel stale render resource generations

`working tree` · area: frontend render resource lifecycle

Icon and font-glyph workers now use the committed resource revision as a
cooperative cancellation generation. They skip queued work when its snapshot
is already stale, check again after preparation, and the render handoff rejects
results whose generation no longer matches the active snapshot. Stale
one-shot external-SVG results are purged alongside the persistent handoff;
current-generation cache and placeholder behavior is unchanged.

Verification: render library tests passed (206, 37 ignored), including the
generation-gate regression; resource tests passed (19); shell discovery tests
passed (6, 1 ignored); `cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and `git
diff --check` passed. Existing shell warnings remain. No performance claim is
made.

**Left open.** This is a cooperative revision gate, not yet the shared
generation-aware resource broker with explicit queue cancellation, byte
budgets, or linked-file watcher invalidation. The focused real-surface
navigation raster test remains blocked by the known fixture layout failure
(`right control cluster` reaches x=1564 on a 1280px surface).

**Next.** Extract the shared icon/glyph queues into the resource broker’s
cancellation and bounded-budget contract, then continue frontend/backend
candidate preparation after the resource stage.

## 2026-08-23 — Share the render resource broker and invalidate linked SVGs

`working tree` · area: frontend render resource lifecycle

Icon bitmap/SVG and font-glyph preparation now enter one generation-aware
`ResourceBroker`. It owns the single bounded worker queue, shared 32 MiB byte
admission budget, resource-revision cancellation token, and cancellation
callbacks. Typed icon and glyph result queues remain separate so cache and
handoff behavior stays explicit; reservations are held until the typed result
is consumed or discarded. If the broker worker cannot be created, the existing
synchronous fallback remains available.

External-resource SVG handoffs now retain fingerprints for resolvable linked
files. A changed link invalidates a ready one-shot result during polling or
consumption, and the next request rasterizes the updated dependency set.

Verification: render library tests passed (210, 37 ignored), including the
broker budget, worker-panic recovery, and linked-file invalidation regressions;
resource tests passed (21); `cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and `git
diff --check` passed.
Existing shell warnings remain. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Text layout/glyph-atlas, `PixelBuffer`, and SHM allocations do
not yet use this broker's byte/dimension accounting. Frontend/backend
candidate preparation after the resource stage remains outside this increment.

## 2026-08-23 — Make surface configuration transactional

`working tree` · area: Wayland platform and presentation

Surface configuration now returns `Result<(), PresentationError>` through the
Wayland, dev-window, and testing presentation backends. Live role replacement
creates the replacement Wayland role before destroying the last-known-good role,
so a protocol or surface-creation error leaves the existing surface available
for retry. Parent and child shell render paths propagate typed configure
failures and update their accepted-config caches only after the backend accepts
the request; keyboard-mode reconciliation follows the same cache rule.

Verification: presentation library tests passed (69, 12 ignored); shell
surface-layout tests passed (18); shell window-role tests passed (8);
`cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The broad
`cargo test --workspace --lib` run remains blocked by the pre-existing
repository-settings fixture, which rejects the `revision` key in
`config/settings.json`. Existing shell warnings remain. No performance claim is
made.

**Left open.** Typed lost outcomes and the idempotent teardown supervisor for
close, dismiss, parent destruction, role replacement, and connection loss are
not part of this increment.

## 2026-08-23 — Unify compositor close and popup dismissal teardown

`working tree` · area: Wayland platform and presentation

Wayland layer `closed` and popup `done` callbacks now remove surfaces through a
shared idempotent teardown helper that releases focus-grab state, auxiliary
viewport/fractional-scale/blur objects, and surface identity indexes. Parent
close tears down popup descendants child-first and publishes typed
`SurfaceLifecycleEvent` values. The shell consumes the lifecycle stream,
invalidates accepted config/size/region state for closed parent targets, keeps
their components mounted, and retries a replacement on the next render. The
testing backend now models lifecycle removal and suppresses duplicate teardown
records per surface generation.

Verification: presentation library tests passed (70, 12 ignored); shell
surface-layout tests passed (19); child-surface tests passed (17); popover tests
passed (14); window-role tests passed (8); the frontend deactivation teardown
regression passed (1); `cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and
`git diff --check` passed. The broad `cargo test --workspace --lib` run remains
blocked by the pre-existing repository-settings fixture, which rejects the
`revision` key in `config/settings.json` after 43 config tests passed. Existing
shell warnings remain. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** Connection-loss lifecycle events, per-seat input cancellation,
and the full teardown supervisor across every external failure path remain for
the next presentation increment.

## 2026-08-23 — Render text-input-v3 composition decoration

`working tree` · area: frontend render and shell text input

The component annotation boundary now projects the compositor's validated
preedit byte range and cursor range as private input attributes. The retained
display-list input payload preserves those UTF-8 boundaries and includes them
in its paint signature, so composition-cursor-only updates rebuild the input
paint. Unmasked focused inputs draw a composition underline and place the caret
at the compositor's preedit cursor; invalid ranges and masked password input
fall back to ordinary input painting.

Verification: the display-list preedit payload test passed; the painter
composition/caret regression passed; shell preedit and text-input transaction
tests passed (1 and 4 tests respectively); the render library passed (218
active, 37 ignored); `cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and `git diff
--check` passed. The broad shell library run compiled and reached 667 passed,
47 existing fixture/runtime failures, and 130 ignored tests; none of the
failures were text-input tests. Existing shell warnings remain. No performance
claim is made.

**Left open.** A live compositor/lifecycle matrix and connection recreation
coverage for text-input-v3 remain outside this increment.

## 2026-08-23 — Model text-input-v3 state publication in the testing backend

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation text-input contract

Text-input state validation is now shared across the Wayland, testing, and
developer presentation entrypoints. The testing backend retains the current
focused surface and UTF-8-byte-indexed surrounding-text snapshot, exposes it
to deterministic tests, and supports an explicit clear transition. Invalid
scalar-boundary offsets and malformed focused/cleared combinations are rejected
before storage, matching the Wayland message contract instead of being silently
ignored by the test backend.

Verification: presentation tests passed (95 active, 11 ignored), including
publication/clear and invalid-boundary regressions; `cargo check --workspace`,
formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. Existing shell warnings remain. No
performance claim is made.

**Left open.** A live compositor/lifecycle matrix and connection recreation
coverage for text-input-v3 remain outside this increment.

## 2026-08-23 — Cancel stale text-input-v3 transactions during teardown

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation input lifecycle

Surface teardown now clears each matching seat's entered text-input surface,
pending preedit/commit/delete transaction, enabled marker, and applied
surrounding-text snapshot before removing the compositor surface identity. This
prevents a late `zwp_text_input_v3.done` from routing stale text to a recreated
surface with the same id. The deterministic testing backend now mirrors the
same boundary by dropping queued events for closed, dismissed, explicitly
destroyed, or connection-lost surfaces while preserving events for other
surfaces.

Verification: presentation tests passed (93 active, 11 ignored), including the
text-input teardown and destroyed-surface queue regressions; shell surface
lifecycle tests passed (20) and shell text-input tests passed (4); `cargo check
--workspace`, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. Existing shell
warnings remain. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** A live compositor/lifecycle matrix and connection recreation
coverage for text-input-v3 remain outside this increment.

## 2026-08-23 — Align deterministic popup identity validation

`working tree` · area: presentation lifecycle simulation

The deterministic presentation backend now applies the live popup contract
before accepting retained state. It rejects unsupported popup promotion,
regular-surface id collisions, missing or nested popup parents, and reparenting
of an existing popup. Rejected requests leave the existing surface or popup
configuration untouched, so the simulator can exercise the same identity
boundaries as the Wayland backend.

presentation test could not build in this environment because the Nix daemon
is unavailable, the direct toolchain lacks `xkbcommon`, and the pinned Skia
binary cannot be downloaded with network access disabled.

and a successful environment-backed presentation test run remain separate
follow-ups.

## 2026-08-23 — Mirror parent-close popup teardown in the simulator

`working tree` · area: presentation lifecycle simulation

The deterministic testing backend now treats a compositor-closed parent like
the live Wayland path: popup descendants are removed child-first, stale input
and pending state are cleared, and `Dismissed` events precede the parent's
`Closed` event. Child ids are sorted so lifecycle delivery remains repeatable,
and repeated close notifications stay idempotent.

Verification: formatting and `git diff --check` passed. The focused
presentation test reached the native build but could not complete because the

**Left open.** Live compositor/lifecycle conformance, connection recreation,
and a successful environment-backed presentation test run remain separate

## 2026-08-23 — Reject unknown visible work in the simulator

`working tree` · area: presentation lifecycle simulation

The deterministic testing backend now returns `SurfaceMissing` when visible
pixel work targets an id with no accepted surface or popup configuration. The
same non-delivery result applies to state-only commits, while hidden targets
retain their intentional no-op behavior. This prevents the recorder from
claiming a frame or compositor state reached an object that does not exist and
keeps shell retry/damage retention paths testable.

Verification: formatting and `git diff --check` passed. The focused
presentation test reached the native build but could not complete because the
Nix development daemon is unavailable and the pinned Skia binary cannot be
downloaded with network access disabled.

**Left open.** Live compositor/lifecycle conformance, connection recreation,
and a successful environment-backed presentation test run remain separate
follow-ups.

## 2026-08-23 — Resolve props from the highest valid precedence layer

`working tree` · area: component language prop resolution

Component runtime prop resolution now validates every declaration default,
global setting, author instance value, and per-instance setting independently,
then selects the highest valid candidate. Invalid higher-precedence values no
longer erase valid lower layers; they are excluded from the published `props`
table and reported with the layer and validation error. Script assignments use
the same rule: an invalid write restores the highest valid host snapshot and
records a stable runtime diagnostic, while a valid script write remains the
higher-precedence value. Settings validation continues to preserve rejected
stored input in the raw namespace and diagnostics while exposing only accepted
overrides to runtime.

Verification: focused resolver tests passed (3); surface-config declaration
validation passed (1); the component prop integration filter passed (13, 2
ignored); `nix develop --command cargo fmt --all -- --check`,
`nix develop --command cargo check --workspace`,
`nix develop --command cargo test --workspace --no-run`, and `git diff --check`
passed. The complete shell library target reached 670 passed, 50
fixture/runtime failures, and 130 ignored tests; the failures remain in the
repository's known fixture/runtime baseline. The workspace library run stopped
at the existing repository-settings fixture rejecting its `revision` key before
reaching later crates. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** JSON-to-prop conversion remains the next component-language
item; structured values are not changed in this increment.

## 2026-08-23 — Derive accessibility focus from live state

`working tree` · area: UI element core accessibility snapshots

Semantic snapshot generation now reads `WidgetNode.state.focused`, the live
interaction state, through `live_accessibility_focus`. The copied
`AccessibilityInfo.focused` value remains a shell compatibility projection,
but accessibility-tree snapshots, retained render-object fingerprints, and
runtime fingerprints no longer depend on it. Regressions cover stale
projections in the core accessibility tree, render snapshots, and AccessKit
updates.

Verification: the focused core, render, and AccessKit tests pass; the full
render library with AccessKit passes (226 tests, 37 ignored); the shell
runtime-tree slice passes (26 tests, 26 ignored); workspace checking, workspace
test compilation, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. The full elements
library reaches 230 passing tests, 10 existing style/theme/props fixture
failures, and 57 ignored tests. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-23 — Complete text measurement contexts and cache identities

`working tree` · area: UI element core text measurement and frontend rendering

Text measurement now uses a typed context carrying every current shaping and
wrapping input: font style, letter spacing, text direction, whitespace mode,
language, shaping features, maximum width, and separate resource/measurer
revisions. Intrinsic layout keys and the renderer’s shaped-layout keys include
the full context. Retained layout detects revision changes independently of
ordinary dirty flags, marks the retained tree dirty, and remeasures before
publishing new geometry. Renderer resource resets, font database changes, and
font-alias changes advance the measurer revision.

**Verification.** Focused layout tests pass (46 active, 7 ignored); the full
elements library reaches 232 passing tests, 10 existing style/theme/props
fixture failures, and 57 ignored tests. The full render library passes (224
active, 37 ignored) plus all 6 paint-performance integration tests. Workspace
checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` pass. No performance claim is
made.

**Left open.** Typed, validated, observable popover placement and
trigger/surface relationships remain the next UI-element backlog item.

## 2026-08-24 — Type and observe popover promotion relationships

`working tree` · area: UI element core popover promotion

Popover placement parsing now returns typed diagnostics for unknown or empty
tokens, invalid offsets, and conflicting constraint values instead of silently
falling back. Inline popover requests carry a typed trigger reference; missing
or empty trigger references and invalid placement prevent promotion and record
stable component diagnostics. Promoted child and legacy popover surfaces retain
the trigger/surface relationship, and the shell exposes the live relationships
for focus, dismissal, accessibility, and debug consumers.

Verification: formatting and `git diff --check` passed; focused workspace
checking passed with existing warnings; placement tests passed (7); typed
invalid-placement, missing-trigger, and promoted-relationship tests each
passed (1). The broad popover filter reached 37 passed, 12 failed, and 1
ignored. The 12 failures are the same known dirty-worktree fixture/runtime
baseline failures recorded before this change (shipped language/hover and
slider interaction cases plus two legacy promoted-popover cases); the two
additional passing tests are this increment's regressions. No performance claim
is made.

**Left open.** The next UI-element backlog item is immutable frame snapshots
with phase stamps, semantic diffs, stable identities, and property-based
invariant tests.

## 2026-08-24 — Prevent disabled and inert activation

`working tree` · area: interaction activation eligibility

Added an ancestor-aware live eligibility check for key-addressed and captured
targets. Pointer-release snapshots, stale focused keyboard targets,
option/radio activation, synthesized touch activation, and programmatic click
refs now refuse activation when the target or any ancestor is disabled or inert.

Verification: `nix develop -c cargo fmt --all -- --check`, focused core element
interaction-contract tests (7 passed), the core interaction library tests (31
passed, 12 ignored), the shell activation module (14 passed), and a relevant
`cargo check` passed. The broader shell `activation` filter still reports three
existing navigation-bar failures with empty requests (19 passed, 3 failed, 1
ignored); these reproduce outside the focused activation module and are not
caused by this change. `git diff --check` passed.

## 2026-08-24 — Preserve keyframe progress across pause/resume

`working tree` · area: UI animation keyframe playback

`ActiveKeyframeAnimation` now treats play-state changes as timeline operations:
pausing records the exact boundary, while resuming shifts the timeline origin
by the paused duration. This preserves delay, direction, iteration-boundary,
and finite-completion semantics across a long pause. The shell bridge now
reuses that stateful transition, and iteration timing uses exact duration
boundaries instead of floating-point division.

Verification: `mesh-core-animation` passed all 21 library tests; the shell
animation interaction module passed 19 tests with 1 ignored; workspace checking,
formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The focused regressions cover long
pauses, all animation directions at iteration boundaries, and the shell bridge.
An initial remainder-based duration implementation failed to compile because
`Duration` has no remainder operator; it was replaced with nanosecond
arithmetic before the final passing validation.

## 2026-08-24 — Add a reduced-motion MotionPolicy snapshot

`working tree` · area: interaction and motion accessibility policy

Added the `shell.motion.reduced` setting and a shared immutable `MotionPolicy`
snapshot. The shell captures it at settings and frame boundaries, immediately
resolves active non-essential work when the preference changes, and passes the
policy through transitions and keyframes. Smooth scrolling, touchpad inertia,
tooltip enter animation, and top-level/child surface transition timing now
also resolve through the same reduced-motion decision.

Verification: `nix develop -c cargo fmt --all -- --check`, workspace checking,
the animation crate (24 passed), the reduced-motion settings test (1 passed),
the reduced-motion scroll/inertia regression (1 passed), and the shell
animation interaction module (19 passed, 1 ignored) passed. The full shell
suite reached 654 passed, 72 failed, and 130 ignored; its failures are the
known dirty-worktree fixture/runtime baseline failures. The full config suite
reached 44 passed and 1 failed on its pre-existing repository `revision`
diagnostic check. `git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

**Left open.** The next interaction-and-motion backlog item is consolidating
focus, pointer capture, press origin, gesture ownership, and scroll ownership
into one transaction.

## 2026-08-24 — Propagate per-keyframe easing

`working tree` · area: interaction and motion animation

Component keyframe parsing now validates and retains each stop's
`animation-timing-function` as a typed easing value. Theme keyframes retain raw
timing functions until theme tokens are resolved, then the shell lowers both
component and theme stops into animation state. The core keyframe sampler and
theme tooltip sampler apply the easing declared at the start of each segment.

Verification: component parsing passed 82 tests; the animation crate passed 27;
the theme crate passed 23; the shell animation slice passed 20 with 1 ignored;
workspace checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.

## 2026-08-24 — Publish typed interaction decisions and dirty outputs

`working tree` · area: renderer-neutral interaction policy

The staged `mesh-core-interaction` transaction now publishes typed focus,
pointer-capture, and ownership decisions alongside categorized dirty output.
Dirty output carries stable node identities and separate style, paint, and
accessibility flags; speculative transitions that return to their starting
state are discarded at commit. The shell adapter consumes those categories at
the renderer boundary, while the state machine remains independent of paint,
Wayland, and script execution.

Verification: `mesh-core-interaction` passed 36 tests with 12 ignored;
`mesh-core-shell` and `mesh-core-interaction` package checks passed;
workspace checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The focused
shell interaction slice reached 123 passed, 14 failed, and 4 ignored. Its
failures are the existing real-surface/audio fixture and localization
diagnostic baseline failures (empty service requests, promoted-popover
geometry/hover, and the known missing-translation assertion), not failures in
the interaction transaction regressions. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Make public box-shadow parsing structured

`working tree` · area: interaction and motion animation

The public animation `parse_box_shadow` API now returns
`Result<BoxShadow, BoxShadowParseError>`. It parses the supported single-shadow
form, including optional `inset`, two to four pixel lengths, supported CSS
colors, and `none`; malformed values, duplicate keywords, negative blur, and
comma-separated shadow lists return typed errors instead of panicking or being
silently truncated.

Verification: `mesh-core-animation` passed 32 library tests; the focused
element-style and component keyframe tests passed; package checks, workspace
checking, formatting, `cargo test --workspace --no-run`, and `git diff --check`
passed. The first workspace no-run attempt exhausted the filesystem while
linking after accumulated Cargo artifacts consumed the available space; the
generated target directory was cleaned and the retry passed. No performance
claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Stabilize animation instance lifecycle

`working tree` · area: interaction and motion animation

Animation playback now uses a typed `AnimationInstanceId` made from retained
node identity, animation-list position, and a declaration generation derived
from timing, direction, fill, easing, iteration, and lowered keyframe content.
Play state remains a control operation on the same instance, so pause/resume
preserves its timeline. Duplicate names occupy distinct list slots; changed
declarations explicitly replace the prior slot occupant, missing declarations
cancel their active instance, and transition target changes report replacement
or reversal while continuing from the currently displayed value.

Verification: `mesh-core-animation` passed 34 library tests;
`mesh-core-shell`'s animation interaction module passed 22 tests with 1
ignored; the duplicate-name replacement, disappearance cancellation, and
pause/resume regressions each passed; workspace checking, formatting, workspace
test compilation (`cargo test --workspace --no-run`), and `git diff --check`
passed. The full `mesh-core-shell` library suite reached 657 passed, 73
failed, and 130 ignored on the existing fixture/runtime baseline. During
implementation, including play state in the declaration fingerprint broke
pause/resume and was removed; the reversal assertion was also corrected to
compare against the eased current display rather than assuming linear output.
No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Introduce the interaction frame contract

`working tree` · area: renderer-neutral interaction policy

Added the public `InteractionFrame` contract in `mesh-core-interaction`. One
frame carries the immutable interaction-state snapshot, input and tree
revisions, typed transaction decisions, stable dirty-node output, immutable
post-layout tree snapshot, and ordered phase stamps for input/state, style
invalidation, layout, animation, paint, and semantics. Late render-time state
reconciliation is deferred to the next frame so a published frame cannot be
rewound or mixed across revisions. The shell now records dirty categories and
publishes its frame at the paint boundary.

Verification: `mesh-core-interaction` passed 40 tests with 12 ignored; the
focused shell frame publication test passed; workspace checking and
`cargo test --workspace --no-run` passed. The full shell suite reached 659
passed, 72 failed, and 130 ignored on the existing fixture/runtime baseline.
The full workspace suite stopped at the existing `mesh-core-config`
repository-settings `revision` diagnostic failure. No performance claim is
made.

`working tree` · area: frontend compiler and host

Added compiler regressions for frontend entrypoints and recursive local

single resolver used by compiler reads and dependency identities.

Verification: the two focused compiler regressions passed; the module path
