formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The full frontend compiler suite
reached 76 passed, 1 failed, and 20 ignored on the existing
`phase87_layout_display_source_semantics_survive_lowering` baseline failure
(`Loading Ready` versus `Main grid`). No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Keep service payloads behind resolved read policy

`working tree` · area: service contracts and frontend scripting

Service snapshots remain in per-context Rust-owned stores and are only applied
when the resolved interface read policy grants state access. Event-only
contracts can resolve a proxy and receive their typed event payloads through
the separate event policy, but event subscriptions do not seed or expose
service state. Shell delivery and Luau proxy creation now enforce those
separate decisions.

Verification: the focused event-only scripting and shell regressions passed;
scripting event tests passed 8 tests and host policy tests passed 1; workspace
checking, workspace test compilation, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
The full scripting suite reached 198 passed, 1 failed, and 26 ignored on the
existing queued-result assertion
(`interface_proxy_method_returns_queued_result`); the shell service
integration slice reached 6 passed and 5 failed on existing nil-proxy fixture
failures. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Reload frontend roots atomically

`working tree` · area: frontend compiler and host

Hot reload now compiles a module's primary frontend root and every currently
resolved contribution root into a candidate catalog before one generation
replacement. Contribution paths remain in the module watch set, changed
contributions mark their consuming hosts affected, and a failed primary or
contribution compile leaves the previous catalog generation and runtime source
intact.

Verification: the focused source-reload group passed 3 tests; workspace
checking, workspace test compilation, formatting, and `git diff --check`
passed. The catalog performance slice reached 3 passed, 3 existing fixture
failures, and 2 ignored; the full shell library suite reached 658 passed, 75
existing fixture/runtime baseline failures, and 130 ignored. No performance
claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Validate contribution interface roots

`working tree` · area: frontend compiler and host

Contribution roots now run through the same interface-import validation as
primary roots. The catalog retains interface ranges from compiled `require`
imports, checks declarations and graph-resolved contract/provider versions, and
keeps built-in or unavailable interfaces on the runtime health/degradation
path. An undeclared or incompatible required contribution import is omitted
with a scoped diagnostic, so the host does not mount an invalid contribution.
Added negative coverage for undeclared imports and incompatible required
ranges.

Verification: `nix develop -c cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib contribution_roots_ -- --nocapture` passed 3 tests; `nix develop -c cargo check --workspace` passed; `nix develop -c cargo fmt --all -- --check` passed; and `git diff --check` passed. The broad `nix develop -c cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib --no-fail-fast` run reached 662 passed, 73 existing fixture/runtime baseline failures, and 130 ignored; both new contribution tests passed in that run. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Validate root and nested expression scopes

`working tree` · area: frontend compiler and host

Frontend compilation now validates root and nested template expressions
through one parser-derived symbol set. Root templates no longer bypass scope
validation; runtime `props` is recognized, keyed-loop key expressions use the
loop item scope, and diagnostics retain expression-body source spans. Added
regressions for unknown root symbols, root props and keyed loops, and nested
keyed-loop source spans.

Verification: `nix develop -c cargo test -p mesh-core-frontend --lib compile::tests -- --nocapture` passed 18 tests; `nix develop -c cargo check --workspace` passed; `nix develop -c cargo fmt --all -- --check` passed; and `git diff --check` passed. The full `nix develop -c cargo test -p mesh-core-frontend --lib --no-fail-fast --quiet` run reached 79 passed, 1 existing `phase87_layout_display_source_semantics_survive_lowering` failure (`Loading Ready` versus `Main grid`), and 20 ignored. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Share compiled template expression semantics

`working tree` · area: frontend compiler and host

Template expressions now compile through one shared `mesh-core-expression`
artifact and cache. Parsed components retain deduplicated compiled expressions;
preview, locale translation, frontend composition, and live Luau closures all
consume that artifact and the same Luau truthiness/operator semantics. The old
production hand-written evaluator is no longer used; its test-only compatibility
wrapper delegates to the shared evaluator.

Verification: the shared expression unit tests passed 4 tests; component parser
tests passed 82 tests; scripting template tests passed 9 tests with 1 ignored;
focused frontend expression tests passed 5 tests, translation and typed-attribute
tests passed 1 each; the compiled-artifact parser regression passed 1 test; workspace checking,
format checking, and `git diff --check` passed. The full frontend library suite
reached 79 passed, 1 existing `phase87_layout_display_source_semantics_survive_lowering`
failure (`Loading Ready` versus `Main grid`), and 20 ignored. No performance claim
is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Diagnose non-ASCII expression syntax safely

`working tree` · area: frontend expression compilation

The shared expression compiler now discovers non-ASCII input with UTF-8-aware
`char_indices()` offsets. Unsupported non-ASCII expression syntax produces a
typed diagnostic containing the character and byte offset, including when the
underlying parser fails or panics; valid Unicode string literals retain normal
Luau behavior. Regression coverage exercises the supported operator families,
panic-free diagnostics, and Unicode literal evaluation.

Verification: the shared expression suite passed 6 tests; the full component
suite passed 84 tests; the focused scripting template suite passed 8 tests with
1 ignored; workspace checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The
full scripting suite reached 200 passed, 1 existing queued-result failure, and
26 ignored; the full frontend suite reached 80 passed, 1 existing
`phase87_layout_display_source_semantics_survive_lowering` failure (`Loading
Ready` versus `Main grid`), and 20 ignored. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Publish runtime props transactionally

`working tree` · area: frontend compiler and host

Runtime prop publication now treats the Luau environment write and Rust state
snapshot as one failure boundary. Failed member writes are returned to the
shell instead of being replaced with a Rust-only fallback, and resolved
`props` publication failures prevent an incomplete runtime from being
published. Added failure-injection coverage proving a blocked Luau write
preserves both the Luau-visible and Rust-visible old value.

**Verification.** The focused scripting transaction test and shell prop suite
passed; workspace checking and formatting passed. The full shell library suite
reached 663 passed, 73 existing fixture/runtime baseline failures, and 130
ignored; the full scripting library suite reached 201 passed, 1 existing
`interface_proxy_method_returns_queued_result` failure, and 26 ignored. No
performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Preserve typed diagnostic categories and source spans

`working tree` · area: frontend compiler and host diagnostics

Component parse errors now expose typed categories beside their existing byte
spans. Frontend compiler errors retain the parser category, owning source path,
and span. Shell catalog diagnostics carry that metadata into structured debug
entries, while runtime script diagnostics distinguish interface failures from
storage failures and handler errors. LSP diagnostics now publish stable
category codes while preserving UTF-8-byte to UTF-16 source ranges.

Verification: component tests passed 85/85; diagnostics tests passed 7/7; the
new LSP category test passed 1/1; workspace checking and formatting
passed. The combined frontend/scripting test run retained the existing
`phase87_layout_display_source_semantics_survive_lowering` and
`interface_proxy_method_returns_queued_result` failures. The focused shell
catalog fixture test also stopped at its existing missing `@mesh/debug-inspector`
fixture assertion; the direct compiler-diagnostic metadata test is used for the
new shell mapping instead. `git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is
made.

## 2026-08-24 — Enforce distinct popover and overflow child-surface semantics

`working tree` · area: frontend compiler and host child surfaces

Child-surface reconciliation now treats authored popovers and geometry-derived
overflow surfaces as separate policies. Popovers retain authored placement,
hover-bridge dismissal, exit transitions, trigger relationships, and focus
return on compositor dismissal. Overflow surfaces are pinned to their escaped
node geometry, never take a click grab or child keyboard ownership, skip the
popover hover bridge and exit lifecycle, and are retried after compositor
dismissal while their geometry still escapes the parent. Dismissal suppression
is keyed by both child kind and node identity, so the two ownership models
cannot suppress or recreate one another's surfaces.

Verification: the focused child-surface suite passed 28 tests with 1 ignored,
including five new overflow/popover behavior regressions; the popover filter
passed 40 tests with 13 known fixture/runtime baseline failures and 1 ignored;
workspace checking and formatting passed; and `git diff --check` passed. The
broad shell library reached 667 passed, 76 known fixture/runtime baseline
failures, and 130 ignored. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Contain frontend expression and runtime failures

`working tree` · area: frontend compiler and host

Template expression failures now produce source-aware template diagnostics and
retain the last-known-good surface tree; an initial failure uses the existing
bounded error placeholder. Embedded/local component failures are contained at
their component boundary so a broken child cannot blank its host. Frontend
runtime initialization, replacement initialization, interface-event delivery,
and surface-visibility state writes now retain control of the shell while
reporting actionable runtime diagnostics.

**Verification.** Focused frontend diagnostics coverage passed (12 tests),
`cargo check -p mesh-core-shell`, `cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and
`git diff --check` passed. The broad shell library reached 674 passed, 73
existing fixture/runtime baseline failures, and 130 ignored; failures remain
in the existing catalog, shipped-surface, service, theme, backend, and graph
fixtures. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Publish public prop schemas at import boundaries

`working tree` · area: frontend compiler and host

Compiled frontend records now publish normalized exposed `PropDef` schemas.
Local and module component imports validate unknown, private, and invalid
static values against the imported component boundary with the importing
source span preserved. Contribution roots use the same import validation, and
manifest contribution props are checked against the compiled public schema
before publication or slot rendering. The shipped navigation settings page
now declares its `namespace` and `title` contribution inputs explicitly.

**Verification.** The component library passed 86 tests. Focused frontend
import validation passed; focused shell catalog schema, contribution-import,
and settings-page tests passed. `cargo check -p mesh-core-shell --tests`,
`cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The full
frontend library retained the documented unrelated
`phase87_layout_display_source_semantics_survive_lowering` failure (80 passed,
1 failed, 20 ignored). No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Enforce one sandbox/resource policy for every Luau realm

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime, frontend contexts, and backend services

`SandboxConfig` now carries shared instruction, memory, output, queue, storage,
and child-process limits. Frontend thread realms and backend Lua contexts use
the same `RuntimePolicy`, which installs Luau sandboxing, memory limits, and
interrupt-based instruction checkpoints. Host APIs use the same broker for
published events, element actions, service tickets, logs, state/config output,
stream lines, subprocess counts/timeouts, and durable storage document quotas.
Queued resource counts are released on drain, reload, and shutdown cleanup.

The initial sandbox integration exposed Luau's import optimization caching a
mutable `mesh` host member; the realm compiler now marks that host table
mutable so capability/runtime updates remain visible. Bounded synchronous exec
also drains capped output before returning, preventing an over-producing child
from blocking the host while its output budget is enforced.

Verification: `RUST_TEST_THREADS=1 nix develop -c cargo test -p
mesh-core-scripting --lib` passed 204 tests with 26 ignored; the backend suite
passed 26/26 and the frontend-host suite passed 5/5. Focused exec tests passed
6/6, storage tests passed 7/7 with 2 ignored, and `nix develop -c cargo check
-p mesh-core-scripting -p mesh-core-backend -p mesh-core-frontend-host` plus
formatting and `git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Keep service state Rust-owned and generation-aware

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime, service delivery, and frontend contexts

Frontend service snapshots remain outside shared Luau globals in a
capability-filtered Rust store owned by each script context. Shell delivery now
attaches the provider identity and monotonic accepted-state generation to
snapshots, propagates that metadata through cached/replayed frontend delivery,
and rejects stale snapshots before they can replace proxy-visible state or
reactive state. Shared-VM isolation, read-only proxy behavior, and event-only
capability boundaries remain covered by the existing regressions; a new
regression covers stale generation rejection.

Verification: `nix develop --command cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff
--check` passed. The scripting library passed 205 tests with 26 ignored. The
focused shell generation tests passed, and shell library compilation passed;
the broad shell library reached 673 passed, 76 existing fixture/runtime
baseline failures, and 130 ignored. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Route backend termination through idempotent cleanup

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime/backend lifecycle

Backend execution now has one outer lifecycle guard. Load, initialization,
publication, command-channel, normal stop, and task-cancellation exits all
converge on idempotent stream termination, storage flushing, and one terminal
`Stopped` record. The scripting context also flushes storage after a failing
stop hook and reclaims Rust-owned resources from `Drop` when cancellation or a
panic prevents the authored hook from running.

**Verification.** The scripting library passed 207 tests with 26 ignored and
the backend library passed 29 tests. Workspace checking, formatting, and
`git diff --check` passed. The focused shell lifecycle filter passed 27 tests
and retained three unrelated existing supervision/fixture failures; no shell
files were changed.

## 2026-08-24 — Give stream subprocesses identity and awaited shutdown

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and sandbox

Stream subprocesses now receive stable IDs and runtime generations, with typed
handles carrying program, arguments, and status. One bounded event queue emits
started, line, EOF, failure, exit, and overflow records; line length, queued
items, queued bytes, and active children are limited. Each stream task owns its
child and awaits reaping, while normal backend lifecycle shutdown awaits all
stream tasks and synchronous drop paths retain a kill-and-detach fallback.
Legacy line hooks remain compatible, but dispatch is grouped by stream identity
instead of executable name; typed `on_stream_event` callbacks expose the new
records to Luau.

**Verification.** The scripting library passed 215 tests with 26 ignored and
the backend library passed 29 tests. Workspace checking, formatting, and
`git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Move `mesh.exec` behind a cancellable worker boundary

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and sandbox

`mesh.exec` now submits each child process to a per-backend worker supervisor
instead of calling `std::process::Command` on the async backend task. The
supervisor bounds child concurrency, kills process groups on cancellation and
deadlines, reaps worker threads, and enforces one aggregate stdout/stderr byte
limit while draining over-producing children. Shell-style `-c` execution also
requires the distinct high-risk `exec.shell` grant; the shipped Hyprland
provider declares it explicitly. Backend shutdown cancels outstanding exec
jobs before stream teardown.

**Verification.** The focused exec tests passed 4/4; the scripting library
passed 219 tests with 26 ignored; the capability library passed 5/5; the
backend library passed 29/29; and workspace checking passed. The broad
workspace test gate reached an unrelated existing
`mesh-core-config::settings::tests::the_repository_settings_file_is_clean`
failure because the repository `settings.json` contains an unrecognized
`revision` key. Formatting and `git diff --check` passed. No performance claim
is made. A separate `mesh-core-module` library run reached 233 passed, 8
existing content-store/graph/scan/shipped-fixture failures, and 3 ignored;
none involved the new capability catalog entry.

## 2026-08-24 — Gate event and side-effect queues through one typed authorization/resource boundary

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime, backend event delivery, and shell effects

Frontend published shell and service operations, service tickets, and
imperative element actions now pass through typed operation validation before
queue acceptance. Backend provider events use immutable interface event
registries for declared names and exact payload schemas, with runtime
generation metadata propagated to the shell so stale events are dropped before
delivery. A shared side-effect reservation accounts for queue items and
retained serialized bytes, rolls back rejected reservations, and releases both
budgets on drain and reload cleanup.

**Verification.** Focused scripting boundary tests passed; the scripting
library passed 236 tests with 26 ignored; the backend library passed 31 tests;
focused shell interface-event tests passed 3; workspace checking and
formatting passed. The broad shell library reported 670 passed, 80 failed,
and 130 ignored, retaining the repository's existing catalog, fixture, and
runtime baseline failures. An initial multi-filter Cargo test invocation was
invalid because Cargo accepts one filter and was rerun with supported filters.
No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Represent locale as a host-owned per-context cell updated with the translation snapshot

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and localization

Frontend script contexts now own one host-locked locale cell containing the
active locale, module translation entries, and catalog revision. The
`mesh.locale.current()` and `mesh.i18n.t()` host APIs read the same snapshot,
and locale changes replace the cell as a unit before the shell invalidates
runtime state. Reinitialization clears the cell so a new context cannot retain
a previous generation's locale or catalog.

**Verification.** The focused locale/i18n scripting test passed 1/1, the
scripting library passed 236 tests with 26 ignored, workspace checking and
formatting passed, and `git diff --check` passed. The shell locale filter
reported 7 passed and 3 existing fixture/catalog failures (`graph_locale_commit_replaces_catalog_and_retains_last_known_good_on_failure`,
`debug_snapshot_resolves_module_graph_layout_label_with_active_locale`, and
`shipped_language_trigger_flag_follows_the_picked_locale`). No performance
claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Treat Luau `nil` writes as explicit deletions in Rust state and dependency invalidation

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and frontend invalidation

Luau writes of `nil` now remove the corresponding public variable from the
Rust `ScriptState`, clear associated host fingerprints, advance mutation and
snapshot generations, and mark the member as changed for template dependency
gates. Scalar proxy teardown and raw compound-global deletion both use the
same path, and later assignments can recreate the public member. Regression
coverage verifies state removal, dependency invalidation, restoration, and a
full conditional-tree render update.

**Verification.** The focused scripting and shell regressions passed, the
scripting library passed 237 tests with 26 ignored, the shell invalidation
filter passed 56 tests with 13 ignored but retained the existing
`phase98_pixel_equivalence_backend_update` failure, workspace checking passed,
and formatting plus `git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Restrict provider event publication to provider-owned handles

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and sandbox

Interface event channels exposed to consumers now provide subscription and
unsubscribe operations only; `emit` and `fire` are no longer available on a
consumer service proxy. Rust host delivery dispatches the channel subscriber
set directly, preserving independent callback failure handling. Backend
providers no longer receive the string-based `mesh.service.emit_event` escape
hatch: declared interface events are published through provider-owned
`self.EventName:fire(...)` handles, with static graph diagnostics and payload
validation following that contract.

**Verification.** Focused scripting event tests (12), provider handle and
callback-isolation tests, backend forwarding (31), and module graph/scanner
regressions passed. The scripting library passed 238 tests with 26 ignored;
the backend library passed 31 tests; workspace checking and formatting passed.
The broad module library retained 233 passing tests, 8 unrelated baseline
permission/content-store, graph/scan, and shipped-fixture failures, and 3
ignored tests. Focused shell backend-delivery and event-only tests passed; the
existing `frontend_component_observes_only_subscribed_interface_events`
service-observation fixture still fails its subscription-index assertion before
runtime event dispatch. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-24 — Bound backend ingress and aggregate runtime resources

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and sandbox

Production backend generations now use bounded command and event bridges, with
explicit queue capacities and an overload boundary instead of unbounded
provider output. Command, service-state, and provider-event JSON payloads use
one shared byte/depth validator before or at every host boundary. Provider
event counts are bounded in the Luau resource broker, while callback output,
queued side effects and stream output, child processes, and storage writes
consume one aggregate runtime budget in addition to their specific limits.

Storage writes roll back when the aggregate charge is rejected, and command
and event ingress tests cover payload bounds, event queue overflow, event
counts, and aggregate exhaustion.

**Verification.** The focused backend JSON/queue tests passed; the full
`mesh-core-backend` library passed 34 tests; the scripting backend filter
passed 83 tests; workspace checking, formatting, and `git diff --check`
passed. The full `mesh-core-scripting` library reached 243 passed with one
shared-VM memory-retention failure and 26 ignored. The focused
`mesh-core-shell backend_lifecycle` filter reached 27 passed with three
existing lifecycle/fixture failures. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Separate effective service read and control grants

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime, service capability policy, and shell delivery

Service read and control grants now have separate effective checks. A
`service.<name>.control`-only module can resolve a contract proxy and invoke its
authorized methods, but it cannot receive the provider's Rust-owned state
snapshot. The same read decision remains in the shell component fan-out, while
shell command lowering continues to require the control grant. Regression tests
cover both the Lua proxy and the shell delivery path, including custom method
policies.

**Verification.** The focused host-policy tests passed 2/2; the full
`mesh-core-scripting` library passed 246 tests with 26 ignored; the focused
control-only shell delivery test passed 1/1; the shell service command boundary
test passed 1/1; workspace checking and formatting passed. The broader shell
service integration filter reported 7 passed and 5 existing fixture/runtime
baseline failures (`frontend_component_observes_only_subscribed_interface_events`,
`frontend_component_keeps_service_updates_for_subscribed_event_services_only`,
`frontend_proxy_command_from_bundled_handler_becomes_service_command_request`,
`quick_settings_audio_slider_publishes_set_volume_service_command`, and
`quick_settings_network_toggle_publishes_set_wifi_enabled_service_command`).
The first shell test attempt also exposed a test-fixture cleanup panic and was
corrected before the focused test passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Reconcile poll intervals after stream callbacks

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and backend scheduling

Stream callback results now pass through the same centralized `refresh_interval`
reconciliation used by poll and command callbacks. Typed stream lifecycle
callbacks and legacy batch/line callbacks therefore apply a changed
`self.poll_interval_ms` before the backend waits for another tick. Regression
coverage starts a bounded stream, changes the interval from 1000 ms to 60 ms in
`on_stream_batch`, and requires the following poll within 300 ms.

**Verification.** The focused stream interval regression passed 1/1; the full
`mesh-core-backend` library passed 35/35; formatting and `git diff --check`
passed. Workspace checking reached the shell dependency build but failed
because the environment lacks the system `xkbcommon` package. The first
regression assertion assumed a stream update would precede the backend's
immediate first poll and failed with a poll update first; the test was corrected
to consume that legitimate initial poll before asserting stream-to-poll
behavior. An earlier link attempt failed because the repository target
consumed 77 GiB and the filesystem had only 155 MB free; the regenerable target
was cleaned before the focused test passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Return recoverable backend host setup errors

`working tree` · area: Luau runtime and backend lifecycle

Backend Luau initialization now returns `BackendScriptError::HostSetup` when
sandbox policy or host API installation fails. The context no longer unwraps
those setup results, clears the partially initialized Lua realm before
returning, and keeps the existing backend load-failure path responsible for
publishing the module-scoped failure and terminal stopped record. Runtime
methods now propagate an uninitialized-realm error instead of manufacturing a
fallback environment. A no-panic regression covers an injected host
registration failure.

**Verification.** The full `mesh-core-scripting` library passed 247 tests with
26 ignored; the full `mesh-core-backend` library passed 35 tests; formatting and
`git diff --check` passed. Workspace checking reached the shell dependency
build but failed because the environment lacks the system `xkbcommon` package.
No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Lower asymmetric four-edge borders and four-corner radii

`working tree` · area: frontend rendering and paint lowering

Retained `DisplayPaintStyle` records all four `Corners` instead of projecting
only the top-left radius. Rounded fills, gradients, shadows, backdrop clips,
and the display-list blur-region bands now use the full corner shape. Border
lowering emits a backend-neutral `DrawBorder` command carrying all four edge
widths and lowers it in Skia as an outer/inner rounded ring, preserving
asymmetric widths and radii. The AnyRender proof adapter likewise avoids
averaging unequal edge widths.

**Verification.** The new display-payload and Skia asymmetric-border
regressions passed. The full `mesh-core-render --lib` suite passed 228 tests
with 37 ignored; the `renderer-anyrender` feature test slice passed 7 tests;
the focused shell render slice passed 6 tests with 1 ignored; workspace
checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. The first pixel assertion
used a 2px corner and correctly exposed antialias coverage rather than a
lowering defect; the regression now uses unambiguous asymmetric corner sizes.
No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Unify cumulative affine paint geometry

`working tree` · area: rendering, retained display lists, shell damage, and interaction geometry

Retained paint nodes now carry their cumulative affine transform, local layout,
and exact transformed ancestor overflow clips. Skia paints rotated nodes,
controls, text, images, and scrollbars in local coordinates under that matrix;
display-list damage, visual effect overflow, backdrop blur regions, shell
subtree bounds, and descendant rebuild decisions use the same cumulative
geometry. This closes the remaining paint/damage/blur gap in the shared affine
interaction contract.

**Verification.** The focused affine interaction, retained display-list,
transformed blur, shell damage, and Skia paint regressions passed. The full
`mesh-core-render --lib` suite passed 232 tests with 37 ignored. The broad
`mesh-core-shell --lib` run reproduced 679 passing and 76 baseline
fixture/environment failures with 130 ignored; the serialized run reproduced
the same family, and representative failures passed or reproduced in
isolation. They span catalog, service, navigation, theme, graph, and debug
tests. Workspace checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. No
performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Isolate node opacity and blend-mode compositing

`working tree` · area: frontend rendering and paint lowering

Retained display-list nodes now preserve their original primitive colors and
lower opacity and `mix-blend-mode` into explicit push/pop compositing groups
around the complete node subtree. The existing Skia layer backend applies the
group opacity and blend mode once, so gradients, images, text, shadows, and
descendants share the same CSS-like compositing boundary. Layer-aware retained
spans and sparse command selection keep each group atomic; filter scopes remain
nested and retain their blur-specific damage expansion.

**Verification.** The focused display-list and Skia opacity/blend regressions
passed; the full `mesh-core-render --lib` suite passed 235 tests with 37
ignored; the `renderer-anyrender` feature slice passed 7 tests; workspace
checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is
made.

## 2026-08-25 — Track paint topology generations and stable equal-z order

`working tree` · area: retained display lists and paint ordering

Retained display-list generation now compares the ordered sequence of command
node identities and command kinds, so paint-order and layer/topology changes
advance the generation even when the primitive entry map is unchanged. Child
z-index ordering uses a stable sort, preserving authored order for equal-z
sibling ties and making reordered retained command streams deterministic.

**Verification.** The focused equal-z/reorder regression passed; the final
`mesh-core-render --lib` suite passed 236 tests with 37 ignored; the
`renderer-anyrender` feature slice passed 7 tests; workspace checking,
formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. An initial topology-derived damage
expansion was reverted after the full suite showed it widened removed-entry
damage from 200 to 1,600 pixels; existing entry-based damage remains
unchanged. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Unify dirty contracts and paint signatures

`working tree` · area: retained rendering, display-list signatures, and shell invalidation

Retained render-object dirty categories and display-list primitive signatures
now derive from one shared `PaintInput` contract. The contract covers material
style, opacity, control payloads, text content and typography, icon resources
and variable axes, and deterministically ordered custom-property variables.
Render dirty classification and display-list entry signatures therefore agree
for checked controls, text attributes and styles, icons, and variables while
preserving tag-aware filtering for irrelevant generic attributes. Shell style
fingerprints use the same variable fingerprint.

**Verification.** The focused shared-contract regression passed; the full
`mesh-core-render --lib` suite passed 237 tests with 37 ignored; the retained
runtime-tree suite passed 27 tests with 26 ignored; workspace checking,
formatting, and `git diff --check` passed. An initial shared hash included
control/icon attributes on generic nodes and failed the existing irrelevant-
attribute signature regression; tag-aware hashing corrected it and the final
render suite passed. The broad `mesh-core-shell --lib` run reached 680 passed,
76 existing fixture/catalog/runtime baseline failures, and 130 ignored; the
known `phase98_pixel_equivalence_backend_update` failure remains in that
baseline. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Include font/resource revisions in text painting and caches

`working tree` · area: retained text painting and font/resource cache identity

Retained paint inputs and display-list paint nodes now carry the active
resource revision, so font and resource replacement invalidates both dirty
contracts and rebuilt text/icon paint payloads. Display text and controls now
share one complete text-style record, including letter spacing, whitespace,
direction, language, and shaping-feature inputs, for measurement, selection,
ellipsis truncation, and glyph painting. Ellipsis and named-font availability
caches include the complete style/revision identity; existing glyph, font-byte,
text-layout, intrinsic-measurement, and glyph-atlas keys remain revision-aware.

**Verification.** Focused text-cache tests passed (6), painter-text tests
passed (5), and display signature tests passed (10 active, 8 ignored),
including a resource-revision invalidation regression. The full
`mesh-core-render --lib` suite passed 239 tests with 37 ignored; render crate
checking passed; formatting and `git diff --check` were run. An initial
multi-filter Cargo test invocation was rejected because Cargo accepts one
test filter; the suites were rerun separately. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Make backdrop blur an explicit renderer/backend capability

`working tree` · area: retained display lists, paint backends, presentation
capabilities, and shell rendering

Backdrop blur now lowers through an explicit retained `BackdropBlurPolicy`:
compositor-owned regions, validated in-surface filtering, or an observable
rejected fallback. The selected policy is part of display-list command
topology and generation identity, while command-span indexing accounts for
the extra policy command so sparse repaint remains ordered and complete.
The presentation layer gates compositor blur on the negotiated
`org_kde_kwin_blur_manager` capability; the shell selects in-surface fallback
only when the active paint backend advertises support, otherwise it emits a
typed backdrop-blur diagnostic and paints flat.

**Verification.** Backdrop-focused render regressions passed (9), the full
`mesh-core-render --lib` suite passed 243 tests with 37 ignored, the focused
presentation capability test passed, and shell/workspace checks passed.
The full `mesh-core-presentation --lib` suite still has two unrelated existing
testing-backend failures (`lifecycle_events_are_typed_and_testing_teardown_is_idempotent`
and `unconfigured_surface_returns_typed_not_ready_without_recording_present`),
which also fail when run individually. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Unify fractional-scale edge coverage

`working tree` · area: fractional-scale layout, paint, SHM copy, and Wayland damage

Added a shared `FractionalScale`/`DeviceRect` contract: logical edges are
converted by flooring near edges and ceiling far edges, then clipped in device
space. Layout damage, retained paint clips and effect regions, shell clears,
physical buffer allocation, SHM copying, and `damage_buffer` now reuse that
representation; protocol damage no longer rescales already-physical damage.

**Verification.** The focused fractional-scale render tests passed (4), the
focused presentation damage tests passed (14), the full `mesh-core-render
--lib` suite passed (247 passed, 37 ignored), workspace checking passed, and
formatting plus `git diff --check` passed. The full
`mesh-core-presentation --lib` suite retains two unrelated testing-backend
failures; the focused shell test target retains its existing fixture compile
failure involving stale `DisplayPaintStyle` fields. No performance claim is
made.

## 2026-08-25 — Preserve release batch and barrier diagnostics

`working tree` · area: retained display-list batching, profiling diagnostics, and debug telemetry

Release display-list updates now retain a compact ordered material stream
containing batch signatures and barrier kinds, so `DisplayListMetrics` keeps
the same batch and barrier counts in optimized builds as in debug builds. The
existing shell profiling projection and `mesh.debug` telemetry therefore
report real release metrics without retaining full display entries solely for
diagnostics. The obsolete debug-only ordered-entry sink benchmark was removed,
and a release regression covers a known batchable scene.

**Verification.** The focused render regression passed in debug and release;
the full `mesh-core-render --lib` suite passed 248 tests with 36 ignored;
`mesh-core-debug --lib` passed, workspace checking, formatting, and
`git diff --check` passed. The focused shell profiling test could not compile
because its existing `shell/runtime/render/tests.rs` fixture is missing
`DisplayPaintStyle` fields (`language`, `letter_spacing`, `resource_revision`,
and two others). An initial regression run exposed and corrected a fixture
expectation because the text node also emitted a background slot. No
performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Validate caller lineage for generation shortcuts

`working tree` · area: retained render-object and display-list generation gates

Retained render-object and display-list fast paths now retain a debug-only
fingerprint of the caller's widget tree. A same-generation caller with a
different lineage emits a tracing diagnostic and falls back to full
synchronization; sparse render-object and display-list paths also refuse to
patch against that untrusted lineage. Release builds keep the generation
shortcut without the fingerprint walk, while debug and release regressions
cover both the mismatch recovery and the production fast path.

**Verification.** The focused caller-lineage tests passed in debug and release;
the full `mesh-core-render --lib` suites passed 248 tests with 36 ignored in
both profiles; workspace checking, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
An initial full debug run exposed an order-sensitive existing Skia shader-cache
assertion; the test passed in isolation and on the rerun. Early focused runs
also corrected test metric expectations and caught a missing retained-generation
equality check in the new display-list gate before final validation. No
performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Include resource revisions in retained-present decisions

`working tree` · area: retained rendering and presentation cache invalidation

Retained display lists now retain the resource revision used to build their
commands, invalidate generation-gated reuse when resources change, rebuild
conservatively, and emit full-surface damage. Parent region state and child
paint caches include the revision, while quiet visible surfaces request a
repaint when their last painted revision becomes stale.

**Verification.** The focused retained-display-list and child-paint-cache
tests passed; the full `mesh-core-render --lib` suite passed 249 tests with 36
ignored; the relevant shell render, surface-layout, and child-surface filters
passed; workspace test compilation, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.
The broad `mesh-core-shell --lib` suite remains red with 680 passed, 76
failed, and 130 ignored, including unrelated current-tree theme, navigation,
graph, and backend assertions.

## 2026-08-25 — Move resource decode and rasterization behind the broker

`working tree` · area: render resource preparation

Source-image decode for painter image commands now enters the shared
generation-aware resource broker with a bounded immutable handoff, revision
and freshness checks, cancellation, and scheduler polling. Production
missing-icon fallback no longer rasterizes an embedded SVG on the frame
thread; it paints a deterministic placeholder while async icon, glyph, and
image work completes.

**Verification.** The icon-resource slice passed 18 tests with 2 ignored; the
full `mesh-core-render --lib -- --test-threads=1` suite passed 250 tests with
36 ignored; production renderer and shell checks passed; relevant shell
filters passed; workspace test compilation, formatting, and `git diff --check`
passed. The default parallel renderer run exposed one order-sensitive
existing shader-cache assertion (expected 1, got 2); the exact test and
single-threaded suite passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Include blur and decorations in presentation change detection

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation surface configuration

Semantic surface diffs now compare the compositor-facing layer namespace,
which includes the `:blur` intent suffix, and the creation-time xdg decoration
request. Blur-only layer changes and client/server decoration changes therefore
take the typed surface-role recreation path instead of being dropped by a
partial presentation comparison. Regression assertions also pin the lowered
namespace values used by layer creation.

**Verification.** The focused blur and window-decoration regressions passed;
the full `mesh-core-presentation --lib` suite passed 107 tests with 2 existing
testing-backend failures (`lifecycle_events_are_typed_and_testing_teardown_is_idempotent`
and `unconfigured_surface_returns_typed_not_ready_without_recording_present`)
and 11 ignored. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `cargo check --workspace`
passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Route settings role reloads through the shared transition supervisor

`FrontendSurfaceComponent` now stages an authorized settings-selected role
until the shell can route it through the existing transactional role
supervisor. Settings reloads therefore use the same child-surface teardown,
focus cleanup, compositor replacement, and cached-config invalidation as
explicit promotion, while unauthorized role changes remain rejected.

**Verification.** The settings-role supervisor regression passed; the
production component staging and non-promotable guard regressions passed; the
window-role slice passed 9 tests; the props slice passed 16 tests with 2
ignored; `cargo check --workspace` passed; and formatting plus
`git diff --check` passed. The broad `mesh-core-shell --lib --no-fail-fast`
run passed 681 tests, failed 78 existing unrelated tests, and ignored 130.

## 2026-08-25 — Share role-field metadata across surface policy boundaries

`working tree` · area: surface policy, settings, and presentation contracts

A dependency-free `mesh-core-surface-policy` contract now owns the role-field
vocabulary, manifest names, and sparse settings keys used by manifest
diagnostics, settings validation, configuration ejection, and presentation
protocol lowering. All role-specific fields are covered, including keyboard
mode, margins, blur, and decorations, so inert settings are warned about and
ejected blocks omit fields that cannot affect their declared role.

**Verification.** The shared policy tests passed (2), surface-config tests
passed (33), surface manifest diagnostics passed (7), and the presentation
configuration slice passed 26 tests with one existing

existing unrelated content-store, event-scan, and shipped-fixture tests, and
ignored 3. `cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and `git diff --check`
passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-25 — Preserve localized identity during configuration ejection

`working tree` · area: surface settings and configuration ejection

Surface settings now accept both literal titles and structured localized text,
so `{ "t", "fallback" }` identity survives resolution and ejection instead of
being reduced to the current fallback string. The CLI now uses an explicit
effective-policy ejection serializer that preserves effective precedence and
materializes the module-derived window `app_id` before writing the value as a
sparse override.

**Verification.** The surface-config suite passed 35 tests, the focused shell
surface-layout suite passed 21 tests, `cargo check --workspace`, formatting,
and `git diff --check` passed. The foundation config suite passed 45 tests with

tests with one existing locale-completion failure; and the CLI suite passed 7
tests with 4 existing package-update/content-store failures. No performance

## 2026-08-26 — Type shell/presentation surface extents

`working tree` · area: surface policy and presentation geometry

The shell/presentation boundary now carries typed `UnmeasuredSize`, positive
`ContentExtent`, padded `SurfaceExtent`, and explicit `LayerWireExtent` axes in
`SurfaceConfig`. Shell lowering and popup/window paths construct the typed
values together; presentation protocol mapping is the only place that lowers
an intentional `Span` to wire zero. Input regions, configure diffs, output
clamping, window hints, and paint measurement now consume the typed boundary
instead of split raw width/height/padding fields.

Regression coverage verifies unmeasured axes, content/surface/padding
round-trips, wire-span lowering, transient oversized padding, surface layout,
and first-measurement/re-show geometry.

**Verification.** `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `git diff --check`, and
`cargo check --workspace` passed. The focused presentation config suite passed
26 tests and the focused shell surface-layout suite passed 21 tests. The full
presentation library reached 109 passed, 2 existing testing-backend failures,
and 11 ignored. The full shell library reached 678 passed, 81 existing
fixture, real-surface, profiling, and lifecycle failures, and 130 ignored.
The directly touched shell child-surface suite passed; the popover and backend
lifecycle slices retained their existing fixture/lifecycle failures.

## 2026-08-26 — Replace split surface field lists with a revisioned semantic policy diff

`working tree` · area: surface policy, shell reload, and presentation configuration

`mesh-core-surface-policy` now owns normalized `SurfacePolicySnapshot` values,
typed semantic `SurfacePolicyDiff` actions, and a monotonic
`SurfacePolicyGenerator`. Settings resolution, shell reloads, keyboard and
position overrides, parent/child surface rendering, and direct keyboard-mode
requests all lower through the shared contract. Presentation `SurfaceConfig`
uses the same diff for role-aware handling of blur, decorations, padding,
geometry, keyboard mode, and role transitions, while accepted configs carry a
revision that is reused across retries and advanced only after a semantic
change is configured successfully.

**Verification.** The surface-policy suite passed 4 tests, surface-config
passed 35, the presentation configuration slice passed 27, shell surface
layout passed 21, window-role passed 9, and props passed 16 with 2 ignored.
`cargo test --workspace --no-run`, `cargo check --workspace`, formatting, and
`git diff --check` passed. The full presentation library reached 109 passed,

`lifecycle_events_are_typed_and_testing_teardown_is_idempotent` and
`unconfigured_surface_returns_typed_not_ready_without_recording_present`.
No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-26 — Build the surface policy compiler

`working tree` · area: surface policy contracts and transition planning

`mesh-core-surface-policy` now compiles a validated `DeclaredSurfaceContract`
and sparse normalized `SurfacePolicyPatch` into an `EffectiveSurfacePolicy`
with a revisioned snapshot, provenance, and explicit policy diagnostics.
`SurfacePolicyCompiler::plan` produces a typed `SurfaceTransitionPlan` for
keep, reject, measurement, input-region, layer/window live updates, role

readiness. Surface settings resolution now exposes the declared and effective

**Verification.** The surface-policy suite passed 6 tests, surface-config

`cargo test --workspace --no-run`, formatting, and `git diff --check` passed.

`lifecycle_events_are_typed_and_testing_teardown_is_idempotent` and
`unconfigured_surface_returns_typed_not_ready_without_recording_present`.
