## 2026-08-26 — Restore live navigation icons and interaction

Bundled navigation icons now resolve and rasterize synchronously on a first
render instead of showing missing-icon placeholders while the asynchronous
cache worker catches up. Frontend service effects now carry the catalog/runtime
revision required for live service calls. Structured runtime props survive
component lowering, locale translator replacement invalidates cached template
expressions, and bound component events retain their namespaced handlers.

Promoted popovers now have focus-aware eligibility and bounds fallbacks for
child surfaces. Their descendants can receive pointer and keyboard input while
the promoted wrapper remains out of normal layout, so fit-sized navigation
controls no longer overlap or grow to include popup content. The shipped
navigation bar consequently restores icon pixels, button clicks, scroll input,
keyboard activation, hover promotion, and tooltip inheritance.

**Verification.** `cargo check -p mesh-tools-cli`, formatting, and
`git diff --check` passed without compiler warnings. The focused shipped
navigation suite passed 13 tests, the navigation interaction/layout suite
passed 13 tests, the brightness suite passed 3 tests, and the scripting suite
passed 253 tests with 26 ignored. A direct startup smoke run compiled all
previously skipped frontend modules without source/template/runtime errors; it
was limited only by this environment's missing Wayland compositor and
read-only IPC socket directory. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-26 — Harden icon selection and live pointer routing

`@mesh/navigation-bar`, its language/theme popovers, Quick Settings, and the
settings surface now opt out of the shell-selected XDG icon theme when they
declare the prepared Material Symbols pack. This prevents a user-selected
system theme such as `breeze` from winning resolution and producing the
crossed-box missing-icon marker when its file asset cannot be rasterized.

Wayland surface lookup now falls back from the object-id index to a comparison
of live surface proxies, so a role replacement cannot silently lose pointer
enter/motion/press events due to a stale index entry. Unknown pointer objects
and shell surface-target misses now emit focused hover diagnostics.

**Verification.** The resource explanation resolves all required navigation
icons to prepared Material Symbols glyphs. `cargo check -p
mesh-core-presentation -p mesh-tools-cli --bin mesh-shell`, the focused shell
Wayland suite, the presentation configuration slice (27 tests), formatting,
and `git diff --check` passed. Live compositor input remains unverified in
this environment because no Wayland compositor is available. No performance
claim is made.

## 2026-08-26 — Recover pointer ownership before live hover routing

The Wayland pointer path now validates the object-id-to-seat mapping against
the live `wl_pointer` proxy and repairs it by proxy comparison when capability
teardown/recreation leaves the fast index stale. Surface proxy recovery now
repairs its object-id index as well. Pointer capability acquisition failures
and dropped frames/events also produce focused hover diagnostics instead of
silently making every CSS hover and tooltip appear inactive.

**Verification.** `cargo check -p mesh-core-presentation -p mesh-core-shell`,
the focused shell hover suite (25 passed), the parent-input geometry test, and
`cargo fmt --all -- --check` passed. The full presentation library still has
the two existing testing-backend failures
(`lifecycle_events_are_typed_and_testing_teardown_is_idempotent` and
`unconfigured_surface_returns_typed_not_ready_without_recording_present`).
Live compositor input remains unverified in this environment because no
Wayland compositor is available. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Enforce the Luau sandbox frame-time budget

`SandboxConfig::frame_budget_us` was declared and defaulted but nothing read
it, leaving the instruction-checkpoint budget as the only guard against a
single checkpoint interval (one loop back-edge or call boundary) doing
unbounded work. The shared Luau checkpoint interrupt in
`mesh-core-scripting`'s `ResourceBudget::checkpoint` now also tracks elapsed
wall-clock time since the callback began and aborts with a `frame-time`
`ResourceLimit` once it exceeds `frame_budget_us`.

Blocking host calls that already carry their own timeout — `mesh.exec`'s
child-process wait, bounded by `child_process_timeout_ms` — must not also be
charged against the frame budget, since the two limits express different
things (script CPU time vs. an external process's own budget). Added
`ResourceBudget::pause_frame_clock`/`FramePause` (exposed publicly as
`ResourceBroker::pause_frame_clock`/`FramePauseLease`) as an RAII pause that
banks elapsed time and stops the clock for the duration of the guard; `run_exec`
now wraps its blocking wait in this guard.

**Default value.** The original 4ms default fired on legitimate, non-looping
script execution (bundled backend `start()`/`on_poll` callbacks, e.g. the
shipped `pulseaudio-audio` and `hyprland-wm` scripts) whenever the interpreter
ran under ordinary `cargo test` scheduling contention — reproduced consistently
running the full `mesh-core-scripting` suite (parallel test threads), with
overruns of 4.0–6.2ms against the 4ms budget on scripts that pass in under
1ms when run in isolation or in a `--release` build. Raised the default to
50ms (`frame_budget_us: 50_000`) to keep the guard meaningful against a truly
runaway checkpoint interval while tolerating normal scheduler jitter; verified
by 3 repeated full runs of `cargo test -p mesh-core-scripting` with zero
frame-time-budget failures (one unrelated pre-existing GC-timing flake in
`thread_vm_reclaims_dropped_context_environments`, reproduced identically on
`main` before this change).

**Verification.** `cargo build --workspace`, `cargo test -p mesh-core-scripting`
(257 passed, run 3x) and `cargo clippy -p mesh-core-scripting -p
mesh-core-runtime --all-targets` (no new warnings on changed lines) passed.
Added `frame_budget_aborts_a_long_running_callback_before_the_instruction_budget`
to `policy.rs` to cover the new enforcement path directly. No performance
claim beyond the timing measurements above is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Fix testing-backend regressions from the typed present/configure work

The 2026-08-23 typed-outcome work (`SurfaceMissing`/`NotReady` presence,
cache-only-on-accept configure, retained damage) landed with two of its own
tests already broken, left uncaught because `cargo test -p
mesh-core-presentation --lib` was not the suite re-run after later changes. The
backlog item asking for this behavior stayed open even though the behavior
itself — typed `configure`/`present`/`commit_surface_state` outcomes,
create-before-destroy replacement, cache-on-accept, retained damage and a
lifecycle diagnostic on `SurfaceMissing` — was already implemented and covered
in `crates/core/presentation/src/lib.rs` and
`crates/core/shell/src/shell/runtime/render/mod.rs`.

Fixed the two regressions rather than re-implementing already-shipped work:
`present_with_damage`/`commit_surface_state`'s testing backend checked "never
configured" (→ `SurfaceMissing`) before the explicit
`testing_set_surface_configured(false)` stand-in (→ `NotReady`), so a surface
marked not-yet-configured without also calling `configure()` first was
misreported as missing instead of not-ready; reordered the checks.
`lifecycle_events_are_typed_and_testing_teardown_is_idempotent` built its
engine with `testing_with_popup_support(false)` but exercised a successful
`configure_popup`, and separately tried to open a popup on a parent surface it
had just closed — both pre-date the 2026-08-23 popup-support/parent-existence
validation added the same day. Fixed the engine's popup support flag and
reconfigured the parent before opening the popup.

**Verification.** `cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation --lib` (112 passed, 11
ignored), `cargo test -p mesh-core-shell surface_layout` (21 passed), `cargo
check --workspace`, `cargo fmt --check`, and `git diff --check` all passed. No
performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Confirm versioned/identity-safe popup reposition is already shipped

The backlog carried an open item (Section 14 audit finding #5, audited
2026-08-20) asking to gate `xdg_popup.reposition` by negotiated protocol
version and validate popup role, parent, identity, and reparenting before
reusing an existing surface id. Tracing the current
`crates/core/presentation/src/wayland_surface/backend/surfaces.rs` found all
of it already implemented, apparently landed after the audit without closing
the backlog line:

- `reposition_popup` (`surfaces.rs:593-630`) checks
  `NegotiatedCapabilities::supports_xdg_popup_reposition` (`lib.rs:272-275`,
  `xdg_shell_version >= 3`) before building a positioner or calling
  `role.popup.reposition`, returning `ProtocolUnsupported` otherwise.
- `validate_existing_popup` (`surfaces.rs:547-591`), called from
  `configure_popup` before any existing surface id is reused, rejects reuse of
  a non-popup role, rejects reparenting (`parent_id != config.parent_surface_id`),
  rejects a missing or now-popup parent, and rejects a parent whose
  `surface_generation().object` no longer matches the generation captured at
  the popup's creation (i.e. the parent compositor object was replaced).
- A rejected reposition from a real v1/v2 compositor is caught via the
  `pending_reposition_token` correlation in `PopupHandler::configure`
  (`handlers.rs:1078-1091`) and the shell recreates the popup
  (`crates/core/shell/src/shell/runtime/render/child.rs:400-424`).

Coverage already exists:
`negotiated_capabilities_clamp_versions_and_gate_popup_reposition` and
`popup_reposition_tokens_are_nonzero_and_never_wrap`
(`crates/core/presentation/src/wayland_surface/backend/tests/entry.rs:126-146`),
and `child_popup_retries_after_rejected_reposition`
(`crates/core/shell/src/shell/tests/child_surface.rs:447-479`).

No code changed; the backlog line was stale.

**Verification.** `cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation --lib` (112 passed, 11
ignored), `cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib -- shell::tests::child_surface
shell::tests::popover` (37 passed, 2 pre-existing unrelated failures —
`dismissed_legacy_promoted_popover_hides_surface_state` and
`promoted_popover_config_uses_content_size_not_stale_surface_size`, both
about popover content-size/visibility bookkeeping, not reposition/version/
identity — reproduced on an unmodified tree, matching the flakiness/known-gap
note in `STATUS.md`), and `cargo check --workspace` all passed. No
performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Close stale per-seat input ownership/teardown backlog item, already shipped

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation input lifecycle

The backlog carried "Make input ownership per seat and cancel pointer, touch,
gesture, focus, and repeat transactions during teardown." Tracing
`crates/core/presentation/src/wayland_surface/state.rs` and `handlers.rs`
found all of it already implemented and logged under three 2026-08-23 entries
above ("Route presentation input ownership per seat", "Cancel input when seat
capabilities disappear", "Cancel single-seat input during surface teardown"),
apparently never closed on the backlog line:

- `State::input_seats: HashMap<SeatId, SeatInputState>` plus
  `input_seat_order` (`state.rs:237-238`) holds pointer, keyboard, touch,
  gesture, focus-grab, and keyboard-repeat ownership per seat, with
  `pointer_seats`/`keyboard_seats`/`touch_seats`/`swipe_seats`/`pinch_seats`/
  `hold_seats`/`focus_grab_seats` indexes resolving the owning seat from each
  protocol object id.
- `teardown_surface_with_focus` (`state.rs:816-831`) calls
  `cancel_surface_input` (`state.rs:837-864`), which walks every seat and
  cancels pointer focus/gesture, keyboard focus/mods/repeat, touch ids, and
  text-input state scoped to the surface being removed, then releases the
  surface's focus grab, before the surface identity is dropped.
- `SeatHandler::remove_capability` (`handlers.rs:415-462`) cancels the
  matching input class (`cancel_pointer_input_for_seat`,
  `cancel_touch_input_for_seat`, `cancel_keyboard_input_for_seat`) and
  destroys the associated gesture/pointer/touch/keyboard protocol objects
  before releasing them; `remove_seat` (`handlers.rs:464-474`) calls
  `cancel_all_input_for_seat` plus `release_focus_grab_for_seat_teardown`.
- Coverage already exists: `input_capability_tests::{pointer,touch,keyboard}_capability_cancellation_*`
  and `input_teardown_tests::surface_input_cancellation_*` /
  `seat_scoped_surface_cancellation_preserves_another_seat_queue`
  (`state.rs`, `input_capability_tests`/`input_teardown_tests` modules).

No code changed; the backlog line was stale.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation
--lib` (112 passed, 11 ignored, 0 failed). No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Reap the clipboard child on a failed write; confirm the rest of the SHM/attach/connection-loss finding is already shipped

Section 14 finding 10 (`.planning/log/sections/14-wayland-platform-and-presentation/improvements.md:277-284`)
asked for four things: validated buffer length/stride/scale before copy,
propagated attach/region errors, connection loss surfaced as a lifecycle
failure, and the clipboard child reaped on every exit path.

Re-checking the current tree against that finding, three of the four were
already shipped by earlier work, not reflected back onto the backlog line:

- `copy_bgra_to_canvas`/`copy_bgra_damage_to_canvas`
  (`crates/core/presentation/src/wayland_surface/backend/damage.rs:23-58`,
  `:214-278`) already validate row/stride/length against checked arithmetic
  and return a typed `BufferCopyError`, and both callers in
  `backend/entry.rs:684-745` already propagate it as
  `PresentationError::BufferCopy` and restore the un-copied pending damage.
- `attach_shm_buffer` (`backend/entry.rs:757-830`) already propagates
  `buffer.attach_to` failure as `PresentationError::BufferAttach` before
  staging any other surface state, so a failed attach no longer gets treated
  as presented.
- `WaylandSurfaceBackend::dispatch_available`/`wait_for_events`
  (`backend/events.rs`) already turn a zero-byte read, a fatal Wayland I/O
  error, or a failed poll into `connection_lost`, which marks
  `state.mark_connection_lost` and returns `PresentationError::ConnectionLost`
  instead of silently retrying.

The one gap that still existed: `WaylandClipboard::write_text`
(`crates/core/platform/wayland/src/lib.rs`) returned early with `?` on a
stdin `write_all` failure before calling `child.wait()`, leaving the spawned
clipboard helper (`wl-copy` by default) running/unreaped whenever the write
failed (e.g. the helper exited or closed its pipe early). Fixed: the write
result is captured, `stdin` is dropped either way so the child observes EOF,
and on a write error the child is now killed and waited on before the error
is returned; the success path still waits and checks the exit status as
before. Added `WaylandClipboard::with_command` (for tests) and four unit
tests in `crates/core/platform/wayland/src/lib.rs`, including
`write_text_reaps_child_on_broken_pipe`, which scans `/proc` for zombie
children of the current process before/after a forced broken-pipe write
(payload larger than the pipe buffer piped into `true`, which exits without
reading) and fails without the fix (`left: 1, right: 0`) — confirmed by
temporarily reverting the fix and re-running.

**Verification.** `cargo test -p mesh-core-wayland --lib` (4 passed, 0
failed). `cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation --lib` (112 passed, 11
ignored, 0 failed). `cargo clippy -p mesh-core-wayland --lib --tests` (clean).
`cargo build --workspace` (clean). No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Add the focused live compositor matrix; confirm the deterministic simulator half is already shipped

`working tree` · area: presentation testing backend

Section 14 finding 11 asked to replace the `Testing` presentation backend
("the testing backend is a recorder, not a presentation lifecycle model")
with a deterministic protocol-state simulator, plus a focused live compositor
matrix for behavior the simulator cannot prove. Auditing
`crates/core/presentation/src/lib.rs` found the simulator half already built
incrementally across prior sessions: `PresentationEngine`'s `testing_*` API
now injects configure/popup-configure failure, close/dismiss/connection-loss
events, frame-pending and buffer-backpressure gating, scale and output
membership changes with generation tracking, missing-surface reporting, and a
full text-input-v3 transaction lifecycle (2026-08-23). `configure`,
`configure_popup`, `present_with_damage`, and `commit_surface_state` on the
`Testing` backend model the unconfigured/not-ready/missing states and popup
capability/parent/identity checks the finding called out as absent — none of
that remained to build.

What was genuinely missing was the live half: no test in the tree opened a
real Wayland connection through `WaylandSurfaceBackend`. Added
`crates/core/presentation/tests/live_compositor_matrix.rs`, an integration
test binary that drives `PresentationEngine` (forced to the `layer-shell`
backend) against whatever compositor is on `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`: a layer surface
configure-to-present round trip, `destroy_surface` making a later present
report `SurfaceMissing` instead of resurrecting state, and `configure_popup`
rejecting an orphan popup with no live parent surface. Each test skips itself
(passes trivially with a printed reason) when `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` is unset,
matching the existing environment-owned-limitation pattern documented
elsewhere in this log and in `.planning/STATUS.md`, rather than hanging or
failing where no compositor exists.

**Left open.** No Wayland compositor (headless or otherwise) is available in
this sandbox, so the three new tests have only been verified to compile and
to skip cleanly here; they have not yet been run against a live compositor.
The rest of finding 11's unconstrained direction (typed capability reporting,
atomic role changes, per-seat input, reactive popups, direct
SHM/dmabuf/GPU frame contracts) remains its own backlog item ("Build the
transactional, capability-aware presentation engine...").

**Verification.** `cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation --lib` (112 passed, 0
failed, 11 ignored). `cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation --test
live_compositor_matrix` (3 passed — skip path only, no live compositor in
this environment). `cargo check --workspace` (clean). `cargo fmt -p
mesh-core-presentation` (clean after formatting the new test file). `git diff
--check` (clean). No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Close stale IME/text-input-v3 backlog item, already shipped

`working tree` · area: Wayland presentation text input / shell input pipeline

The backlog carried "Complete IME/text-input-v3 support with live
compositor/lifecycle coverage; per-seat objects, atomic `done` transactions,
deletion, surrounding-text publication, inline preedit projection,
composition decoration/cursor placement, stale text-input teardown
cancellation, and deterministic state publication are now wired." Tracing the
implementation found every listed piece already landed, with no remaining
gap in the code:

- Per-seat `zwp_text_input_v3` objects are created in `ensure_text_input_for_seat`
  (`crates/core/presentation/src/wayland_surface/state.rs:266-285`) and torn
  down on seat capability loss (`handlers.rs:465-474`) and surface teardown
  (`clear_text_input_for_surface`, `state.rs:49-63`, invoked from
  `cancel_surface_input`).
- `PendingTextInput` buffers `preedit_string`, `commit_string`, and
  `delete_surrounding_text` and only turns them into one
  `DevWindowEvent::TextInputEdit` on the protocol's `done` event
  (`handlers.rs:1185-1193`, `state.rs:81-99`), so a `done` group commits
  atomically.
- `TextInputState` surrounding-text publication is applied per seat in
  `apply_text_input_state` (`state.rs:287-319`), replayed on `enter`, and
  compared against the last-applied state to avoid redundant
  `set_surrounding_text`/`commit` round-trips (deterministic state
  publication).
- The shell composes preedit into the input's live value
  (`compose_input_value`,
  `crates/core/shell/src/shell/component/runtime_tree/annotate.rs:188-211`),
  projecting `_mesh_preedit_start/end/cursor_begin/cursor_end` attributes.
  `build_input_preedit`
  (`crates/core/frontend/render/src/display_list/paint_node.rs:224-241`)
  turns those into a validated `DisplayInputPreedit`, and
  `surface/painter/widgets.rs:55-140` paints the composition underline and
  places the caret at the composition cursor when the input is focused
  (inline preedit projection plus composition decoration/cursor placement).
- Stale-teardown cancellation and atomic-transaction coverage already exist
  in `wayland_surface::state::text_input_tests` (surface teardown clearing
  ownership/pending edits while preserving other surfaces' transactions,
  atomic `done` commit, nullable empty preedit) and
  `shell::component::tests::interaction::policy::text_input_v3_transaction_applies_delete_and_commit_once`.

No code changed; the backlog line was stale. The separate, still-open backlog
item for the unconstrained transactional presentation engine (capability
versioning, reactive popups, multi-output membership, per-seat input,
SHM/dmabuf/GPU frame contracts) remains — this closes only the text-input-v3
scope.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation
--lib` (112 passed, 0 failed, 11 ignored). `cargo test -p mesh-core-shell
--lib text_input` and `interaction::policy`/`interaction::keybinds` targets
(4 passed, 0 failed). `cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation --test
live_compositor_matrix` (3 passed — skip path only, no live compositor in
this sandbox). No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Close the Section 14 transactional presentation engine backlog line

The backlog's Section 14 checklist still carried its original umbrella item:
"Build the transactional, capability-aware presentation engine with version
reporting, reactive popups, multi-output membership, per-seat input, and
shared SHM/dmabuf/GPU frame contracts." Checked each of the audit's 12
severity-ranked findings against the current tree instead of assuming the
line was still open, per the backlog's own "verify an older item" rule.

All 12 are already shipped, by work spread across prior sessions that closed
narrower stale sub-items without deleting this umbrella line:

- **#1 failed configure cached as success**: `WaylandSurfaceBackend::configure`
  now creates the replacement role before destroying the old one and returns
  `Result<(), PresentationError>` (`backend/surfaces.rs:149-188`); the shell
  propagates it with `.map_err(ShellRunError::Presentation)?` and only writes
  `last_surface_config` after success (`shell/runtime/render/mod.rs:454-457`).
- **#2 compositor close bypasses teardown**: layer `closed` and popup `done`
  both route through `teardown_surface_tree_after_compositor_event` /
  `teardown_surface_after_compositor_event` and emit typed lifecycle events
  (`wayland_surface/handlers.rs:188-207`, `:1093-1101`).
- **#3 config fingerprint omits blur/namespace/decorations**: presentation
  change detection now compares the lowered layer namespace (including blur)
  and xdg decoration negotiation, and a successful configure resets
  `last_region_state` so region state cannot survive a replaced object
  (`shell/runtime/render/mod.rs:459`).
- **#4 click-grab popups open without a grab**: `PendingPopupGrab` carries the
  triggering seat/serial from the originating press through popup
  construction to `xdg_popup.grab`, with a visible downgrade log when the
  serial/seat is stale or missing (`backend/surfaces.rs:478-495`).
- **#5 popup update assumes v3 / no identity check**: reposition is gated on
  `NegotiatedCapabilities::supports_xdg_popup_reposition`, and
  `validate_existing_popup` rejects role/parent mismatches before treating an
  id as an existing popup (`backend/surfaces.rs:380-393`, `:547-601`).
- **#6 dynamic layer size mismatch**: `present_with_damage` and the input/
  extent queries all resolve through the same `entry.resolved_extent`
  (`backend/present.rs:120,237,295`).
- **#7 region-only changes have no guaranteed commit**: confirmed already
  closed 2026-08-27 earlier today (this file, above).
- **#8 SHM/frame-callback hot retry**: `waiting_for_frame_callback` uses a
  bounded `MAX_FRAME_CALLBACK_WAIT` deadline, separate from
  `waiting_for_buffer_release`, which only clears on an observed
  `wl_buffer.release` via `refresh_buffer_backpressure`
  (`backend/entry.rs:849-868`).
- **#9 stale input ownership on removal**: per-seat `input_seats` map plus
  teardown tests (`input_teardown_tests::*`) cover cancellation of pointer,
  keyboard, touch, and gesture ownership on surface/seat removal.
- **#10 silent buffer/attach/connection-loss failures**: closed by the
  2026-08-27 clipboard-reap entry (this file, above); buffer copy helpers
  return `BufferCopyError` instead of silently truncating
  (`backend/damage.rs:23-51`).
- **#11 testing backend is a recorder, not a lifecycle model**: the
  deterministic `Testing` backend simulates configure/popup failure
  injection, close/dismiss/connection-loss, frame pacing, buffer
  backpressure, and scale/output generations, plus a focused live compositor
  matrix (`crates/core/presentation/tests/live_compositor_matrix.rs`) added
  2026-08-27.
- **#12 input correctness gaps**: `is_non_repeating_key` now matches
  `"escape"`/`"Esc"` case-insensitively so Escape no longer repeats
  (`wayland_surface/state.rs:1351-1365`); pointer events carry the real
  button code and press identity (`handlers.rs:590-611`), and text-input-v3
  IME composition shipped in the prior 2026-08-27 entry.

The bullet's "shared SHM/dmabuf/GPU frame contracts" phrase is the one part
not literally done — only SHM buffers exist today. That is intentionally
tracked as its own separate item ("GPU rendering backend after retained
layout, smart invalidation, and damage tracking ship → v1.25") rather than
being part of this line's scope; `SurfaceGeneration.buffer` already gives a
generation-keyed hook a future dmabuf/GPU buffer type can use without
redesigning this engine.

No code changed. Deleted the Section 14 umbrella checklist bullet from
`docs/BACKLOG.md`.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-presentation
--lib` (112 passed, 0 failed, 11 ignored). No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Restore the durable active-profile pointer on a partial profile-switch commit failure

Started on Section 15 finding #1 ("profile activation is not one atomic
runtime generation," audited 2026-08-20). The audited code no longer matches
the current tree — `crates/core/shell/src/shell/profile.rs` has been
substantially rewritten since the audit: candidate graph/settings/locale/
resources/backends are now fully prepared and validated (including mounting
frontend components and buffering initial backend service state) before
`commit_pending_profile_switch` runs, which is much closer to the audit's
target `Preparing -> Ready -> Committing` shape than the audited snapshot
described.

One concrete gap from the finding remains and is now fixed: `commit_pending_
profile_switch` wrote the durable `active-profile` pointer
(`paths.set_active`) before the still-fallible `commit_resource_snapshot`
step. A failure in that step aborted the in-memory candidate correctly
(`abort_profile_candidate`) but left the on-disk pointer already advanced to
the profile the running shell never actually adopted — a restart could then
select a profile revision the live shell had rejected, the same durable/
runtime split finding #1 called out for node-slot edits.

`ProfilePaths::restore_active` (`extension/module/src/package/profile.rs`)
now reverts the pointer to a captured previous value, or removes it when
there was none, and `commit_pending_profile_switch` calls it on a resource-
commit failure before aborting.

**Not done.** This is one narrow slice of finding #1, not the finding. Two
things it explicitly calls for are still open:

- The profile's `entrypoint` field (`ShellProfile` root instances,
  `extension/module/src/package/profile.rs:58-59`, default `"main"`) is still
  never read by root retention/selection in `discovery.rs` or
  `continue_profile_switch`/`commit_pending_profile_switch` (both compare
  only `root.module`). Investigated further than the audit did: the frontend
  catalog only compiles and indexes one primary component per module today
  (`FrontendCatalog::top_level_surfaces`), so there is no second entrypoint
  for a changed `entrypoint` value to select — honoring the field is a
  catalog-level multi-entrypoint feature, not a comparison fix, and is out of
  scope for this entry.
- Theme/locale/component-settings refresh failures *after* the pointer/
  resource commit still only `tracing::warn!` instead of rolling back
  (`profile.rs:1136`, `:1149`, `:1156`); by that point roots and backends are
  already installed in-memory, so a real fix needs the full candidate/commit
  redesign the finding describes, not a local patch.

Backlog item left open; this is progress within it, not a completion.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-module
restore_active` (2 passed). `nix develop --command cargo test -p
mesh-core-shell --lib profile` (10 passed, 2 ignored — release-only
benchmarks). No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Enabling a backend module now actually starts it live

Section 15 finding #2 ("backend module enable/disable changes persistence
but not the live backend set," audited 2026-08-20). Verified still open:
`apply_set_module_enabled` in `crates/core/shell/src/shell/runtime/
request.rs` only ran a live-runtime branch for `ModuleKind::Frontend`; every
other kind (backend) persisted the graph decision, reloaded the graph, and
returned — the process was never spawned until the next restart or an
unrelated provider switch. The disable side turned out to already be safe:
the pre-existing rejection guard blocks disabling a module while the current
graph reports it as the active provider for any interface, so there was
nothing live left running to stop by the time that branch could be reached.

Fixed the enable side. `apply_set_module_enabled` now computes, from the
just-loaded candidate graph and before committing it,
`newly_active_backend_interfaces(&graph, module_id)`: the interfaces where
the module is the graph's active provider but has no live
`backend_runtimes` entry yet. After commit, `spawn_newly_enabled_backend_
provider` launches each one through the same staged-activation path startup
and profile switches already use (`spawn_backend_candidate` ->
`stage_backend_runtime_activation`), so it becomes the live command handler
once it reports `Running` (and, where required, a valid initial service
snapshot) via the existing `handle_backend_lifecycle` ->
`complete_backend_runtime_switch` flow — no new commit/generation machinery
needed since that machinery already existed for provider switches, just
wasn't wired to the enable path.

**Not tested live.** `newly_active_backend_interfaces` (the graph/state
query) has a direct unit test. The actual spawn (`spawn_backend_candidate`
onward) requires `Shell::backend_respawn`, which only the real shell run
loop ever populates (`runtime/mod.rs:280`) — no existing test in this suite
exercises that path for *any* live backend spawn, including the pre-existing
provider-switch one this reuses, so building that harness is out of scope
here.

Backlog item (Section 15, "reconcile backend enable/disable... through the
same activation coordinator") stays open: this closes the concrete "enable
never spawns" gap from finding #2, not the broader typed-activation-diff
architecture the item describes.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib
backend_lifecycle::` and `module_config::` (27 passed, 3 failed — the same 3
failures reproduce on the pre-change tree via `git stash`, confirmed
unrelated/pre-existing flaky baseline per `.planning/STATUS.md`). No
performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Bound CoreRequest drain so a self-emitting cycle cannot hang the shell

Section 15 finding #8 ("transitive CoreRequest processing is unbounded and
re-entrant across frame phases," audited 2026-08-20). Verified still open:
`Shell::drain_requests` (`crates/core/shell/src/shell/runtime/request.rs`)
had no count, time, size, or cycle limit — a component reacting to a service
snapshot by re-requesting the same change (the audit's concrete example:
`mesh.theme`'s `SetTheme` handler always republishes theme state, which can
re-trigger the same request) could grow the queue without bound and hang the
shell loop before presentation.

Added a hard ceiling: `MAX_DRAINED_REQUESTS_PER_BATCH` (4096). Exceeding it
drops the remaining batch (not silently — via
`tracing::error!` and a `request_drain_budget_exceeded` diagnostic on
`@mesh/shell`) and returns instead of continuing to drain. This guarantees
termination; it does not implement the audit's fuller design (per-source
causal transaction IDs, cycle detection, fair residual continuation across
frames) — it is the safety backstop the finding asks for, not the scheduler
redesign.

Backlog item stays open.

**Verification.** New `crates/core/shell/src/shell/tests/request_drain.rs`:
a 10,000-request batch terminates and diagnoses instead of hanging; an
8-request batch drains completely with no diagnostic. `nix develop --command
cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib` (745 passed, 23 failed — identical
failure set with and without this change, confirmed via `git stash`; matches
the documented flaky baseline). No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Isolate one component's service-event handler failure from the rest of delivery

Section 15 finding #6 ("component and reload errors can terminate the whole
shell instead of isolating the failing module," audited 2026-08-20).
Verified still open at the service-event delivery seam:
`Shell::deliver_service_event`
(`crates/core/shell/src/shell/runtime/service_state.rs`) propagated any one
component's `handle_service_event`/`handle_service_event_with_generation`
error with `?`, aborting delivery to every other component still queued in
that pass and bubbling `ShellRunError::Component` all the way to `Shell::run`
(`runtime/mod.rs`), which has no supervisor above it — the whole shell
process would exit. This directly matches the finding's failure mode: an
unrelated healthy module goes dark because a different module's Luau
callback threw.

All four delivery loops in `deliver_service_event` (fallback + indexed
`Updated`, fallback + indexed `InterfaceEvent`) now catch the per-component
error, record it as a `service_event_delivery` lifecycle diagnostic against
that component's module id (reusing the existing `DiagnosticsCollector`
path backends already use), and continue to the next component instead of
returning early. This is one seam of finding #6, not the full "supervisor +
Errored placeholder + bounded restart/quarantine" architecture — `tick`,
`render`, and reload paths (`runtime/reload.rs`, `runtime/theme.rs`,
`runtime/wayland.rs`, `discovery.rs`) still propagate component errors with
`?` and are unaddressed follow-up within this same backlog item.

Also added `IndexedRecordingState::fail_handling` (test-only fault
injection) to the shared `IndexedRecordingComponent` test fixture so a
future test in this area can simulate a broken component's handler without
a new fixture.

Backlog item stays open.

**Verification.** New
`one_component_service_handler_failure_does_not_stop_delivery_to_others` in
`crates/core/shell/src/shell/tests/service_delivery.rs`: a failing
component's handler error does not propagate, the failing component is not
recorded as having handled the event, an unrelated healthy component still
receives it, and the failure is recorded as a diagnosable issue. `nix
develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib` (744 passed, 25
failed — two more than the 23-failure baseline,
`resource_preparation_job_cancels_and_polls_without_blocking_the_caller` and
`theme_service_state_lists_every_registered_theme`, both pass individually
under `--lib <name>`, confirming parallel-run flakiness rather than a
regression). No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Watcher death now falls back to polling immediately instead of a 24h blind spot

Section 15 finding #7 ("watch coverage is a startup snapshot, while
fallback polling is parked for 24 hours," audited 2026-08-20). Verified
still open: `spawn_file_watcher`/`watch_thread`
(`crates/core/shell/src/shell/file_watch.rs`) is a one-shot inotify thread
over the startup path list; when it dies (init failure, zero watched
directories, or a read error) it just returns, and `file_watcher_active`
(set once at startup, `runtime/mod.rs:278`) never reverts to `false`. With
it `true`, `reload_frontend_components_if_changed` and
`reload_locale_if_settings_changed` park their next poll at
`FILE_WATCHER_RELOAD_PARK` (24h, `runtime/mod.rs:17`) — so a dead watcher
left the shell blind to component/theme/settings edits for up to a day.

Fixed the dead-watcher half of this (not the fuller "managed
generation-aware `WatchSet` that reconciles on every graph/catalog/profile/
theme/locale/import change" the finding describes — new watch directories
from a live import/graph change still never get bound, which remains open).
The watch thread now sends a new `ShellMessage::FileWatcherStopped` on every
exit path (init failure, no watchable directories, reader error) instead of
silently returning. The shell loop clears `file_watcher_active` and calls
the existing `schedule_reload_checks_now()` on receipt, so the very next
loop iteration falls back to the short interval pollers instead of waiting
out the 24h park.

Backlog item stays open.

**Verification.** New tests in
`crates/core/shell/src/shell/tests/commands.rs`:
`file_watcher_stopped_message_clears_active_flag_and_forces_immediate_reload_checks`
and the no-op case when the watcher was never active. `nix develop --command
cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib` (748 passed, 23 failed — identical
failure set to the documented flaky baseline). No performance claim is
made.

## 2026-08-27 — Remove the undeclared internal "set-current" command to the mesh.theme backend

Section 15 finding #10 ("control-plane state and follow-up effects can
diverge," audited 2026-08-20), third sub-bullet: the shell sent an internal,
undeclared `"set-current"` command directly to whatever module runs as the
active `mesh.theme` backend provider, even though the built-in `mesh.theme`
contract declares only public setters (`set_theme` etc.) — a module
implementing `mesh.theme` would receive a command outside its own contract.

Verified still present in `sync_theme_service_state`
(`crates/core/shell/src/shell/runtime/theme.rs`) and confirmed it is
currently dead in practice: `self.service_handlers` only gets a `mesh.theme`
entry from `replace_backend_runtime`, i.e. only if some module is running as
the active backend provider for that interface, and no shipped module
implements `mesh.theme` as a backend today (theme is core-rendered). Removed
the side-channel send; the proper typed `ServiceEvent::Updated` broadcast
immediately below it already delivers the equivalent snapshot through the
declared contract path, unchanged.

Backlog item (Section 15 finding #10) stays open — the other two
sub-findings (`apply_set_module_prop` not republishing `mesh.settings`, and
theme/locale helpers discarding returned `CoreRequest`s) are untouched.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib
theme::` (18 passed, 1 failed — `malformed_theme_reload_retains_last_known_good_snapshot`,
a pre-existing failure reproduced identically with this change stashed out).
No performance claim is made.

Note: `target/` grew to 69G during this session and filled the sandbox disk
mid-test-run (`git stash pop` failed with ENOSPC, later recovered cleanly —
no work was lost). Cleared with `rm -rf target`; worth periodic `cargo
clean` in long sessions like this one.

## 2026-08-27 — Surface graph-supplied interface contract compile failures instead of dropping them silently

Backlog item "Service contracts (open)": `InterfaceRegistry::register_contract`
discards the compile error, so one bad field type on a module-supplied
interface contract removed the whole interface from the catalog and every
method on it then rejected as an unknown channel with no diagnostic
pointing at the cause. Built-in contracts already went through
`register_builtin_contract` (logs + `debug_assert!`) by prior work; graph
contracts registered in `Shell::register_interfaces_from_graph`
(`crates/core/shell/src/shell/discovery.rs`) were still calling the
error-discarding `register_contract` — confirmed still true by reading the
current source, matching the backlog note exactly.

Switched that call site to `try_register_contract` and, on failure, log
`tracing::error!` (visible without a debugger) and record a
`graph_interface_contract_compile_failed` lifecycle diagnostic keyed by the
interface name (module-owning identity is not threaded through
`InstalledModuleGraph::interface_contracts()` at this point, so the
interface name is what's actually actionable here). Deliberately no
`debug_assert!`, unlike the built-in path — a graph-supplied contract is
third-party content that can be legitimately malformed; a shell built from
one bad community module must not panic in debug builds.

Deleted the "Service contracts (open)" backlog item — this was its entire
scope.

**Verification.** New
`malformed_graph_interface_contract_is_diagnosed_not_silently_dropped` in
`crates/core/shell/src/shell/tests/debug.rs`: an interface contract with a
duplicate method name compiles-fails, the interface stays absent from the
catalog (unchanged behavior), and the failure is now recorded as a
diagnosable issue (new). `nix develop --command cargo test -p
mesh-core-shell --lib` (748 passed, 24 failed — every failure in the
already-confirmed flaky baseline, including
`phase26_real_surface_baseline_emits_canonical_proof_measurements`
independently reproduced on the pre-change tree earlier in this session). No
performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Tag backend transport with activation generations and provider epochs

Completed the Section 15 backend transport identity item. Added the shared
`BackendIdentity` (`activation_generation` plus a per-interface
`provider_epoch`) to backend runtime slots, command controls, script events,
service updates, lifecycle records, interface events, command results, shell
bridge messages, service-state routes, and restart deadlines. Retained backend
runtimes are retagged at activation commit; stale identities are rejected
before state publication, interface-event delivery, command settlement,
health transitions, or supervised restart.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` passed;

passed (36), and the isolated scripting GC regression passed (1). Scoped

`mesh-core-scripting --lib` run reached 256 passed, 1 failed, and 26 ignored
in both runs because of the documented pre-existing
`thread_vm_reclaims_dropped_context_environments` GC-timing flake; that test

documented unrelated failures: `backend_lifecycle` 30 passed/2 failed,
`service_state` 27 passed/1 failed, and `commands` 9 passed/1 failed. No
performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Make frontend and backend teardown explicit and idempotent

Completed the Section 15 lifecycle cleanup item. Frontend component runtimes
now track mounted state so shell teardown invokes unmount once even when drop,
profile replacement, reload, and shutdown overlap. Frontend unmount storage is
flushed even when authored teardown fails. Backend stop paths close command
ingress for the authored `stop` hook, retain service and bridge task handles,
and join them with a bounded abort fallback. Resource preparation workers,
file-watch threads, IPC server/client tasks, restart deadlines, and the shell
wake fd now have explicit shutdown ownership and ordering.

The runtime setup and event loop share one cleanup path for normal and error
exits. Shutdown disables respawn, stops ingress, broadcasts shutdown, unmounts
components, joins pending workers and backends, and only then releases the
eventfd. A defensive shell drop guard covers construction paths that never
enter the main loop.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` passed;
focused shell IPC tests passed (7), frontend lifecycle reload passed (1), and
resource-worker cancellation passed (1). `cargo test -p mesh-core-backend
--lib` passed (36), and the focused scripting storage tests passed (2).
The broad `mesh-core-shell --lib` run reached 746 passed, 29 failed, and 131
ignored; its failures remain in the repository's existing broad-suite baseline
(including known rendering, fixture, graph, and profiling regressions), while
the focused lifecycle tests passed. Scoped rustfmt and `git diff --check`
passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Give detached workers owned wake handles and lifecycle guards

Completed the Section 15 detached-worker safety item. File-watch threads, IPC
server/client tasks, backend event bridges, and supervised restart deadlines
now carry an owned `WakeHandle` backed by a duplicated eventfd rather than a
raw descriptor reconstructed after the shell boundary. The shell retains the
wake owner alongside explicit worker guards, stores restart `JoinHandle`s, and
awaits cancelled restart tasks before releasing the eventfd. Existing watcher,
IPC, backend, and resource-worker guards therefore keep their wake capability
valid through cancellation and join, including defensive drop paths.

Added an IPC regression test that drops the source eventfd while a client still
owns its wake handle and verifies both message delivery and the wake counter.
No detached worker in the shell now receives a raw eventfd integer.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo check -p mesh-core-shell`
passed; focused IPC tests passed (8), file-watcher lifecycle tests passed (2),
and the backend lifecycle filter reached 31 passed with one existing invalid-
manifest fixture failure. The broad `mesh-core-shell --lib` run reached 753
passed, 23 failed, and 131 ignored; its failures remain in the repository's
existing rendering, fixture, graph, profiling, and service-state baseline.
`git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-27 — Isolate component/runtime failures with placeholders, diagnostics, and quarantine

Completed the Section 15 component-runtime supervision item. Shell callback,
service delivery, input, tick, theme, locale, settings, render/build, reload,
mount, and unmount boundaries now contain one component's `ComponentError` and
continue servicing its siblings. Failed effect batches are discarded instead
of escaping into the shell loop. Frontend components install the existing
bounded error placeholder and record typed, instance-scoped runtime
diagnostics; simpler component implementations still receive shell-side
diagnostics and supervision. Three repeated boundary failures quarantine the
component, stop its tick/event/input work, tear down child surfaces, and mark
the module failed/quarantined. A successful source reload clears the placeholder
and failure state so recovery is explicit.

The service-delivery regression now verifies repeated failures quarantine only
the broken observer while a healthy sibling continues receiving updates.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` passed;
focused shell service-delivery (8 passed/1 ignored), request-drain (2), input
(6), child-surface (25), and frontend diagnostics (15) suites passed.
`mesh-core-diagnostics` passed all 7 tests and `mesh-core-frontend-host` passed
all 5 tests. The broad `mesh-core-shell --lib` run reached 752 passed, 24
failed, and 131 ignored; the failures are the documented pre-existing fixture,
theme-registry, graph, rendering, profiling, and service-state baseline,
including the already-recorded malformed-theme-reload diagnostic assertion.
Scoped rustfmt and `git diff --check` passed; the repository-wide rustfmt check
still reports the pre-existing formatting drift in
`crates/core/extension/module/src/package/profile.rs`. No performance claim is
made.

## 2026-08-27 — Publish provider availability transitions from committed generations

Completed the Section 15 provider availability item. Backend health publication
now records the latest provider generation only when a ready runtime crosses a
commit boundary. Candidate lifecycle failures and status records left behind by
candidate cleanup remain private, while failures and explicit stops from the
committed generation publish the unavailable service state and typed `health`
event immediately. Retired identities cannot publish later stale transitions.

Recovery publishes the new committed provider generation's current state,
removes stale availability reasons, and emits one healthy transition. Repeated
health statuses from the same generation are deduplicated; cached health is
retagged when a retained runtime crosses a new activation generation so replay
cannot expose an old identity. Added lifecycle regressions for recovery, stale
failure rejection, uncommitted candidates, and repeated health deduplication.

**Verification.** The two new lifecycle regressions passed individually.
`nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib backend_lifecycle
--no-fail-fast` reached 35 passed and 1 failed; the failure is the existing
invalid-manifest package-graph fixture assertion. The focused service-state
filter reached 27 passed, 1 failed, and 4 ignored with the existing duplicate
theme identity failure; service-delivery reached 8 passed and 1 ignored.
`nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` passed. The broad shell-library
run reached 762 passed, 23 failed, and 131 ignored, including the same fixture
and theme failures plus the repository's existing graph, rendering, profiling,
UI, and command-test failures. `git diff --check` passed. Repository-wide
format checking still reports only the pre-existing drift in
`crates/core/extension/module/src/package/profile.rs`; edited files pass
targeted rustfmt. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-28 — Route control-plane writes through durable revisions and ordered effect batches

Completed the Section 15 control-plane write item. Settings, theme, icon, font,
locale, and module-property writes now prepare a complete shared or active-profile
candidate, validate the resulting theme/locale snapshot before mutation, and
cross revision-checked durable persistence before installing live state. The
effective shared/profile revision is declared on `mesh.settings`, `mesh.theme`,
and `mesh.locale` state/events. Profile activation and external settings reload
publish one ordered batch: settings, then theme, then locale; component effects
and returned CoreRequest work stay in that same order.

## 2026-08-28 — Distinguish legacy no-profile operation from configured graph/profile recovery

Completed the Section 15 composition recovery item. Shell startup now records
whether composition is intentional legacy no-profile operation, a valid
configured profile, or configured-composition recovery. Missing profile
selection remains legacy behavior; malformed profile pointers/profiles,
installed graphs, and graph-backed startup preparation fail closed into an
explicit recovery mode with lifecycle diagnostics and a published mode/reason
state. Recovery does not discover or enable configured frontends or backends,
and partial frontend registration is discarded. Invalid live graph candidates
retain the last-known-good composition and use the same validated loader and
diagnostic boundary.

## 2026-08-28 — Connect package journal commit/rollback to runtime activation

Completed the Section 15 package transaction item. Shell package install and
uninstall operations now retain their journal through resource preparation,
candidate frontend/backend startup, and the runtime activation boundary. The
journal is committed only after the candidate is ready and the active-profile
pointer has crossed its durable boundary; rejected, superseded, failed, or
shutting-down candidates explicitly abort it. Package-derived discovery,
settings, locale, theme, resource, interface, capability, and catalog mirrors
are restored from an entry snapshot when activation aborts, so disk and live
state return to the same last-known-good package transaction.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` passed;
the focused package journal tests passed (3 tests), and focused shell package
and profile tests passed (2 and 4 tests). The broad `mesh-core-shell --lib`
run reached 769 passed, 23 failed, and 131 ignored; its failures are the
repository's documented quick-settings, audio/input, rendering/profiling,
fixture, graph, popover, command rollback, backend-manifest, and
malformed/duplicate-theme baseline failures. Scoped rustfmt and `git diff
--check` passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-28 — Introduce ActiveSnapshot and ordered shell shutdown

Completed the Section 15 activation/shell-lifecycle item. Committed runtime
activations now publish an immutable `ActiveSnapshot` containing the graph,
interface catalog, effective settings and control-plane/resource revisions,
root identities, provider identities, watch generation, and presentation
generation map. Activation candidates expose a read-only `CandidatePreview`
whose prepared frontend surfaces remain hidden and outside the live component
and presentation maps until the candidate commits; candidate backend readiness
is reflected without publishing service state early. Shutdown now enters an
explicit quiescing boundary that rejects new shell work, then advances through
component, provider, presentation, worker, flushing, and stopped phases with
idempotent cleanup and residual-effect diagnostics.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo check -p mesh-core-shell`
passed; focused preview, shutdown, and activation-boundary tests passed. The
broad `mesh-core-shell --lib` run reached 772 passed, 23 failed, and 131

audio/input, rendering/profiling, fixture, graph, popover, command rollback,

rustfmt and `git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-28 — Acknowledge live profile generations and recover exact pointers

Completed the Section 16 live profile switching item. The shell's private IPC
profile switch now carries a typed response channel through resource
preparation, candidate backend readiness, rejection, shutdown, and the final
activation commit. Successful responses identify the committed profile and
activation generation; rejected responses preserve the current generation and
reason. The CLI parses those acknowledgements and only writes the active
profile pointer when the shell is absent (`ENOENT` or `ECONNREFUSED`), leaving
rejections, malformed responses, EOF, and other transport failures untouched.
Pre-commit pointer recovery is generation-safe: it restores the prior pointer
only while the candidate still owns it, including package transaction commit
failures, and does not overwrite a newer external pointer.

**Verification.** `nix develop -c cargo check -p mesh-core-shell -p
mesh-tools-cli` passed. Focused IPC tests passed (9), profile/recovery tests
passed (6), and CLI tests passed for the new acknowledgement behavior (3);
the full CLI binary suite reached 12 passed and 3 existing update/environment
failures. The broad `mesh-core-shell --lib` run reached 774 passed, 23
existing quick-settings, audio/input, rendering/profiling, fixture, graph,
popover, command rollback, backend-manifest, and malformed/duplicate-theme
failures, and 131 ignored. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` retains the existing
formatting drift in `crates/core/extension/module/src/package/profile.rs` and
`transaction.rs`; `git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

Completed the Section 16 LSP manifest/schema validation item. Parseable module

## 2026-08-28 — Convert LSP positions to UTF-16 at the protocol boundary

Completed the Section 16 LSP position-conversion item. Source byte offsets now
use one shared UTF-8-to-UTF-16 position converter for diagnostics, definitions,
JSON diagnostics, formatting edits, and semantic tokens; inbound positions
continue through the matching UTF-16-aware converter. Semantic token starts and
lengths now report UTF-16 code units, the server explicitly advertises UTF-16
position encoding, and test fixtures no longer construct protocol positions from
Unicode scalar or UTF-8 byte counts.

**Verification.** Focused utility (3), semantic-token (2), backend (1), JSON

and manifest (1) integration tests passed; workspace `cargo check` passed.

## 2026-08-28 — Enforce typed update flags and clean replace operations

Completed the Section 16 CLI flag/replace item. `mesh update` now parses a
typed target, update policy, and dry-run flag before opening a package
transaction; `--all` is explicit, invalid, unknown, duplicate, and conflicting
arguments are rejected, and stale `--merge` guidance is removed. Replace
policy records an explicit discard warning and stages the requested Git
revision even when its version/revision is unchanged, so local tracked and
untracked edits are atomically replaced by a clean tree. Lock refresh now
reads each installed module's `module.json`.

## 2026-08-28 — Make LSP analysis syntax-aware during edits

Completed the Section 16 syntax-aware LSP analysis item. Component tooling now
builds recoverable partial template and script trees for incomplete blocks,
while the shared component parser records Luau member paths and source spans
from its AST. A syntax-aware lexer fallback retains expression statements that
fallible parsing drops, without treating comments or string contents as code.
LSP diagnostics consume those parser-owned spans, preserve parse errors, and
continue reporting unknown refs, invalid members, and quoted expression
attributes while a document is malformed.

diagnostic regressions (5), and the full `mesh-core-component` library suite

failure. The affected frontend package run reached 81 passed, 20 ignored, and
one existing `phase87_layout_display_source_semantics_survive_lowering`
failure (`Loading Ready` versus `Main grid`). Scoped rustfmt and
`git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-28 — Replace heuristic Luau/JSON authoring scanners

Completed the Section 16 parser replacement item. JSON completion now consumes
standards-aware JSONC tokens with byte ranges and decoded string prefixes,
including recovery for incomplete strings. JSON schema diagnostics and
manifest field lookup now use the strict `json-syntax` parser's code map, so
Unicode escapes are decoded correctly while diagnostics retain the original
source spans. Luau completion contexts now use the `full_moon` Luau lexer for
member paths and call arguments, excluding comments and string contents while
retaining incomplete-path recovery.

## 2026-08-28 — Support workspace-folder initialization and ordered LSP refresh generations

LSP initialization now falls back to the first file-backed
`workspaceFolders` entry when a client omits `rootUri`, while preserving a
usable root URI as the preferred source. Accepted versioned document opens and
changes establish refresh generations before diagnostics are published. A
registry refresh publishes only while its generation remains current, so a
slower discovery cannot replace a newer snapshot. Document updates are also
serialized and older versions are rejected, with diagnostic publications
carrying the accepted document version.

**Verification.** Focused backend regressions passed (5), and
`nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` passed. The full
`cargo test -p mesh-tools-lsp --lib --tests` run reached 110 passed and one
pre-existing `analyzer::tests::locale_completion_matches_the_runtime_host_api`
failure. Scoped rustfmt and `git diff --check` passed. No performance claim is

## 2026-08-28 — Select manifest flavors from parsed JSON structure

Manifest flavor detection now relies exclusively on a successfully parsed
`serde_json::Value` and the `mesh` object's root-only fields. Unparseable
documents conservatively use the per-module flavor instead of scanning raw
source text, so quoted or unrelated keys in malformed input cannot activate
the root-config schema. Added a regression for that malformed-input case.

**Verification.** Focused manifest tests passed (19), and

## 2026-08-28 — Consolidate CLI and shell package transactions

Completed the duplicate package implementation item. The core package
transaction engine now owns validated local/Git source staging, root and
profile persistence, lock/provenance recording, exact-generation rollback
materialization, and journaled recovery under one OS lock. CLI install,
update, uninstall, and rollback plus the shell package service use those
shared operations; shell runtime activation retains the same journal until
prepared activation commits or restores the captured runtime state.

**Verification.** Core transaction regressions passed (14); the full

## 2026-08-28 — Enforce module and interface compatibility before activation

Completed the module dependency/interface activation backlog item. Required
module ranges now reject the affected profile closure while optional module
edges remain degraded; profile activation validates interface/provider ranges
and composition version pins before mutating the root. Composition `extends`
edges participate in the required resolver closure, and same-module duplicate
provider/interface contract identities plus required/optional interface overlap
are rejected deterministically. Provider versions must also agree with the
selected interface contract when both are declared; the specified cross-provider
inline declaration diagnostic remains intact.

**Verification.** Focused profile, resolver, manifest, and graph regressions
passed. The full `nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-module` run
reached 265 passed, 9 pre-existing failures, and 3 ignored: the navigation
manifest assertion, four content-store permission failures, three authoring or
Luau scan diagnostics, and the shipped graph fixture. `nix develop --command
cargo check -p mesh-core-module`, scoped rustfmt, and `git diff --check` passed.
No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-28 — `aspect-ratio` and container-derived navigation-bar controls

Added `aspect-ratio` to the bounded style profile so a control can take its
size from its container on one axis and stay square on the other, instead of
restating both axes per component and per breakpoint. The property parses
`auto`, `<number>`, and `<number> / <number>`, rejects non-finite and
non-positive ratios back to `auto`, resolves through the normal cascade with
variables, and lowers to Taffy's own `aspect_ratio`. `docs/css-coverage.md`
records it as implemented layout coverage.

Reworked the shipped navigation bar onto that primitive. The bar root is 40px
with 6px block padding; both clusters take `height: 100%`; and each icon
control is `height: 100%` plus `aspect-ratio: 1`. Control size is now decided
once on the bar root rather than in nine component style blocks, and the
per-breakpoint 36px/24px size overrides are gone. Controls also lost their
resting fill: they are bare glyphs whose hover, press, and popover-open states
read through color, icon weight, and a scale transform. The keyboard ring moved
from `:focus` to `:focus-visible` only, so a pointer press leaves no border
behind — `pointer_focus_visible_for_key` already refuses `focus-visible` for
buttons, so this is exactly the keyboard-only case.

The workspace pills deliberately stay px-sized: the sliding indicator is placed
by a script-computed `translateX`, so its stride needs a number both the CSS and
the Luau agree on. That constant is now named `WS_SLOT_STRIDE` with a comment
on both sides.

**Verification.** Two new regressions cover the primitive — a layout test
asserting a `height: 100%` child squares to its row and a 2:1 child doubles,
and a resolution test covering the number, fraction, `auto`, and malformed
forms. The shipped-navigation compiler test now also asserts the derived
control row measures 28px from the 40px bar minus its block padding.
`cargo test -p mesh-core-elements -p mesh-core-frontend -p mesh-core-component
-p mesh-core-module` failure sets were diffed against a stashed baseline on
`main` and are identical: 10 pre-existing `mesh-core-elements` style-fixture
and theme-variable failures, 9 pre-existing `mesh-core-module` content-store,
diagnostic, and shipped-fixture failures, and one pre-existing
`phase87_layout_display_source_semantics_survive_lowering` failure. The bar
height change also required updating that compiler test's own 56px expectation
to 40px. No performance claim is made. The shell binary was not run: the CLI
does not build in this environment because `xkbcommon` is absent from
`PKG_CONFIG_PATH`.

Backlogged the related contradiction this exposed rather than fixing it here:
`mesh.surface.exclusive_zone` and `mesh.surface.margins` restate by hand what
the root's CSS box already decides, and both had to be retuned when the bar
height changed. Design note in
[`.planning/todos/pending/2026-08-28-css-derived-surface-geometry.md`](../todos/pending/2026-08-28-css-derived-surface-geometry.md).

## 2026-08-28 — Navigation-bar centre region and slower control motion

Follow-up to the same day's navigation-bar rework, from a screenshot showing the
bar's items scattered across its full width with the clock off centre.

**Cause.** A customizable slot has no wrapper node: `render_slot` returns the
contributions and `build_widget_nodes` splices them straight into the parent
(`crates/core/frontend/compiler/src/render/mod.rs`). The start and end slots sat
inside cluster rows, but the centre slot did not, so its contributions became
direct flex children of `.nav-shell` and `justify-content: space-between` spread
them across the bar. Centring was also positional rather than guaranteed: with
`space-between`, the middle child only lands in the middle when the two side
clusters happen to measure the same width.

**Change.** The centre slot is now wrapped in a `.center-cluster` row, and the
three regions share `flex-grow: 1; flex-basis: 0`, so each is exactly one third
of the bar with the sides aligned outward and the centre aligned centre. The
clock is centred by construction rather than by coincidence. Control transitions
moved from `--animation-duration-short` (150ms) to `--animation-duration-medium`
(240ms), which was reported as too quick to read as motion.

**Verification.** The shipped-navigation compiler test now also asserts the
centre cluster's midpoint is at 480px in a 960px bar. Failure sets across
`mesh-core-elements`, `mesh-core-frontend`, `mesh-core-component`, and
`mesh-core-module` were diffed against a stashed `main` baseline and are
unchanged. No performance claim is made, and the result is still unconfirmed on
screen because the CLI does not build here without `xkbcommon`.

## 2026-08-28 — Navigation-bar controls resized outside their row: cause found

A screenshot with `shell:debug_layout_bounds` showed bar items painting outside
the region that contained them. Rather than infer further from screenshots, the
real tree was dumped by painting `real_frontend_module_component` for
`@mesh/navigation-bar` at 1092x48 and walking `last_tree`. The dev shell builds
the wayland crates fine, so this was available all along: `nix develop --command
cargo test -p mesh-core-shell`.

**What the tree showed.** The three regions were correct and the centre region
was exactly centred, at x=377 width=338 in a 1092 bar, so the previous entry's
centring fix holds. The controls were not: 22px and 26px boxes in a 28px row,
and a clock measuring 58x68 placed at `y = -14`, above the bar's top edge and
below its bottom edge, clipped only by `.nav-shell`'s `overflow: hidden`. That
is the reported overlap.

**Cause.** Slot contributions are spliced in wrapped in the compiler's embedded
`surface` root, whose cross size is auto, so `height: 100%` on a contributed
root resolves against an indefinite parent and collapses to content size.
Attempting to fix it by giving `embedded_root_style()` `align_self: Stretch`
had no effect at all: the function runs, but its assigned style does not reach
the rendered tree. A `width: Px(999)` probe was likewise discarded. That
overwrite was not located and the compiler change was reverted rather than
shipped as dead code.

**Change.** Navigation-bar controls carry explicit 28px sizes again, and the
text-bearing ones 28px and 24px. The clusters keep `height: 100%`, which does
work because they are direct children of the bar root with no wrapper between.
Re-dumping the tree confirms every control now measures 28x28 at y=6, inside
both the 6..34 row and the 0..40 bar, with the clock centred at 546.

Both defects are backlogged with the evidence:
[wrapper layout](../todos/pending/2026-08-28-embedded-contribution-wrapper-layout.md).
`aspect-ratio` itself stays: it is tested and correct, and it is what
container-derived sizing will use once the wrapper is fixed.

**Verification.** Failure sets across `mesh-core-elements`, `mesh-core-frontend`,
`mesh-core-component`, and `mesh-core-module` diffed against a stashed `main`
baseline and are unchanged. The temporary dump test was removed.

## 2026-08-28 — Only the first `transition` entry ever animates

A report that hover scale "just jumps" was reproduced by driving a real pointer
move into `@mesh/navigation-bar` and sampling the theme trigger over successive
paints: `scale_x` sat at its hover target of 1.12 from the first frame, while
`color` on the same node interpolated normally.

**Cause.** `TransitionAnimator::step_node_with_policy_state` reads
`node.computed_style.transitions.first()` and uses only that entry's properties,
duration, delay, and easing. A comma-separated `transition` list is parsed
correctly and stored in full — verified by dumping the node's parsed
`TransitionStyle` vector — and then everything after the first entry is
discarded. The shipped bar listed `color`, `border-color`, `transform` in that
order, so colour animated and the other two snapped. The pre-existing bar had
the same latent bug with `background-color` first.

**Change.** Every multi-property transition in the navigation bar moved from the
`transition` shorthand to `transition-property` / `transition-duration` /
`transition-timing-function`. The longhands write into a single entry through
`first_transition_mut`, and `transition-property` sets several flags on that one
entry, so the entry the animator does read now covers all of the properties.
Re-probing shows the hover scale starting at 1.0 and interpolating. The trade is
one shared easing per element, which these controls did not need to differ.

The animator defect is backlogged under interaction and motion. Fixing it means
one in-flight instance per entry rather than the current one per `NodeId`, since
entries carry independent duration, delay, and easing.

**Also.** Workspace hover moved off the number and onto a pill that slides
between slots behind the digits, at the active pill's shape and 0.16 opacity,
driven by per-button `onpointerenter` and a row-level `onpointerleave` through
the same `WS_SLOT_STRIDE` the active indicator uses.

**Verification.** The full `mesh-core-shell` suite was baselined against stashed
`main` and now matches it exactly. Four shipped-surface tests encoded values this
session's work changed and were updated: the unmeasured bar's declared height
(56 to 40), the workspace indicator stride (28 to 26), the settings button's
measured box (40 to 28), and the theme-repaint test's button pixel sample. That
last one no longer has anything to sample, because nav controls have no fill in
any state, so it now asserts the button's resolved colour changes with the theme
rather than sampling a fill that no longer exists.

## 2026-08-28 — One in-flight transition instance per entry

Follow-up to the entry above, which worked around the animator by rewriting the
navigation bar's shorthands as longhands. The animator itself is now fixed.

**Change.** `TransitionAnimator` keys `HashMap<NodeId, Vec<ActiveTransition>>`
instead of one instance per node. `step_node_with_policy_state` walks every
entry of `computed_style.transitions`, reconciles each against its own instance
from the previous frame by list position, and starts, continues, reverses,
replaces, or ends them independently. Each instance carries its own duration,
delay, and easing, so a 100ms colour fade and a 400ms transform ease on one
element now run on separate timelines.

Three supporting decisions:

- **Later entries win shared properties.** Each entry runs its authored property
  mask minus everything a later entry claims, matching CSS's last-declaration
  rule. `transition: color 400ms, color 100ms` runs only the 100ms entry.
  `TransitionProperties` gained `union`, `difference`, and `is_empty` for this;
  `difference` expands the `all` shorthand first so subtracting one property
  cannot leave `all` re-enabling it.
- **Entries compose, they do not overwrite.** Instances own disjoint property
  sets, so each frame layers every instance's own properties onto the node's
  desired style. Retargeting one entry no longer restarts another: the
  continue/replace check compares only the entry's own properties, not the whole
  `AnimatableStyle`.
- **`visibility` belongs to no entry.** It is discrete and not in
  `TransitionProperties`, so the entry that finishes last carries it. A node
  stays visible until its slowest exit transition ends rather than its first.
  A visibility change still re-targets every entry, as it did before.

`displayed_style` now takes the node's desired style as the base to compose
over, because an entry's stored `to` is only authoritative for its own
properties. `active_unfinished` became `unfinished`, an iterator, so the shell
merges the animation-property bucket across every live entry instead of reading
one.

**Not changed.** The navigation bar keeps the longhand form from the previous
entry. The longhands still collapse into entry 0, which is now the remaining
gap and is backlogged: shorthand authors can give two properties different
timing, longhand authors cannot.

**Verification.** Six new tests: independent timelines across two entries, later
entry winning a shared property, retargeting one entry leaving another's
`started_at` alone, and the three `TransitionProperties` set operations. Full
`cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` failure set diffed against a stashed
`main` baseline and is identical (45 pre-existing failures, unchanged). Two
extra failures in the first comparison run —
`display_list_uses_cached_icon_opacity_for_conservative_barriers` and
`explicit_color_scheme_is_reported_without_id_heuristics` — pass in isolation
and did not recur; both are in crates this change does not touch or are driven
by the locally modified `config/settings.json`.

## 2026-08-28 — Make module lifecycle and health authoritative across activation boundaries

Backend candidates now remain private to the activation coordinator: spawning
or failing an uncommitted candidate cannot load, recover, error, or quarantine
the module represented by the currently active runtime. Candidate failure marks
the module unavailable only when no committed runtime remains, while late
events from the retired identity cannot recover it. Successful normal and
profile activation records the running state only after the runtime is
committed, explicitly clearing quarantine at that recovery boundary.

Graph synchronization now reconciles enabled, disabled, and removed modules
with resolved/unloaded lifecycle states and preserves health-state timestamps
across same-state refreshes. Frontend deactivation and shell shutdown mark
modules unloaded even when no component instance was mounted; backend shutdown
records stopped generations; and recovery waits for all instances of a module
to be healthy before restoring its running state.

Added regressions for candidate failure without a current runtime and for a
failed replacement preserving the current module lifecycle. Service
unavailability remains delivered for committed backend failures while
uncommitted candidate health stays private.

**Verification.** `nix develop --command cargo check --workspace` passed;
`nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib backend_lifecycle:: --no-fail-fast`
reached 37 passed and 1 failed at the existing invalid-manifest package-graph
fixture assertion. The broad
`nix develop --command cargo test -p mesh-core-shell --lib --no-fail-fast`
reached 779 passed, 22 failed, and 131 ignored; the failures are the existing
quick-settings, audio/input, rendering/profiling, fixture, graph, popover,
command rollback, backend-manifest, and malformed/duplicate-theme baseline
failures. `nix develop --command cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff
--check` passed. No performance claim is made.

## 2026-08-29 — Complete lock schema and provenance

The shared `mesh.lock` authority now retains the newly installed manifest when
rebuilding metadata, so direct dependency requirements and reverse requesters
cannot be lost when a package client supplies a pre-install inventory. Explicit
composition inheritance ranges are preserved instead of being replaced with an
unbounded placeholder. Lock validation now checks semantic module/composition
versions, dependency ranges, and composition identity/version consistency.

Rollback refreshes dependency and composition-related metadata from the exact
module manifests it restored before publishing the selected generation. This
also upgrades historical v2 locks with complete v3 dependency provenance at
the rollback boundary.

## 2026-08-29 — Replace additive interface registry with resolved service catalog

The interface registration boundary is now a mutable graph-build input that
compiles into an immutable `ResolvedServiceCatalog`. Contract/provider matching,
feature negotiation, operation-policy fingerprints, and graph-selected provider
identity are stored in one generation-stamped binding. The shell publishes that
complete snapshot through an atomic `ResolvedServiceCatalogHandle`, and frontend,
scripting, backend launch, dispatch, rollback, and debug paths consume the
resolved type rather than independently selecting from a live registry. Graph
activation preserves provider metadata for diagnostics while routing only the
active compatible provider, preventing stale or higher-priority inactive
providers from being selected.
